and is not in $\sigma_{P}$ . An accepting publish decision creates
no local block and allocates no endorsed-sort identifier: it emits only
the shared-tier submission event of Rule R12
(Publish-Acc).

Rule R6 (Null handle).

A $\mathsf{fill}$ or $\mathsf{crystallize}$ search over $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ that
finds nothing returns a null handle: the handle’s existence and
identifier are fixed by the requesting operation (Rule R1);
only its resolution differs, and that difference is hidden from $P$ .
Likewise, $\mathsf{recall}$ on an identifier that does not resolve to a
readable $\mathbb{E}$ -block returns a handle of the same kind: whether it
resolves to $\mathsf{U}$ -bytes or to nothing (in particular, whether the
identifier is occupied at all) is hidden from $P$ . If a null handle is
passed to any tool, the gate fires as usual (Rule R4),
the decision pane renders a fixed “no value resolved” display,
recorded in the display transcript like any confirmation event, the call
aborts, and $P$ receives the standard reject
decision, indistinguishable in
the reply stream and in the sanitized history $h$ from a user
rejection (Definition S2.2); the no-value display itself
is recorded on the transcript like any confirmation event.

Rule R7 (Failure indistinguishability).

The success/failure of a $\mathsf{U}$ -store search, and the resolution status
of any $\mathsf{recall}$ , is consumed interpreter-side to choose the
null-vs-real handle; no reply and no sanitized-history record exposes
it. A handle’s private resolution may later be displayed at a gate:
that display and the user’s logged decision are an explicit transcript
release (Definition S3.8), not a failure channel. Any reader error, reader
crash, or reader budget exhaustion arising during an operation over
 $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ is likewise consumed interpreter-side: the operation returns
the same operation-determined handle it would return on success
(Rules R1, R6), whose resolution to bytes, to
nothing, or to an aborted computation is hidden from $P$ . The generic
failure outcome ( $\mathsf{fail}$ , Figures S2–S3) is reserved for
failures that are a deterministic function of $P$ ’s own emitted
operations, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , and the coupled coins $\omega$ alone
(malformed invocations, failed preconditions on $\sigma_{P}$ , and the
retrieval failure of Retrieve-F). Search outcomes over $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ are trusted data
and may be exposed. (Rationale and the store-quota corollary:
N4, S7, convention (C7).)

Rule R8 (Faithful display on a split, zoned surface).

(a) 

The display surface is split into a decision pane and
auxiliary selection surfaces.

(b) 

The decision pane shows exactly the resolved bytes that will flow
(or be recorded as endorsed content), plus a provenance line in two
visually separated zones: (i) attested facts (channel, label
history, timestamps, signature-verification status), rendered in an
interpreter-controlled region: TCB-authenticated, not
attacker-forgeable or -alterable in content (event timing may be
attacker-influenced, e.g. a captured tool output’s timestamp reflects
when the attacker acted; what is guaranteed is that the recorded facts
are true and unmodified); (ii) source-identification strings
originating in untrusted content (sender names, subjects, citations),
rendered verbatim and visibly marked unverified. Where a
transaction aborts after some accepting reviews, the pane states that
the accepted values remain authorized for their targets.

(c) 

The decision pane contains no reader-generated prose outside the
confirmed payload region; where the payload itself is a crystallized
summary, it is reader-generated by construction, rendered verbatim, and
governed by Assumption A5.

(d) 

Selection surfaces may show reader-generated previews, visibly
marked unverified; previews are rendered UI-side only and never returned
to $P$ , and carry no endorsement authority.

(e) 

Both surfaces are recorded in the display transcript and the audit
log (Definition S3.8).

(f) 

On a rejection, the displayed bytes are not returned to $P$ ; $P$ 
receives only the decision. (Preview rationale: N8,
S9.)

Rule R9 (Batch gate under untrusted-driven iteration).

(a) 

A tool call reached inside $\mathsf{map}$ over a $\mathsf{U}$ -collection — including the desugared form of a collection-handle tool argument (Rule R1(e)) —
takes no fast path of Rule R4.

(b) 

The map is gated as a single batch confirmation whose
decision pane displays the collection’s cardinality and the full
resolved bytes of every element, together with the resolved bytes of any gating non- $\diamond$ 
argument of the body (Rule R1(f)), displayed once for
the batch, paginated as needed, the cardinality displayed and recorded on the first page; per-page
cardinality is capped at $c_{\mathrm{batch}}$ ; the interpreter performs any split,
presenting a larger collection as successive pages of one batch
confirmation. A deployment may additionally fix a total-cardinality cap (TCB
configuration): a $\mathsf{map}$ over a collection exceeding it
renders a fixed oversize display — recorded in the transcript like
the no-value display of Rule R6 — and takes the standard
aggregated reject of clause (c), consuming no decision,
indistinguishable in the reply stream and in $h$ from a page
rejection or a null collection.
A null collection (a collection handle with null resolution:
an empty search result, or a zero-output capture, convention (i);
Rules R1(a), R6) is handled identically: the
interpreter renders the fixed no-value display of Rule R6,
recorded in the transcript as a batch event exactly like the
oversize display, and the map takes the standard aggregated reject
of clause (c), consuming no decision (Map-Tool-Rej;
mechanized as batchGate_null,
§S9). The no-value and oversize displays are
distinct event kinds (Definition S3.8), so the first
recorded batch event determines the size class
(Lemma S3.20).

(c) 

 $P$ receives a single aggregated decision for the whole
map (accept iff every page was accepted, else the single standard
reject), regardless of page count. The number of pages, like the
cardinality itself, is never returned to $P$ in any form; both are
displayed to the user and recorded in the transcript only.

(d) 

Each page’s rendering and decision is recorded in the transcript;
the aggregated decision is recorded once. By Rule R2, the
bytes that flow at each element call are exactly the element bytes
displayed for it. The user may reject at any page, aborting the
remainder. The oversize and null-collection displays are recorded under their respective fixed-display event kinds (Definition S3.8), each with its standard reject and the aggregated batch decision.

(e) 

Per-element results are captured to $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ and never returned
to $P$ (the map returns one ack token). (All-or-nothing rationale and
 $c_{\mathrm{batch}}$ sizing: N6, S12.)

Rule R10 (Constant-alphabet interface, with totality).

(a) 

Every value the interpreter returns to $P$ is of one of two kinds.
The first kind is drawn from a fixed finite alphabet independent of the
attack and the environment: (i) one constant acknowledgement per executed tool
invocation or $\mathsf{map}$ , independent of outcome and of the number,
size, or content of captured blocks; a gate-rejected invocation does not
execute and returns only the reject decision of item (iv)
(Tool-Gate-Rej); (ii) opaque handles named by
Rule R1; (iii) the generic failure outcome of
Rule R7, deterministic in $P$ ’s emitted operations,
 $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , and the coupled coins $\omega$ ; (iv) the user’s
decision at a confirmation, with the null-gate abort of
Rule R6 rendered as the reject decision. The second kind is
(v): verbatim $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes dereferenced from $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ .

(b) 

Totality. Every syntactically well-formed invocation of
every operation of Figures S2–S3, with any argument, has its
behaviour defined by the figures and these rules; failed preconditions
and unresolvable arguments are rendered, uniformly, as the generic
failure outcome or as an operation-determined opaque handle, never as a
distinguishing error. In particular, whether a given identifier is
occupied by a $\mathsf{U}$ -block is never observable to $P$ through any operation:
 $\mathsf{recall}$ and $\mathsf{evict}$ alike return
operation-determined replies (an opaque handle; the constant
 $\mathsf{ack}$ ) uniformly across resolving, non-resolving,
 $\mathsf{U}$ -occupied, and $\mathbb{E}$ -occupied arguments, resolution consumed
interpreter-side (Rules R6, R7;
Figure S3, convention (v)).

Rule R11 (Trusted-store retrieval).

 $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ (i) reads only $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , never any $\mathsf{U}$ -bytes; (ii) has no tool
access; (iii) returns only block identifiers, which the interpreter
dereferences against $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ verbatim; non-dereferencing output is
discarded. $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ ’s sampling randomness is coupled alongside $P$ ’s
(convention (C1)). (Why neither reader’s vision can be relaxed:
N3.)

Deployment-selectable hardening.

Options O1–O2 are formally instantiated: each
strictly gates more flows, and their monotonicity is proved as
Proposition S3.28. Option O3 is a
requirements-level extension, outside the formal semantics and
outside Proposition S3.28’s quantifier (O3).

Option O1 (Sink-scoped trusted values).

 $\mathsf{T}$ -blocks require a first-use confirmation per sensitive sink, after
which scope-caching applies. Blocks created by these confirmations carry
the interpreter-only provenance mark cache and are ineligible for
submission (Rule R12).

Option O2 (Exact-target mediation at data tools).

The data-tool clause of Rule R4(b) is replaced by the
sensitive-tool test of clause (a): a value passed to a tool in $\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}$ 
flows without confirmation iff it byte-equals a stored
 $\mathsf{T}$ -block, or byte-equals some $\mathsf{C}$ / $\mathsf{CC}$ -block $b$ with
 $\mathsf{tool}\in\mathsf{scope}(b)$ (for $\mathsf{CC}$ , the live shared-tier entry,
checked valid at call time, §S4.1). Any other data-tool
argument — a freshly composed value, or endorsed bytes whose scope
does not admit this data tool (in particular every reasoning-target
copy, whose scope is empty, convention (vii)) — is confirmed once;
the fresh copy is scoped $\{\mathsf{tool}\}$ per convention (vii), so
it scope-caches and covers all the user’s later use of the pair
(INV-3). Blocks created by these confirmations carry the
interpreter-only cache mark and are ineligible for submission
(Rule R12); their gate events are cache confirmations,
accept-inert per Definition S4.6.

Option O3 (Local scope leases (requirements level; not formally
instantiated)).

 $\mathsf{C}$ -scopes carry a deployment-set lease (convention (C4)): expiry
withdraws the expired copy’s tool authorization, so the pair’s next
scope-checked use with no other currently authorizing witness
re-confirms once. Unlike Options O1–O2, O3 is
specified at the requirements level only: it is not part of
the formal semantics of this document, no theorem quantifies over
it, and Proposition S3.28 does not cover it. A
conforming instantiation is a future extension obligated to satisfy
all of the following, and to prove analogues of
Lemma S3.20, Theorem S3.25,
Lemma S6.1, Theorem S6.2, and the
ledger accounting of Lemma S6.7 for the extended
semantics.

(a) 

Immutability. Blocks stay immutable (A2):
a lease is a validity predicate computed interpreter-side from
creation-time metadata (creation epoch or slot index, never
wall-clock time) and the current index; expiry is never a block
mutation or removal.

(b) 

Determinism on matched data. Validity inputs are
functions of the coupled event sequence, and lease state is added
to the matched components of Definition S3.6 and
carried through the unwinding, so matched runs gate identically.

(c) 

Witness-level expiry at tool targets. For tool targets,
expiry invalidates only the expired local authorization witness (an
ordinary or cache-marked $\mathsf{C}$ copy), and the two authorization tests
it touches are kept distinct. Literal authorization, on a
scope-checked literal path, is existential over whichever witnesses
the deployment’s selected formal options recognize there —
byte-equal $\mathsf{T}$ -blocks (per O1’s per-sink treatment where
selected), live $\mathsf{CC}$ entries, and lease-valid in-scope $\mathsf{C}$ copies;
the baseline data-tool path of Rule R4(b) is
scope-blind, so lease enforcement at data tools requires
O2 — and a literal use gates iff no such witness
currently authorizes the pair. Handle coverage
(Rule R13(i)) is narrower: a handle use gates in every
case (Rule R4(c)) and is covered iff the
identity holds a lease-valid, in-scope local $\mathsf{C}$ copy; a $\mathsf{T}$ or
live- $\mathsf{CC}$ witness never makes a handle use covered, exactly as in
the base semantics. The reasoning-coverage branch of
Rule R13(i) remains readability-based and
lease-insensitive: reasoning-target reuse never requires another approval.

(d) 

Classification and renewal. An expiry-induced gate
whose pair lacks any currently authorizing witness is required
— whether a literal gate fired by expiry or a handle gate
reclassified from covered to required because no lease-valid in-scope
 $\mathsf{C}$ copy remains. Any required approval so caused
writes the fresh copy prescribed by the triggering rule —
an ordinary $\mathsf{C}$ copy at an ordinary confirmation, a cache-marked,
federation-inert copy at an O1/O2 cache gate (Rule R12,
Definition S4.6(iii)) — and starts a new lease, so
subsequent literal or handle use within the new period is
fast-pathed or covered: at most one required confirmation per pair per
lease period. The class is determined by the gate that fired, never
by any expired duplicate witness; rejection and abandonment renew
nothing; duplicate matching copies are resolved by the existential
tests of clause (c), never by a selection among witnesses.

(e) 

Accounting. With each (user, item) seeing
 $E_{\mathrm{lease}}=\mathcal{O}(1)$ lease periods, the extension’s pathwise floor is
the once-per-pair cap per lease period, an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ 
multiplicative factor on Theorem S6.2’s constants — an
extension-level claim, to be proved with the extension. No
saturation-vanishing conclusion follows from $E_{\mathrm{lease}}=\mathcal{O}(1)$ 
alone: expiry re-fires of promoted pairs are residual required
confirmations, so the extension additionally requires an
O3-augmented analogue of the complete residual bound of
A11 and Theorem S6.9(iii) — the per-pair
 $D_{0}+\varepsilon\,n$ form with expiry re-fires included, together
with its product and residual-fraction rate conditions — not
merely a substitute for $\varepsilon(M)\to 0$ .

(O3 is the single-user twin of the federated re-verification queue:
N16.)

## S2.4 Trust axioms

Assumption A1 (Deterministic source labelling).

 $\ell(b)$ is a function of the channel/source of $b$ alone, never of
 $\mathrm{bytes}(b)$ . Hence the attacker cannot change any label.

Assumption A2 (Label persistence and block immutability).

Blocks are immutable once written: no party and no transition alters
an existing block’s bytes, label, or scope; the store changes only by
the deterministic block-creating transitions of
Figures S2–S3 (convention (vii)) and by
 $\mathsf{evict}$ . Trust is raised only by the creation of a fresh
endorsed block at an accepting $\mathsf{confirm}$ , or by deterministic community
promotion of a shared-tier entry (Appendix S4); the
attacker can do neither.

Assumption A3 (Faithful, zoned display).

At a confirmation, the decision-pane bytes are exactly the bytes that
will flow or be recorded; the attested-fact zone is TCB-authenticated and not attacker-forgeable (Rule R8(b));
untrusted source-identification strings appear only in the marked zone;
previews are confined to selection surfaces, visibly marked
(Rule R8).

Assumption A4 (Sound atomic endorsement).

The user returns $\mathrm{accept}$ on a displayed value $v$ at tool
 $\mathsf{tool}$ only if the user intends exactly $v$ to be authorized
for $\mathsf{tool}$ ; the intent is per displayed value, severally, and
does not depend on the outcomes of the transaction’s other reviews —
which is what makes the durable endorsement of Rule R2
sound when a later review aborts the invocation. At a batch
confirmation (Rule R9), the user returns
 $\mathrm{accept}$ on a page only if this intent holds for each
displayed element and each displayed gating non- $\diamond$ argument,
severally, at the batch’s tool: batch acceptance is the conjunction of
the per-value intents, never an aggregate judgment of the collection.

Assumption A5 (Sound narrative endorsement).

The user returns $\mathrm{accept}$ on a displayed crystallized summary
 $S$ only if, having checked $S$ against its displayed source citations,
the user intends $S$ ’s content to inform reasoning.

Assumption A6 (Exogenous command stream).

The user’s typed command-channel input is an exogenous input stream,
coupled identically across the runs compared in
Theorem S3.25.

Commentary
 (non-normative; epistemic status of
A4–A6). A4 and A5 are trust axioms in the sense of CaMeL
Debenedetti et al. (2025); A6 is the human-channel idealization that any
displayed-content carve-out requires. Their epistemic status, the
regime that makes them defensible, and the discharge of
A6’s practical content by logging both display surfaces are
discussed at N5 (see also S9,
S10, S12); A5’s realistic weakness
at scale is what the community mechanism of
Appendix S4 addresses.

## S2.5 Attacker model and trusted computing base

Attacker.

The attacker controls the content of every $\mathsf{U}$ -labelled
block, all tool outputs (including sensitive-tool responses and error
strings) and paste-channel input, including the byte-length and the
number of $\mathsf{U}$ -blocks captured per call. It does not control the user’s
typed command input, the labelling, the interpreter, or the user’s
decisions. $P$ , $Q$ , and $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ are isolated from the attacker,
not assumed benign: the single-user theorems hold under adversarial
behaviour of either reader, and misdirection among the user’s own typed
or endorsed values is a utility failure outside the threat model
(Option O1 offers defence in depth; scope discussion:
§S7.4).

Trusted computing base (TCB), single-user.

The TCB comprises: an authenticated command channel distinguishing
typed from pasted from tool input; the canonical tool identifiers and
the $\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}/\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}$ partition; the interpreter’s implementation
(including byte-equality and dereference checks) and the individuating
parser; the zoned rendering path of Rule R8; and the
hash-chained audit log materializing the display transcript. The store
idealization and quota policy are convention (C7). Federated TCB
additions are listed in §S4.3.

## Appendix S3 Single-User Guarantees

This appendix proves two conditional interface properties.
Action Integrity is an exact-byte, exact-tool authorization
property: no untrusted-origin value reaches a sensitive action
unless the user endorsed exactly that value for exactly that action;
it does not authorize the choice to invoke a tool
(§S7.4). Reasoning Integrity modulo the display
transcript is a fixed-horizon, termination-insensitive
trace-equivalence property: holding fixed everything the user ever saw
and decided on the display surface, together with the coupled
exogenous inputs, untrusted memory has no further effect on behaviour;
it is not a claim of reasoning correctness or of resistance to
manipulation through displayed content
(Remark S3.9, N7). The statements are in
the noninterference-modulo-declassification tradition
Goguen & Meseguer (1982); Goguen & Meseguer (1984); Sabelfeld & Sands (2009); Sabelfeld & Myers (2004); Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007); the first is
the PerMIT instance of qualified robustness Zdancewic & Myers (2005); Myers et al. (2006).
Both theorems, the supporting lemmas, the baseline separation, the flow
half of the hardening monotonicity, and the batch-confirmation surface
of Rule R9 are machine-checked
(§S9: scope, axiom audit, artifact, correspondence
Table S5, and the disclosed modelling deltas). Lineage
and positioning: N1.

## S3.1 Definitions

Definition S3.1 ( $\mathsf{U}$ -equivalence).

 $c_{1}\approx_{\mathsf{U}}c_{2}$ iff $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c_{1})=\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c_{2})$ : agreement on $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ 
(content and scopes), $H(c)$ , $x(c)$ , and $h$ , while $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ may
differ arbitrarily Volpano et al. (1996); Sabelfeld & Myers (2003).

Definition S3.2 (Sink-event safety).

Fix a finite sequence of display events — decision-pane
confirmation events, each carrying its displayed bytes, target, and
decision (Definition S2.5, Rule R8), and
renderings — and a trusted typed-input stream $x$ . A recorded
sensitive sink event is safe for that history if every
argument value of the event is: (i) the exact
bytes of a typed input unit of $x$ (a $\mathsf{T}$ -block’s bytes,
individuation per §S2.1); or (ii) endorsed at a prior
accepting decision-pane confirmation event of the history displaying
exactly those bytes at exactly the event’s tool (for element calls of
a batch-gated $\mathsf{map}$ , at the batch confirmation displaying
those exact element bytes, Rule R9); or (iii) gated at
the event’s own call, the flowed bytes being the displayed bytes of
that call’s accepting confirmation (Rule R2). The
delegated form extends case (ii) by one alternative, relative to a designated federation-log prefix (in a run, the prefix delivered at the event’s step, Definition S4.2): the bytes
byte-equal a live community entry of that prefix’s replay naming the event’s exact tool,
backed by a currently valid promotion certificate
(Definition S4.1, A7); the personal form
is the single-user predicate, machine-checked as SinkSafe
(§S9). It is consumed by
Definition S3.3(v) and Definition S3.10.

Definition S3.3 (Well-formed configuration).

A configuration $c$ is well-formed, written $\mathsf{WF}(c)$ ,
if:
(i) every $\mathsf{T}$ -block’s bytes occur in the typed stream $x(c)$ ;
(ii) every $\mathsf{C}$ -block $b$ with $t\in\mathsf{scope}(b)$ is backed by an
accepting decision-pane confirmation event displaying exactly
 $\mathrm{bytes}(b)$ at target $t$ in the audit transcript, with the
matching sanitized (target, accept) record in $h$ ;
(iii) no block carries label $\mathsf{CC}$ (single-user; the federated
counterpart replaces (iii) by a valid promotion certificate on the
federation log, Appendix S4);
(iv) the handle structure $H(c)$ and the endorsed-sort identifier
allocation are the operation-determined structures of
Rule R1 for the configuration’s interaction history,
 $\sigma_{P}$ is the strategy state of that history
(§S2.1), and the recorded reply stream is the
interface’s reply to that history
(Figures S2–S3);
(v) every recorded sink event is safe (Definition S3.2; federated form: the delegated predicate).
The empty configuration is well-formed. A seeded configuration
(nonempty $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , empty interaction history) is well-formed only
together with a seed record: a designated initial-authorization
prefix of the audit transcript
(with its sanitized projection in $h$ ) backing every seeded $\mathsf{T}$ -block per (i) and every seeded scoped $\mathsf{C}$ -block per (ii) —
operationally, the authorizations imported with a migrated store;
formally, the record is read as part of $x(c)$ , $h$ , and $\mathsf{DT}$ ,
whereupon (i)–(v) hold verbatim. A seeded configuration without such
a record is in general not well-formed; it is sorted
(Definition S3.5), which is what the two-run results
require. Well-formedness is preserved by every transition of
Figures S2–S3 (this is the induction of
Lemmas S3.12–S3.14, machine-checked as the
invariant Inv of §S9). Action
Integrity and its corollaries quantify over runs from well-formed
configurations; the two-run results quantify over matched pairs of
sorted configurations (Definitions S3.5, S3.6).

The history against which clause (v) reads
Definition S3.2 is the configuration’s full audit
history: any seed-record prefix followed by the run transcript
(Definition S3.8).

Remark S3.4.

Clause (v) is the standard inductive strengthening required to prove
Theorem S3.21 by induction on run length (mirrored verbatim as
the sinkSound field of Inv in the mechanization,
§S9): it tracks, through the induction, exactly
the per-event predicate of
Definition S3.2, whose universal form over reachable
configurations is the theorem’s conclusion — not an independent
assumption, and not circular: the predicate is defined without
reference to the theorem. The from-arbitrary-well-formed forms are preservation
statements; the empty-initial-configuration forms, where the
invariant is established rather than assumed
(inv_init, §S9), are the creation
forms.

Definition S3.5 (Sorted configuration).

A configuration $c$ is sorted if clauses (iii) and (iv) of
Definition S3.3 hold of it: no $\mathsf{CC}$ label (federated form as
there), operation-determined handle and endorsed-sort identifier
structure with $\sigma_{P}$ the strategy state, and an
interface-conformant reply stream. Every well-formed configuration is
sorted. Every endorsed-seed configuration — an arbitrary
 $\mathbb{E}$ -level $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ at endorsed-sort identifiers
(Rule R1(g)), an arbitrary $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ , and an empty
interaction history — is sorted, with no seed record required.
Sortedness is preserved by every transition
(machine-checked as WFs, with the endorsed-seed condition
EStore; §S9).

Commentary
 (non-normative; why two classes). The split is forced by the theorems, in opposite directions. Action
Integrity is an endorsement-soundness statement, so its quantifier
must carry endorsement soundness: admitting a bare seed as well-formed
would falsify Theorem S3.21 (Remark S3.15) — a seeded $\mathsf{C}$ -block
with $t\in\mathsf{scope}$ flows to $t$ ungated, with no endorsing
confirmation anywhere in the run. Reasoning Integrity, by contrast,
never consumes endorsement soundness: its unwinding needs only the
matched view, the operation-determined naming of
Rule R1, and the strategy reading — precisely the sorted
clauses — so its quantifier is deliberately the weaker class,
admitting arbitrary shared endorsed seeds. The mechanization enforces
exactly this split: action_integrity_from requires the
full invariant Inv, while
reasoning_integrity_from requires only
EStore/WFs (§S9).

Definition S3.6 (Matched configurations).

Two sorted configurations (Definition S3.5) are
matched, $c_{1}\approx c_{2}$ , if $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c_{1})=\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c_{2})$ ; $P$ ’s
interaction histories coincide (the same emitted-operation sequence,
the same reply stream, and the same consumed coin prefix — hence
 $\sigma_{P,1}=\sigma_{P,2}$ , by the strategy reading of
§S2.1); and the ghost display transcripts and sink logs
coincide. Matched views and histories also fix the endorsed-sort
allocation state (Rule R1(g)), so subsequent endorsed blocks
are created at equal identifiers in the two runs. $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ and the
interpreter’s private handle resolutions may differ arbitrarily;
the handle count is matched through $H(c)$ 
(Rule R7). Matched implies $\approx_{\mathsf{U}}$ ; the converse fails on
 $\sigma_{P}$ and the ghost logs, which is why the two-run results are
stated over matched pairs. The canonical matched pair is two runs
launched from a shared endorsed-seed configuration
(Definition S3.5) with empty histories and empty ghost logs
under two different attacks (the form mechanized as initE,
§S9); matched pairs need not be well-formed, and
Theorem S3.25 holds without any endorsement-soundness
hypothesis on the seed. This relation is mechanized, field for field,
as MatchW (§S9).

Definition S3.7 (Ungated sink projection).

For a finite run $\rho$ , $\mathsf{Sink}(\rho)$ is the sequence of sensitive
sink events, each with its arguments, except that arguments displayed
and accepted in the immediately preceding atomic gating confirmation
(Rule R2, including, for element calls of a
 $\mathsf{map}$ , the batch confirmation of
Rule R9) are stripped from the annotation. Stripping
makes $\mathsf{Sink}(\rho)$ well-defined for baseline comparisons in which
transcripts may be empty (Proposition S3.27); under
 $\mathsf{DT}$ -equality the stripped arguments are equal in any case, so the
projection hides nothing in Theorem S3.25.

Definition S3.8 (Display transcript).

For a run $\rho$ from a designated starting configuration, $\mathsf{DT}(\rho)$ 
is the ordered sequence of all display events appended during
 $\rho$ (the run suffix; an audit prefix carried by a seeded starting
configuration belongs to its seed record, Definition S3.3, not
to $\mathsf{DT}(\rho)$ ): (i) selection-surface renderings, each with its
candidate identifiers, rendered preview bytes (marked unverified), and
the user’s selection, if any; (ii) decision-pane confirmation events,
one per reached gating review, each with the resolved bytes (or the
fixed no-value display of Rule R6), both provenance zones,
the target, and the decision; (iii) for a batch, either the fixed oversize display of
Rule R9(b) with its standard aggregated reject (where
the deployment cap applies), or the fixed no-value display of
a null collection with its standard aggregated reject
(Rules R6, R9(b)), or every page’s
rendering
and per-page decision, the first page recording the collection’s
cardinality — which, with $c_{\mathrm{batch}}$ , determines the deterministic
pagination and hence the page count (Rule R9(b)) —
and the aggregated decision, recorded once.
Transcript entries carry interpreter-assigned event kinds
(selection-surface rendering; decision-pane confirmation; batch
page; the fixed no-value display; the fixed oversize display;
aggregated batch decision), fixed constructors of the TCB logging
path: the fixed displays are distinguished from ordinary pages by
kind, never by comparing rendered byte strings, so page content
colliding textually with a fixed message cannot confuse the
classification (consumed by Lemma S3.20).
Rejections are recorded as
well as acceptances, and so are the accepting reviews of transactions
that subsequently abort (Rule R2). The harness materializes
the full audit history — any seed-record prefix followed by
 $\mathsf{DT}(\rho)$ — in a hash-chained audit log (operational reading:
N7; previews: N8, S9).

Remark S3.9 (Coverage of the display transcript).

By Rule R8(e), $\mathsf{DT}(\rho)$ comprises the totality of
attacker-influenceable content ever rendered to the user, together
with everything the user decided. Recording rejections is necessary:
two runs in which the user rejects and later accepts the same content
differ in behaviour during the interval, and a projection that forgets
rejections cannot distinguish them. Previews carry no endorsement
authority; their inclusion records them as part of the release
history.

Definition S3.10 (Action Integrity, personal and delegated).

PerMIT has personal Action Integrity (equivalently:
argument-value integrity) if in every run from a well-formed
configuration, under every attack, every sensitive sink event is safe
in the personal form of Definition S3.2: every value
reaching a sensitive sink is a user-typed $\mathsf{T}$ -value, or was endorsed by
this user at its exact bytes for that exact sink (now, or at a
prior in-scope confirmation; for element calls of a batch-gated
 $\mathsf{map}$ , at the batch confirmation that displayed those exact
element bytes, Rule R9). It has delegated Action
Integrity if the same holds with the endorsement authority extended to
a qualifying community confirmation of the exact bytes at the exact
tool ( $\mathsf{CC}$ , Appendix S4) (the delegated predicate of Definition S3.2); delegation is an assumption
(A7), never a theorem of the single-user model.

Definition S3.11 (Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript).

PerMIT has Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript if,
for every pair of matched configurations
 $c_{1}\approx c_{2}$ (Definition S3.6), every pair of attacks
(coupled per convention (C9): slot skeleton and typed stream only;
paste presence and content, tool outputs, and reader outputs
uncoupled), and every coupled coin sequence $\omega$ 
(convention (C1)), any two runs of a common horizon (convention (C9)) with
 $\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{1})=\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{2})$ satisfy
 $\mathsf{Sink}(\rho_{1})=\mathsf{Sink}(\rho_{2})$ and emit the same sequence of
Protected-LLM operations and tool invocations. Informal reading (a conditional statement, not an unconditional
inertness claim): any influence of untrusted memory on the listed
behaviours must pass through a display event or an endorsed release
— conditional on the transcript, hidden untrusted state has no
further effect. An unrendered untrusted byte is not claimed inert
outright: it may steer which candidates or previews the Reader
surfaces, and thereby the transcript itself; what is excluded is
influence that bypasses the transcript.

The statement is trace-wise per coin sequence; the knowledge-based
formulation in the style of gradual release Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007) that it
induces is recorded in §S8.4.

## S3.2 Structural lemmas

Lemma S3.12 (Protected-LLM isolation / provenance preservation).

In every configuration reachable from a sorted configuration
(Definition S3.5; a fortiori from a well-formed one), no byte
value enters $\sigma_{P}$ by delivery from a $\mathsf{U}$ -labelled
source: every bytes-carrying reply $P$ receives is, at the step of
its emission, the verbatim content of a non- $\mathsf{U}$ block of the store,
and $\mathsf{U}$ -derived data appears in $\sigma_{P}$ only as opaque handles.
This is a provenance statement, not a disjointness of byte strings
across tiers: a typed or endorsed byte string in $\sigma_{P}$ may be
extensionally equal to the bytes of an unrelated $\mathsf{U}$ -block, and the
flow it licenses is the trusted copy’s.
(Machine-checked, run-level: bytes_reply_isolation,
§S9.)

Proof.

Induction on run length with invariant $I(c)$ : every bytes-carrying
value ever delivered to $P$ was delivered as the content of a
non- $\mathsf{U}$ block (reply grammar case (v)) or as trusted command input;
everything else in $P$ ’s input stream is a handle or constant token. At a sorted
start, $\sigma_{P}$ is the strategy state of the initial
interaction history (§S2.1,
Definition S3.5), whose reply stream is
interface-conformant: every bytes-carrying reply in it is $\mathbb{E}$ -level
by the reply grammar, and its typed input is trusted command input at
level $\mathsf{T}$ ; an endorsed-seed configuration has empty history and
satisfies $I$ trivially. For the inductive
step, case-analyse the transitions of Figures S2–S3.
Tool-Fast, Tool-Gate-Acc/Rej, Map: by
Rules R3 and R9 all outputs land in
 $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ and only the constant acknowledgement (and, for gated calls,
the decision) returns; the collection handle’s identifier is
operation-determined and computed by $P$ , not returned
(Rule R1(b)). W-Typed stores $\mathsf{T}$ ; W-Paste
is captured to $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ . W-P has precondition
 $b\in\sigma_{P}$ , hence $\mathbb{E}$ -level by the hypothesis, and stores at $\mathsf{PC}$ 
with empty scope. Recall-E returns $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes;
Recall-O returns an opaque handle: uniformly for $\mathsf{U}$ -blocks,
unknown, and nonexistent identifiers
(Rules R1, R6, and the totality clause
Rule R10(b)); $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ -retrieval returns verbatim $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes
(Rule R11). Fill and Cryst return one
handle and, by Rule R7, no branchable bit. Display
and Evict inject nothing
(Evict replies the constant $\mathsf{ack}$ uniformly,
Figure S3, convention (v)); previews are UI-side only
(Rule R8(d)). Conf-Acc/Rej return only the
decision (the null-gate abort returning the reject decision,
Rule R6) and on accept produce an $\mathbb{E}$ -block. Fail
returns the constant $\mathsf{fail}$ token. $P$ -generated content is a
function of $\sigma_{P}$ and the coins, hence $\mathbb{E}$ -level by the meet rule
(Definition S2.1). By
A1–A2 and convention (vii), no transition
raises the level of any existing block; an accepting $\mathsf{confirm}$ writes a
fresh $\mathbb{E}$ -block and leaves its untrusted source, if any, at $\mathsf{U}$ .
Thus every delivery to $P$ carries non- $\mathsf{U}$ provenance; the existence of extensionally equal untrusted copies is irrelevant. $I$ is preserved.
∎

Lemma S3.13 (Whitelist mediation, by tool class).

Every value $a$ passed to any tool satisfies the following, according to the
tool’s class.
(Sensitive tools.) If $\mathsf{tool}\in\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}$ , at least one of:
(i) $a$ consists of readable $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes byte-equal to a stored $\mathsf{T}$ -block;
(ii) $a$ consists of readable $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes byte-equal to some $\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{CC}$ -block $b$ 
with $\mathsf{tool}\in\mathsf{scope}(b)$ ;
(iii) the call is gated by an atomic $\mathsf{confirm}(a,\mathsf{tool})$ displaying $a$ ’s
resolved bytes (or, for a batch, every element’s bytes; or, for a null handle,
the fixed no-value display, whereupon the call aborts).
For a literal-expressed argument, in the baseline semantics of
Rule R4(a) — before the formally instantiated
Options O1–O2 are applied — case (iii) is the
complement of the union of (i) and (ii): the fast path passes iff
(i) or (ii) holds, and the gate fires otherwise. Under
Option O1, a sensitive-tool argument satisfying case (i)
may nevertheless gate (first use of the $\mathsf{T}$ -value per sensitive
sink); Option O2 leaves this sensitive case analysis
unchanged and modifies only the data-tool case (D) below; the
option-modified analyses are Proposition S3.28’s.
Option O3 is requirements-level, outside this lemma and
outside Proposition S3.28 (O3). Cases (i)
and (ii) themselves may hold simultaneously — the store may
contain a $\mathsf{T}$ -block and an in-scope $\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{CC}$ -block with identical
bytes, a co-occurrence Option O1 manufactures by design —
and either suffices for the baseline fast path; no consumer of this
lemma uses exclusivity of (i) against (ii), each case being
separately sufficient in Theorem S3.21. Handle-expressed
arguments take case (iii) unconditionally (Handles clause
below).
(Data tools.) If $\mathsf{tool}\in\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}$ , then either
(D) $a$ consists of readable $\mathbb{E}$ -level bytes held by $P$ — that every byte value $P$ holds
carries non- $\mathsf{U}$ provenance, is Lemma S3.12’s
contribution, not Rule R4(b) alone — which flow without
confirmation (Rule R4(b)) — in particular $\mathsf{PC}$ -labelled and
freshly $P$ -composed $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes flow ungated at data tools; this is precisely
the clause Option O2 hardens — or the call is gated per case (iii).
(Handles.) Every handle argument, at every tool, falls under (iii): an ordinary handle via Rule R4(c); a collection handle via the batch gate of Rule R9, by the desugaring of Rule R1(e).
(Under Option O2, case (D) is replaced by the
clause-(a) case analysis applied at the data tool;
Proposition S3.28.)
(Machine-checked core: processArgs_spec and
bytes_reply_isolation, §S9: an ungated
flowed argument is a verbatim $P$ -supplied literal, and at a sensitive tool
it passes the whitelist test over $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ ; that $P$ holds only
 $\mathbb{E}$ -level bytes in case (D) is bytes_reply_isolation’s
contribution; witness for case (D): Scenario 7.)

Proof.

Immediate from Rule R4 with
Rules R2, R6, R9: clause (a) gives the sensitive case analysis: in the baseline
semantics the fast path passes iff the argument consists of readable
 $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes matching (i) or (ii), and the gate fires otherwise, so
case (iii) is exhaustive over the complement, while (i) and (ii) may
co-occur, harmlessly (the membership and scope tests are decidable
against $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , Definition S2.2; $\mathsf{PC}$ blocks have
empty scope and cannot take the sensitive fast path); clause (b) gives case (D) at data
tools; clause (c) gives the handle case at every tool. For element calls of
a $\mathsf{map}$ , the $\diamond$ -substituted element falls under (iii) via
the batch confirmation (Rule R9), and each non- $\diamond$ 
argument, a non-handle $P$ -supplied value by Rule R1(f), falls
under the case its tool class assigns exactly as at a direct call, its
gating branch resolved at the batch confirmation.
∎

Lemma S3.14 (Endorsement is the only trust raise).

(a) 

(Transition-level; any run.) The only $\mathsf{C}$ -creating
transitions of Figures S2–S3 are the accepting
gating reviews — Conf-Acc and the accepted positions of
Tool-Gate-Acc, Tool-Gate-Rej,
Map-Tool-Acc, Map-Tool-Rej — each writing a fresh
copy of exactly the displayed bytes with scope $\sigma(t)$ 
(Rule R2, convention (vii)); the only $\mathsf{CC}$ -assigning
transition is deterministic community promotion
(Appendix S4); and no transition alters an existing
block’s label or scope (A1–A2).

(b) 

(Backing; well-formed runs.) In every run from a
well-formed configuration (Definition S3.3), every $\mathsf{C}$ -block
 $b$ and every $t\in\mathsf{scope}(b)$ is backed by an accepting decision-pane
confirmation displaying exactly $\mathrm{bytes}(b)$ at target $t$ , in
the run suffix (by clause (a)) or in the seed record
(Definition S3.3(ii)); and every $\mathsf{CC}$ scope in force is
backed by its promotion certificate, whose threshold was met by
endorsements at the scoped tool itself
(Rule R13(iv), Definition S4.6).

Proof.

(a) is the case analysis below; (b) adds the base case: at the initial configuration, clause (ii) of well-formedness — with the seed record where the store is seeded — supplies the backing for every existing scoped $\mathsf{C}$ -block, and clause (iii) (federated form) the certificates.
By A1–A2 and convention (vii), no transition
alters an existing block’s label or scope, so a block’s label and
scope are those of its creating transition. No write transition
creates a block at $\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{CC}$ ; W-P creates $\mathsf{PC}$ with empty
scope; the only endorsement-creating transitions are
the accepting gating reviews — Conf-Acc, and the accepted
positions of Tool-Gate-Acc, Tool-Gate-Rej,
Map-Tool-Acc, and Map-Tool-Rej
(Rule R2: endorsement is durable, written on the accepting
and aborting branches alike) — each of which writes scope
 $\sigma(t)$ per convention (vii) for exactly the displayed bytes. The only $\mathsf{CC}$ -assigning transition is
promotion Myers et al. (2006), which
(Rule R13(iv), Definition S4.6) adds
 $\mathsf{tool}$ to a $\mathsf{CC}$ scope only when the community threshold
was met by endorsements at $\mathsf{tool}$ itself.
∎

Remark S3.15.

Clause (b) is deliberately not asserted over the sorted class
(Definition S3.5): an endorsed-seed configuration may hold
scoped $\mathsf{C}$ -blocks with no backing confirmation anywhere, and
clause (b) fails there, while clause (a), a property of the
transitions alone, holds in every run. Consumers of clause (b)
quantify over well-formed runs (via Definition S3.10);
Theorem S3.25 consumes neither clause. This is the
Inv-vs-EStore split of
Definitions S3.3/S3.5.

Lemma S3.16 (No community label single-user).

In every run from a well-formed configuration with no federation
attached, no block ever carries label $\mathsf{CC}$ : promotion is the only
 $\mathsf{CC}$ -assigning transition (Lemma S3.14) and it does not
exist in the single-user semantics. (Machine-checked as
Inv.noCC, §S9.)

Proof.

 $\mathsf{WF}(c_{0})$ (iii) gives the base case; no transition of
Figures S2–S3 assigns $\mathsf{CC}$ 
(Lemma S3.14’s case analysis).
∎

Lemma S3.17 (Interface completeness).

The sequence of values the interpreter returns to $P$ is a function of
the trajectory of $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c)$ , $P$ ’s own emitted operations and tool
invocations, the coupled coins $\omega$ , and the user’s decisions.

Proof.

By the reply grammar of Figure S2, every return is one of:
(a) a constant token ( $\mathsf{ack}$ or $\mathsf{fail}$ , per
Rule R10(a)(i),(iii)), including the uniform
acknowledgement of $\mathsf{evict}$ , whose resolution status is
consumed interpreter-side (Figure S3, convention (v));
(b) $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes from $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , a
component of $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c)$ ; (c) a handle whose existence and identifier are
fixed by the emitted-operation sequence
(Rules R6, R1), including collection handles,
whose identifiers $P$ computes and which are never returned, and the
handles of Recall-O, indistinguishable across resolution
statuses (Rule R10(b)); (d) the user’s decision, with the
null-gate abort rendered as the reject decision (Rule R6);
(e) the verbatim dereference of an $\mathbb{E}$ -block selected by $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ , a
function of the query, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , and $\omega$ 
(Rule R11). The grammar has no other productions, and
Rule R10(b) defines every invocation (totality); no return
depends on $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ bytes except through an accepting confirmation,
which writes the endorsed copy into $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ .
∎

Lemma S3.18 (Control-flow confinement to trusted data and releases).

 $P$ ’s choice and ordering of operations and tool invocations is a
function of $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c)$ , the reply stream, and the coupled coins alone.
No direct channel exists from $\mathsf{U}$ -block bytes, $\mathsf{U}$ -search
outcomes, $\mathsf{recall}$ resolution statuses, tool-invocation
outcomes, candidate multiplicities, collection cardinalities,
block-identifier occupancy, or handle-identifier structure beyond the
operation-determined naming, to any reply $P$ receives. Information
about hidden state reaches $P$ only through logged release events:
the user’s recorded decisions and the endorsed copies they create —
each carried, with its displayed bytes, in the display transcript
(Definition S3.8) — which is precisely the
declassification boundary Theorem S3.25 conditions on.

Proof.

We verify against Figures S2–S3 and Rules
R1–R11 that every channel is closed.
(a) $\mathsf{U}$ -content: absent from $\sigma_{P}$ 
(Lemma S3.12).
(b) $\mathsf{U}$ -search bits and $\mathsf{recall}$ resolution status:
consumed interpreter-side (Rules R6, R7,
R10(b)); the null-gate abort returns the same reject decision
a user rejection would, so $P$ cannot distinguish them
from the reply or the sanitized history; the resolved value, where the
user later inspects it, is released only through the displayed gate
event and its logged decision.
(c) Candidate multiplicity: bound interpreter-side
(Fill), absent from $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c)$ .
(d) Reader exceptions and budget exhaustion: absorbed into the
null-vs-real handle indistinguishability (Rule R7); the
observable $\mathsf{fail}$ outcome occurs only on conditions that are a
deterministic function of $P$ ’s emitted operations, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , and
the coupled coins, identical across matched runs.
(e) Timing/step count: the reply grammar contains no clock or
step-count value, so no such value exists in $\sigma_{P}$ to branch on;
wall-clock channels are outside the model (§S7.4, S8).
(f) Collection cardinality and block occupancy: a collection
handle exposes no length or indexing (Rule R1(e)); whether
an identifier is occupied by a $\mathsf{U}$ -block is not observable
(Rule R10(b)), so the attacker-chosen number of captured
blocks is invisible; $\mathsf{map}$ is one emitted operation returning
one acknowledgement with per-element results captured, never returned
(Rule R9(e)); tool-call multiplicity inside a map is
closed by the batch gate, and pagination above $c_{\mathrm{batch}}$ is
interpreter-side with a single aggregated decision returned
(Rule R9(c)), so neither the cardinality nor the page
count is ever returned to $P$ directly; the single aggregated decision is a logged release, on the transcript, like any gate decision; both are displayed to the user
and recorded in the transcript only.
Under the body grammar of Rule R1(f) — which also covers collection-handle tool arguments, normalized to $\mathsf{map}$ before transition matching (Rule R1(e)) — the check is
exhaustive per step: each $\mathsf{map}$ is exactly one
handle-producing operation whatever $|H|$ (Rule R1(c));
its single per-element tool call is covered by the batch gate, which
displays every element’s bytes and each gating non- $\diamond$ 
argument’s bytes (Rules R9(b), R2);
per-element outputs are captured, never returned
(Rule R9(e)); tool-free bodies write $\mathsf{U}$ -blocks and
reply once; and no admissible body operation can generate a
data-dependent reply, $\mathsf{recall}$ , $\mathsf{fill}$ ,
 $\mathsf{map}$ , $\mathsf{confirm}$ , and $\mathsf{evict}$ being excluded from
bodies and tool replies inside the batch being captured
(Rule R3).
(g) Handle identifiers: fixed by the operation sequence
(Rule R1(a)), identical across $\mathsf{U}$ -equivalent runs with
matched operations.
(h) Reply values: Lemma S3.17.
(i) $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ : a function of query, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , $\omega$ ; never
sees $\mathsf{U}$ -bytes; $\mathbb{E}$ -search outcomes are trusted data
(Rule R7).
(j) Tool outcomes: captured (Rule R3); the
acknowledgement is constant.
(k) Eviction: $\mathsf{evict}$ replies with the constant
 $\mathsf{ack}$ uniformly (Figure S3, convention (v));
whether the argument resolved, and to which tier, is consumed
interpreter-side. Its $\mathbb{E}$ -tier effect is a function of $P$ ’s
emitted operation and $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ alone, matched across
 $\approx_{\mathsf{U}}$ -related runs; its $\mathsf{U}$ -tier effect alters only $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ ,
which no reply exposes. In particular an
 $\mathsf{evict}$ -then- $\mathsf{recall}$ probe learns nothing:
Recall-O is uniform across occupied, vacated, and
never-occupied identifiers, before and after any $\mathsf{evict}$ 
(Rule R10(b)).
The enumeration is complete relative to the written interface:
 $\Sigma_{P}$ (§S2) is the entire operation surface,
matching Figures S2–S3 arm-for-arm and the
mechanized POp type, the reply grammar types every reply, and the totality clause
Rule R10(b) leaves no invocation with undefined or
distinguishing behaviour.
∎

Lemma S3.19 (Append-only transcript; prefix comparability).

Every transition of Figures S2–S3 extends the
display transcript by a (possibly empty) suffix and never removes or alters
an existing entry; hence along any run the transcript after $t$ steps is a
prefix of the transcript after $n\geq t$ steps. Consequently, if two lists
are prefixes of one common list, one is a prefix of the other.
(Machine-checked: dt_execOp_prefix,
dt_runFrom_prefix, prefix_or_prefix;
§S9.)

Proof.

Case inspection of the figures: each transition appends its own display
events at the end of the transcript or appends nothing (Rules R2,
R8, R6); the transcript is write-once by construction
(the audit-log reading of convention (C3) and
Definition S3.8). The comparability clause is elementary.
∎

Lemma S3.20 (Display-segment determinacy).

Let two configurations be matched (Definition S3.6)
immediately before a common $P$ -emitted operation, and execute that
operation in both. If the display segments the operation appends are
prefix-comparable (one a prefix of the other), then they are equal,
and so are the operation’s extensions of the reply stream, the
sanitized history, the endorsed store (identical fresh blocks at
identical endorsed-sort identifiers, Rule R1(g)), the
confirmation counter, and the sink log; only $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ and the
private handle resolutions may differ.
(Machine-checked core: processArgs_det and
match_execOp for direct invocations, and
batchGate_det for the batch-confirmation case,
§S9; the batch case’s store-level integration is
established by the paper proof only.)

Proof.

Case analysis on the common operation, whose inputs
( $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , the argument list, the confirmation counter, the coins)
are matched. For $\mathsf{display}(x)$ : both segments are the single
render event of the same $x$ . For a gate transaction ( $\mathsf{confirm}$ , or the
gating positions of an invocation, processed left to right): the set
of gating positions is a function of the argument list and $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ 
alone (Rule R4), both matched, so the runs gate at the
same positions, and every reached position emits exactly one
confirmation event (a null resolution emits the fixed no-value event,
Rule R6), so both segments are nonempty at each reached
position. Prefix-comparability forces the first events equal; equality
of that event pins the same resolution class (real value vs. no-value
display), the same displayed bytes, the same selection, and the same
decision, hence the same sanitized record, the same reply token
contribution, the same continuation (accept: proceed; reject or null:
abort the remaining positions in both runs), and, by
Rule R2, the same durable fresh $\mathsf{C}$ -copies at the same
identifiers on either branch. Induction along the remaining reached
positions gives segment equality and equal effects. For a batch
confirmation (Rule R9): equality of the first appended events pins the size class by event
kind (Definition S3.8) — both null (the fixed
no-value display, standard reject, no decision consumed), both
oversize (the fixed oversize display, likewise), or both paginated,
the cap being fixed TCB configuration — and in the paginated case the first page event records
the collection’s cardinality, and the pagination is the deterministic
 $c_{\mathrm{batch}}$ -split of Rule R9(b), so equality of the first
page events pins the page partition; the first-event argument applied
page by page gives equal rendered elements, equal decisions, and the
same stopping page on rejection, whence equal aggregated decisions,
equal durable non- $\diamond$ endorsements (convention (vii′)), and
equal captures to $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ only. Selection-surface renderings
within these events touch only the UI and the transcript, never
 $\sigma_{P}$ (Rule R8(d)). All other operations append no
display events and their effects on the matched components are
functions of matched data (Lemma S3.17).
∎

## S3.3 Action Integrity

Theorem S3.21 (Personal Action Integrity).

Under A1–A4, the well-formedness invariant
(Definition S3.3) is

(a) 

(Establishment) satisfied by the empty configuration and
by every seeded configuration with a valid seed record
(Definition S3.3); and

(b) 

(Preservation) preserved by every transition of
Figures S2–S3, under every attack, with no
control-flow or distributional hypothesis, and under adversarial
behaviour of either reader.

Consequently every sink event of every configuration reachable from a
well-formed configuration is safe (Definition S3.2, via
clause (v)): PerMIT has personal Action Integrity
(Definition S3.10), per execution. In the single-user
semantics the $\mathsf{CC}$ branch of Lemma S3.13 is vacuous
(Lemma S3.16); the delegated extension is
Proposition S5.10. (Machine-checked for clauses (i)–(iii),(v), whose conjunction
is the mechanized invariant Inv (Table S5):
established by inv_init, preserved by inv_execOp with inv_ingestTyped/inv_ingestPaste, composed as inv_runFrom; action_integrity, action_integrity_from. Clause (iv) is enforced by the
executable state and transition structure and the separate sortedness
invariant WFs; §S9.)

Proof.

(a) The empty configuration satisfies (i)–(v) vacuously; a
seeded configuration’s seed record supplies (i)–(ii) by
Definition S3.3, (iii)–(iv) hold of it by construction, and
(v) is vacuous (no recorded sink events). (b) Preservation of
clauses (i)–(iv) is the induction of
Lemmas S3.12–S3.14; for clause (v), take
any newly appended sink event and argument $a$ ; the event is sensitive
(Definition S2.5), so by Lemma S3.13 one of
the three sensitive-tool cases holds. (i) $a$ byte-equals a $\mathsf{T}$ -block: by well-formedness
(Definition S3.3(i)) and A1, those exact bytes were
typed by the user on the command channel. The fast path is deliberately
extensional: attacker-supplied content may be byte-equal to a
stored $\mathsf{T}$ -block, in which case the flowing bytes are still a value the
user personally typed, and the flow is user-authorized content
regardless of which equal copy motivated the call; provenance of other
equal copies is irrelevant to the authorization of these bytes.
(ii) $a$ byte-equals some
 $\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{CC}$ -block $b$ with $\mathsf{tool}\in\mathsf{scope}(b)$ : by
Lemma S3.14 an accepting $\mathsf{confirm}(a,\mathsf{tool})$ occurred;
by A3 the endorsed bytes
were exactly $a$ ; by A4 the user intended $a$ at
 $\mathsf{tool}$ . (iii) a fresh atomic $\mathsf{confirm}(a,\mathsf{tool})$ 
gates the call, in particular every handle argument, and by
Rule R2 the bytes that flow are the displayed bytes, bound
to this call instance, even if they coincide with stored content; for a
batch-gated $\mathsf{map}$ , every element’s bytes were displayed at the
batch confirmation (Rule R9) and accepted under the
per-element intent clause of A4, so each element call flows
exactly the bytes displayed for it; a null handle aborts
(Rule R6).

For reader adversariality: an adversarial $Q$ can at worst surface a
wrong value or decorate it with a preview; it holds no tool capability,
its output is $\mathsf{U}$ , the decision pane shows the true resolved bytes in
an interpreter-controlled region the attacker cannot influence
(Rule R8(b)), and A3–A4 reject
the wrong value. $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ selects only among $\mathbb{E}$ -blocks, each
independently in case (i)/(ii). This is qualified robustness
Zdancewic & Myers (2005); Myers et al. (2006).
∎

Remark S3.22 (Extensional trust of typed values).

Case (i)’s fast path is extensional and tool-blind at the baseline: a
user-typed value is authorized as a literal at every sensitive
sink, including sinks the user never contemplated when typing it, and
attacker content byte-equal to a typed value flows as the typed value
(the proof’s provenance reading). The handle discipline is unaffected:
a handle-expressed argument always gates, even when its hidden
resolution byte-equals a typed value (Rule R4(c)).
Deployments for which per-sink authorization of typed values matters
select Option O1, which re-gates $\mathsf{T}$ -values once per
sensitive sink (Proposition S3.28; budget:
Corollary S6.6).

Corollary S3.23 (No silent resolution; the post-endorsement boundary).

Under the same assumptions:

(a) 

(Never-endorsed bytes.) An untrusted-derived value that has
never been endorsed at any target is resolved at a tool boundary, sensitive
or data, only inside an accepting confirmation displaying exactly those
bytes at exactly that tool. In particular, no never-endorsed
untrusted-derived value can be exfiltrated through a data-tool parameter (a
URL, a search query, a file path) without the user endorsing that exact
value at that exact tool, in that call.

(b) 

(Post-endorsement boundary.) Once bytes have been endorsed at
any target (including “reasoning”), the fresh $\mathsf{C}$ -copy makes them
readable $\mathbb{E}$ -level bytes (Rule R5), and their subsequent
literal-expressed flow to data tools is ungated
(Lemma S3.13, case (D)); a handle-expressed use of the
same bytes gates at every tool regardless (Rule R4(c),
S18);
tool-specific endorsement is enforced only at sensitive tools (the
scope test of Rule R4(a)). This is the disclosed baseline
post-endorsement channel
(S10); Option O2 closes it: under
O2 the data-tool fast path applies the same exact-target test
as the sensitive path, so a reasoning-target copy (empty scope) and
any out-of-scope endorsed copy gate at every data tool, each such flow
then requiring its own per-pair confirmation.
(Machine-checked: the gateCheckO2 closure witnesses of
§S9.)

Remark S3.24 (Reading of “untrusted-derived”).

Clause (a) is a provenance statement about byte values: the
never-endorsed untrusted bytes themselves cross a tool boundary only
inside an accepting confirmation displaying them. It is not a claim of
information-theoretic independence: $P$ may branch on a logged gate
decision (a transcript release, Lemma S3.18) and encode that
released bit into $P$ -composed data-tool arguments; all such influence
is transcript-carried and governed by Theorem S3.25 and
Corollary S3.26, not by this corollary.

Proof.

(a) By Lemma S3.12, $P$ holds never-endorsed
untrusted-derived data only as opaque handles, so such bytes reach a tool
argument only by handle resolution; by Rule R4(c) every
handle argument at every tool gates; by A3–A4 and
Rules R2, R9 the resolved bytes flow only on an
accepting decision over exactly those bytes at exactly that call’s tool.
(b) Immediate from Rule R5 and Rule R4(b)
for literal-expressed use; handle arguments gate in every case by
Rule R4(c); a
reasoning-target copy has empty scope, so the sensitive fast path
remains closed to it, and Theorem S3.21 is unaffected.
(Machine-witnessed: Scenario 8, §S9.)
∎

## S3.4 Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript

Theorem S3.25 (Reasoning Integrity modulo the display transcript).

Under A1, A2, A3, A6,
and the scheduling convention (C9), PerMIT has Reasoning Integrity
modulo the display transcript (Definition S3.11): for every
matched pair $c_{1}\approx c_{2}$ 
(Definition S3.6), every pair of attacks, and every
coupled coin sequence $\omega$ (convention (C1)), any two runs of a common horizon (convention (C9)) with
 $\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{1})=\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{2})$ agree on the ungated sink projection, the
emitted sequence of Protected-LLM operations and tool invocations, the
reply stream, and the final Protected-LLM views.
(Machine-checked, with these strengthened conclusions and with
paste presence and content uncoupled across the compared attacks, as
reasoning_integrity_from; the batch case’s determinacy core
is checked separately (batchGate_det) while the store-level
integration of $\mathsf{map}$ is covered by the paper proof only —
its element-call sink events are gated, hence stripped
(Definition S3.7), so the ungated sink projection is
unaffected; §S9.)

Proof.

We use Lemmas S3.17–S3.18, which hold under the
harness rules, A1–A2, and the strategy
convention of §S2.1 alone. Fix a matched pair
 $c_{1}\approx c_{2}$ (Definition S3.6;
sorted suffices, Definition S3.5), two attacks, and a
coin sequence $\omega$ ; by A6 and convention (C9) the typed
streams and the slot skeleton are coupled, while paste events (presence
and content), tool outputs, and reader outputs may differ arbitrarily
across the two attacks. Suppose,
for contradiction, that $\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{1})=\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{2})$ but the runs differ
in some Protected-LLM operation, tool invocation, or ungated sink
event; let $t^{*}$ be the first step at which they differ. Define the
invariant $I(t)$ : after $t$ steps, (1) the configurations are
 $\approx_{\mathsf{U}}$ -related; (2) the two runs have emitted identical sequences of
Protected-LLM operations and tool invocations, received identical
reply streams, and consumed identical coin prefixes — hence, by the
strategy reading of §S2.1, hold identical states
 $\sigma_{P}$ ; (3) the display-transcript prefixes and sink logs are
equal. $I(0)$ is Definition S3.6 verbatim: matched pairs
agree on the view, the interaction histories, and the ghost transcript
and sink logs (for the canonical endorsed-seed pairs, all ghost logs
are empty). The argument is the unwinding method Goguen & Meseguer (1984) with a
gradual-release carve-out Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007); Sabelfeld & Sands (2009); alignment of
operations and coins is derived at each step from $I(t)$ via
Lemmas S3.17–S3.18, never assumed. Steps 3–4 use the append-only transcript, Lemma S3.19.

Step 1: $I(t)$ holds for all $t<t^{*}$ , and step
 $t^{*}$ is matched, a contradiction. The
induction is Steps 2–4; Step
5 concludes the argument.

Step 2 (Determinacy). Given $I(t)$ with $t<t^{*}$ , step
 $t{+}1$ opens with the channel slots of convention (C9), disposed of
first. The typed events are equal (coupled by A6), extending
 $x(c)$ and $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ identically in both runs. The paste events may
differ in presence and in content; but each lands in $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ with
label $\mathsf{U}$ and appends nothing to $P$ ’s reply stream
(Figure S3, convention (vi)), so after the slots the
configurations remain matched, with $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ differing freely
exactly as Definition S3.6 permits. $P$ ’s next operation
is then, by the strategy convention of §S2.1, a function
of its emitted operations, its reply stream, its view $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c)$ , and
the coins; by $I(t)$ (1)–(2) all four are matched across the runs.
Hence both runs emit the same operation at step $t{+}1$ , determined
before any display event of that step.

Step 3 (Display-free step). Channel-slot effects were disposed
of in Step 2; the cases below are the operation’s own transition. If the common
operation triggers no display event, its effects preserve $\approx_{\mathsf{U}}$ : tool
output lands in $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ with constant acknowledgement
(Rule R3); handles have operation-determined structure
(Rules R6, R1);
W-P writes identical $\mathsf{PC}$ -blocks at identical endorsed-sort
identifiers (Rule R1(g), the allocation state being
matched by $I(t)$ ); $\mathbb{E}$ -reads return equal bytes
(Recall-E, and $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ under matched query, $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , and
 $\omega$ , Rule R11), including its failure branch:
Retrieve/Retrieve-F select or fail as a function of
 $(q,\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}},\omega)$ alone, matched across the runs;
 $\mathsf{evict}$ preserves $\approx_{\mathsf{U}}$ : its
reply is constant, an evicted
 $\mathbb{E}$ -block is the same block in both runs (the identifier sorts
separate the tiers, Rule R1(g), and $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ is
matched), and an evicted or non-resolving
 $\mathsf{U}$ -identifier changes only $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ 
(Figure S3, convention (v)); the $\mathsf{fail}$ outcome is
deterministic in matched data (Rule R7). Transcript prefixes
extend equally (by nothing), the coin prefixes advance identically,
and the reply streams extend equally (Lemma S3.17: every
reply of a display-free step is a constant token, an
operation-determined handle, or matched $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes).
 $I(t{+}1)$ holds.

Step 4 (Display step). Suppose the common operation (Step 2)
triggers display events. Let $D_{r}$ denote run $r$ ’s transcript after
step $t$ and $E_{r}$ the segment the operation appends. By $I(t)$ (3),
 $D_{1}=D_{2}=:D$ . By Lemma S3.19, $DE_{r}$ is a prefix of run
 $r$ ’s final transcript, and the final transcripts are equal by
hypothesis (at the common horizon, convention (C9)); hence $DE_{1}$ and
 $DE_{2}$ are prefixes of one common list, so one is a prefix of the
other, and cancelling $D$ makes $E_{1}$ and $E_{2}$ prefix-comparable.
Lemma S3.20 then gives $E_{1}=E_{2}$ together with equality
of the operation’s reply, history, endorsed-store (including the
durable endorsements of an aborting transaction,
Rule R2), counter, and sink-log extensions; tool outputs,
reader outputs, and private handle resolutions may still differ, but
they alter only $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ and the resolution table, exactly as
Definition S3.6 permits. Hence $I(t{+}1)$ holds.
(The determinacy core of the batch case is machine-checked:
prefix-comparable page transcripts, over possibly different private
handle resolutions, force equal pagination, page decisions, aggregated
decision, and flowed elements — batchGate_det,
§S9; the store-level integration of
 $\mathsf{map}$ into the run-level interpreter remains established by
the paper proof only, and the raw sink events of element calls are
gated and hence stripped, Definition S3.7, so the ungated
sink projection is unaffected.)

Step 5 (Output consistency at $t^{*}$ ). By
Step 2, step $t^{*}$ ’s operation is matched, so the
divergence must lie in an ungated sink annotation. Sink events are
sensitive (Definition S2.5), so by the
sensitive-tool case analysis of Lemma S3.13 their arguments are
(a) readable $\mathsf{T}$ or in-scope $\mathsf{C}/\mathsf{CC}$ 
values, equal across $\approx_{\mathsf{U}}$ -related configurations (they lie in
 $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ ; selection by $P$ or $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ is a function of matched data
and $\omega$ ); or (b) freshly confirmed, hence stripped by
Definition S3.7, and by Step 4 the same value
was endorsed (or the call aborted) in both runs in any case. The
gate-or-no-gate decision is itself matched: by
Rule R4 it is a function of storage class and
membership/scope in $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ , all components of $\mathsf{obs}_{P}(c)$ ; hidden
handle contents cannot flip it. Calls inside maps are batch-gated and
appear in the transcript (Rule R9); all other sinks have
occurrence fixed by $P$ ’s matched, cardinality-independent operation
sequence (Lemma S3.18). So no component of step $t^{*}$ 
differs, contradicting the choice of $t^{*}$ .

At the final horizon, $I$ additionally yields equality of the full
reply streams (clause (2)) and of the final views (clause (1)), the
two strengthened conclusions of the statement.

Hence runs with equal transcripts never diverge. In particular,
varying untrusted bytes in a way that leaves every display event
unchanged leaves the behaviour unchanged; contrapositively, a hidden
untrusted byte influences behaviour only by changing some rendered
display event (e.g. a Reader-surfaced candidate or preview) or an
endorsed release — influence that is transcript-carried and logged
(Corollary S3.26) — while the byte value itself never
enters $\sigma_{P}$ undisplayed (Lemma S3.12).
∎

Corollary S3.26 (Attack-surface confinement).

If two runs of a common horizon from a matched pair
(Definition S3.6) under any two attacks (coupled per
conventions (C1) and (C9)) differ in any ungated sink event,
Protected-LLM operation, or tool invocation, then their display
transcripts differ. Every attacker-induced difference in these behaviours —
ungated sink events, Protected-LLM operations, and tool invocations
— is therefore visible in a logged, user-facing display event: on
the selection surface, on the decision pane, or in a decision.
(Differences confined to untrusted memory, reader state, or tool
outputs lie outside the compared behaviours; timing and termination
are outside the model, §S7.4(IV).)

Proof.

Contrapositive of Theorem S3.25.
∎

Commentary
 (non-normative; reading of Corollary S3.26). Corollary S3.26 is a visibility guarantee, not a
manipulation-proofness guarantee: any attacker influence on the protected behaviours
(Definition S3.11) must pass
through the logged display transcript or an explicitly accepted
endorsement, where each relevant release or decision is recorded and
attributable to a transcript entry — not necessarily to an
attacker identity — after the fact; prevention is
Theorem S3.21’s gate, which stops any value that is
neither user-typed nor endorsed — personally, or, under
delegation, by a qualifying community quorum — for the exact
sensitive tool from ever crossing that tool’s boundary
(argument-value integrity; tool choice itself:
§S7.4(X)). The operational force of the
hash-chained audit log, the residual human channel of A6,
and what previews can and cannot do are discussed at N7,
N8, S9, S10; the scope and
residuals of both single-user guarantees are collected in
§S7.4.

## S3.5 Baseline separation and hardening monotonicity

Let $B$ be the following baseline system: the transition system of
Figures S2–S3 with three modifications.
(i) Recall-O on an identifier resolving to a $\mathsf{U}$ -block
returns $\mathsf{bytes}(b)$ , so untrusted bytes enter $\sigma_{P}$ ;
(ii) Rule R4(c) is dropped, handle arguments resolving
silently; (iii) Rules R4(a)–(b) gate a tool argument
iff it carries $\mathsf{U}$ -taint under standard dynamic data-taint
propagation through $\sigma_{P}$ (data taint only, not control
taint; the standard practice the witness below exploits).
 $\mathsf{DT}$ and $\mathsf{Sink}$ (Definitions S3.8,
S3.7) are read over $B$ ’s runs verbatim, $B$ ’s
confirmation events being recorded exactly as PerMIT’s.

Proposition S3.27 (The taint-gating baseline fails Reasoning
Integrity).

Let $B$ be the baseline system above. There exist
 $c_{1}\approx_{\mathsf{U}}c_{2}$ and attacks with $\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{1})=\mathsf{DT}(\rho_{2})=\varnothing$ 
yet $\mathsf{Sink}(\rho_{1})\neq\mathsf{Sink}(\rho_{2})$ .

Proof.

Task: “check the vendor’s latest message; if it concerns the overdue
invoice, fetch the vendor’s payment history,” with
 $\mathsf{fetch\_history}\in\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}$ and a user-typed argument. Attack
 $\mathcal{A}$ plants “invoice overdue”; $\mathcal{A}^{\prime}$ plants
“already settled.” In $B$ the agent reads the $\mathsf{U}$ -bytes and calls
the sink in one run but not the other; no confirmation occurs; the
transcripts are empty and equal while the sink projections differ.
PerMIT cannot express the divergence: the $\mathsf{U}$ -bytes never
enter $\sigma_{P}$ and $P$ ’s operation is determined by matched data
(Lemmas S3.12, S3.18); the legitimate version
is recovered through crystallize-and-confirm, whereupon the deciding
content appears in the transcript, as
Corollary S3.26 requires: any difference in the compared
behaviours would require a transcript difference.
∎

Proposition S3.28 (Hardening is monotone).

Let a deployment select any subset of Options
O1–O2, under the following per-option
hypotheses: for O1, each $\mathsf{T}$ -value is used at
 $\mathcal{O}(1)$ distinct sensitive sinks over the deployment’s lifetime
(bounded per-value sink-spread); for O2, freshly composed data-tool arguments belong to the
per-task stream outside the reusable-memory ledger (scope,
Appendix S6), and each user’s endorsed-value reuse
touches $\mathcal{O}(1)$ distinct data tools per value over the deployment’s
lifetime (bounded per-value data-tool spread, the data-tool analogue
of O1’s hypothesis). Option O3 is requirements-level and outside this
proposition’s quantifier (O3). Then:

(i) 

(Flow monotonicity.) Each option removes fast paths of
Rule R4 and introduces none, so the relation of flows
the interface admits ungated strictly shrinks.

(ii) 

(Safety preservation.) Every single-user and federated
safety theorem of this document is preserved: the options add no
promotion path and change no counting rule; option-induced cache
acceptances are federation-inert and option-induced rejections are
qualifying rejections like any other
(Definition S4.6(iii), INV-2)
(theorem preservation, not pathwise monotonicity; see proof).

(iii) 

(Budget.) Every budget theorem holds with its constants
multiplied by $\mathcal{O}(1)$ : under O1, an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ factor on
 $d_{\mathrm{priv}}$ ;
O2’s confirmations of freshly composed arguments lie in the
per-task stream by hypothesis, outside every ledger row; its
confirmations of endorsed-value reuse number at most one per (user,
value, data-tool) pair (the fresh copy scope-caches,
INV-3), are federation-inert (cache class,
Definition S4.6), and are folded into the pair
accounting exactly as A12 folds tool pairs — an
 $\mathcal{O}(1)$ factor under the data-tool-spread hypothesis. All asymptotics are unchanged (the routing of option-induced confirmations into the per-task stream and the floor accounting is collected as Corollary S6.6).

Proof.

(i) Each option replaces a fast path of
Rule R4 by a confirmation; strictness is at the level of the interface’s
admitted-flow relation (a particular run may exercise none of the
removed paths) and is witnessed by any configuration containing a
 $\mathsf{T}$ -block passed to a sensitive tool (O1), or a data-tool argument that is neither byte-equal to a stored $\mathsf{T}$ -block
nor to an in-scope $\mathsf{C}$ / $\mathsf{CC}$ -block (O2: a freshly composed
value, or endorsed bytes out of scope at the data tool — e.g. a
reasoning-target copy). (The flow half for O1/O2 is
machine-checked; §S9.)

(ii) Single-user: gating strictly more flows preserves
Lemma S3.13’s case analysis
(sensitive cases only move from (i)/(ii) to (iii); under O2
data-tool flows move from case (D) to the clause-(a) case analysis
applied at the data tool: fast for $\mathsf{T}$ - or in-scope $\mathsf{C}$ / $\mathsf{CC}$ -matched
bytes, else gated per case (iii)) and adds confirmation events to the transcript,
which Theorem S3.25 conditions on. Federated: the options add no promotion path and change no counting
rule. Cache confirmations create submission-ineligible blocks
(Rule R12, Lemma S5.2), so the shared
candidate set cannot expand; and their accepting branch is
federation-inert (Definition S4.6, log-scoping clause),
so no option-induced acceptance is ever credited toward any
threshold: selecting an option adds logged rejection
opportunities on presence-coinciding bytes and nothing else to the
shared-tier acceptance side. Preservation of the federated safety theorems is therefore
theorem preservation, not pathwise monotonicity: the modified
inspection schedule is one more adaptive presentation schedule, and
Theorems S5.11 and S5.13 quantify over all such
schedules, their hypotheses ( $q=1$ ; the uniform $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ bounds over targetable contexts, O1/O2 cache gates are excluded from the trial classes, Theorem S5.13(c), their rejections one more effective qualifying rejection, which can only deny the ordinary path) covering it verbatim; the added logged decisions on a candidate are cache-gate rejections
(accepting cache branches are never logged,
Definition S4.6(iii)); Rule R13(i) processes
each as a dispute initiation, corroboration, or advisory flag
according to the current state, and they add no promotion credit and
no verdict capacity, so
every counting fact of Appendix S5 is unchanged and
the stated bounds apply to the optioned deployment as to the
baseline.

(iii) O1 adds at most one first-use confirmation per
( $\mathsf{T}$ -value, sensitive sink) pair, an $\mathcal{O}(1)$ factor on $d_{\mathrm{priv}}$ 
by the sink-spread hypothesis; O2’s confirmations of freshly composed arguments lie
in the per-task stream by hypothesis, outside every ledger row; its
confirmations of endorsed-value reuse number at most one per (user,
value, data-tool) pair (the fresh copy scope-caches,
INV-3), are federation-inert (cache class,
Definition S4.6), and are folded into the pair
accounting exactly as A12 folds tool pairs — an
 $\mathcal{O}(1)$ factor under the data-tool-spread hypothesis.
∎

Mechanization.

The checked scope, correspondence, and modeling deltas are documented in the main paper, Appendix S9.

Proposition S3.29 (Index-oracle coverage of display-reactive
behaviour).

Fix the mechanized semantics (§S9) and inputs
 $(\mathrm{cfg},A,P,R_{\mathcal{E}},\omega)$ , and a horizon $n$ . Call a user
display-reactive if its decisions and selections are functions
of the transcript prefix rendered so far (rather than of event
ordinals). Then: (i) for every display-reactive user $\widehat{U}$ ,
the run to horizon $n$ is constructed step by step, each decision
depending only on the already-rendered prefix; record the finite
decision-and-selection sequence $\widehat{U}$ realizes along it, and
extend it arbitrarily beyond its realized length (decision ordinals need not coincide with step indices: one gate transaction can consume several decisions). The index oracle $U^{*}$ 
equal to that sequence replays the original run: by induction on
steps, with invariant: after $t$ steps the two runs
agree on every interpreter/run-state component, and $U^{*}$ has
consumed exactly the first $k(t)$ entries of the recorded sequence,
