where $k(t)$ is the number of decisions and selections
 $\widehat{U}$ ’s run consumed in $t$ steps. A step consuming no
decision preserves the invariant trivially (matched states, same
operation, same transition); a step consuming $j\geq 1$ user responses (decisions or selections)
consumes them at gate events determined by the matched state, in
order, so $U^{*}$ ’s entries $k(t){+}1,\dots,k(t){+}j$ are consumed
at exactly the events where $\widehat{U}$ gave them, and the states
re-align at the step’s close. Hence every mechanized theorem,
quantifying over all index-oracle users, covers $\widehat{U}$ ’s run.
(ii) For the two-run theorem (reasoning_integrity_from):
given two attacks and a display-reactive $\widehat{U}$ , transcript
equality pins every datum $\widehat{U}$ could react to (each consumed
decision and selection is recorded with its displayed bytes,
Remark S3.9), so the two realized finite sequences
coincide; instantiating the mechanized statement at that common index
oracle yields the conclusion for $\widehat{U}$ . The same construction
covers history-adaptive attackers via their realized output
sequences.

## Appendix S4 The Federated Protocol (PerMIT-Attest)

Only Confirmed content is shared: generalizable atomic values and
crystallized knowledge an owner has verified and explicitly published;
 $\mathsf{T}$ - and $\mathsf{U}$ -blocks are never candidates
(Lemma S5.2). The tier is built on machinery network
security has already formalised: threshold endorsement
Maurer (1996), transparency and revocation Laurie et al. (2013); Laurie et al. (2021),
and the identity-cost requirement of the Sybil literature
Douceur (2002); Dwork & Naor (2007), the precise reason the scaling
results are conditional on an attested or cost-bounded setting. The
dispute mechanism is a bounded, demand-paced jury whose competence
condition is the classical one Grofman et al. (1983).
(Lineage: N10.)

The federated layer is specified as a protocol over the log service of
convention (C3): its state transitions (promotion, suspension, demotion,
freeze) are given by Rules R12–R13 as deterministic
log-event semantics, each transition a function of the prior log and the
triggering event. This layer is not integrated into the
single-user small-step relation of §S2.2; no theorem in this
document requires that integration. The single-user theorems consume the
shared tier only through the live-entry test of Rule R4(a);
in the federated two-run comparison the log is an exogenous coupled input
stream (A8), and Proposition S5.10 extends
Theorems S3.21 and S3.25 on exactly this basis.

## S4.1 Identities, pools, epochs, and demand

Weighted attestation and the corruption model.

Each attested identity $i$ carries weight $w_{i}\in[w_{\min},w_{\max}]$ ,
 $w_{\min}>0$ , assigned by the attestation layer on cryptographic facts,
never by a model. Because endorsements and verdicts persist on
the log, the adversary’s resources are counted over the deployment’s
lifetime: $f$ is the number of distinct identities the adversary
ever controls, $W_{\mathrm{adv}}\leq f\cdot w_{\max}$ the total attestation
weight those identities ever carry while controlled, and $f_{\mathrm{s}}\leq f$ 
the number of ever-controlled identities that ever meet the panel-seat
floor $w_{\mathrm{seat}}$ . This is a static-corruption reading: an identity once
controlled is counted adversarial forever, so a mobile
adversary, controlling different identities at different times, is
covered with $f$ counting every identity it ever touches.
Peak-simultaneous bounds would not suffice here, precisely because
persistent endorsements accumulate across control periods. Because
 $f$ , $W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ , and $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ are lifetime totals, reputation farming cannot
carry the adversary past any cap, and penalties that shrink
adversarial capacity leave every bound stated against them valid
(N12, S3). Deployments whose attestation layer
revokes compromised identities may additionally cancel their
endorsements (Definition S4.6(c) counts only
un-revoked endorsers), tightening the effective $f$ below the
lifetime total; no proof relies on this.

Canonical tools; singleton scoping.

Tool identifiers are canonical across the federation: the same
capability carries the same identifier at every deployment, fixed in
TCB configuration and covered by attestation. All community
endorsement, promotion, and reuse is per (value, tool) pair: an
endorsement names the exact tool displayed on the endorser’s decision
pane, promotion accrues one tool at a time, and a $\mathsf{CC}$ scope contains
exactly the tools at which the community threshold was met. There is no
endorsement transfer between tools, however similar.
(Scope of this discipline: as at the endorsement-unit paragraph of §S2.1.)

The reasoning pseudo-target.

The target name $\mathsf{reasoning}$ is registered as one more
canonical accounting target of the federation: it belongs to neither
 $\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}$ nor $\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}$ , is the subject of no invocation, and is never
matched by the scope test of Rule R4(a) for any tool.
Every per-(value, tool) counting rule of this appendix
(Definitions S4.4–S4.6,
Rules R12–R14) applies verbatim to the pair
 $(v,\mathsf{reasoning})$ : reasoning-context inspections gather
promotion weight at $\mathsf{reasoning}$ , and a reasoning-context
qualifying rejection freezes the item like any other. Promotion to
 $\mathsf{CC}$ at $\mathsf{reasoning}$ makes the entry’s bytes readable
community-wide, through the live endorsed view
(Definition S4.1), with no personal narrative
confirmation (A5); it authorizes no tool flow through any
scope test: a $\{\mathsf{reasoning}\}$ scope matches no member of
 $\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}\cup\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}$ , so the sensitive fast path remains closed to it at
every tool; at data tools, the baseline Rule R4(b)
admits any readable $\mathbb{E}$ -bytes when literal-expressed — the
disclosed post-endorsement channel of
Corollary S3.23(b); handle-expressed use gates
regardless (Rule R4(c)) — and under
Option O2 the data fast path is closed to it likewise (the
exact-target test). Locally, an accepting reasoning
confirmation continues to write its fresh $\mathsf{C}$ copy with empty
tool scope (convention (vii)): the pseudo-target names the logged
decision, never a whitelist scope; and a repeated $\mathsf{confirm}$ on locally
readable bytes at $\mathsf{reasoning}$ is a covered gate
(Rule R13(i), reasoning coverage), so reasoning pairs obey
the single-approval cap exactly as tool pairs (INV-3), the
budget’s $c_{\mathrm{tool}}$ count including $\mathsf{reasoning}$ on this basis
(A12).

Two-tier storage of community status.

Community status is a property of the shared tier, never of any
local store. A promoted item is represented by a shared-tier entry
(bytes, per-tool scope, status), backed by its promotion certificate
on the federation log; what a user’s agent holds locally is at most a
reference. The fast-path test of Rule R4(a) for the
 $\mathsf{CC}$ branch is evaluated against the live shared entry at call
time: a value flows on community authority iff the shared entry is
currently valid (promoted, not suspended, not demoted, not retired)
and names the tool. Demotion, suspension, and retirement therefore act
by invalidating or flagging the shared entry, withdrawing community
authority from every user who has not personally confirmed, while no
local block of any user is created, altered, or removed by any of
them: a personally confirmed $\mathsf{C}$ copy is a distinct local object and
continues to license the pair it was endorsed for
(Lemma S6.1). This is the storage reading under
which Lemma S5.8 and Proposition S5.10 are
stated, and it is the federated form of
Definition S3.3(iii): every $\mathsf{CC}$ authority in force is backed
by a currently valid certificate.

Definition S4.1 (Federated product configuration; live endorsed view).

A federated configuration is $\widehat{c}=(c,L)$ : a local extended
configuration $c$ (Definition S2.6) and the federation
log $L$ (convention (C3)). The shared-tier state
 $S_{\mathrm{sh}}(L)$ is the deterministic replay of $L$ under
Rules R12–R14; $\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ denotes the
set of virtual community entries of $S_{\mathrm{sh}}(L)$ that
are currently promoted, unsuspended, undemoted, and unretired, each
carrying its exact per-tool scope from its promotion certificate.
Virtual entries are shared-tier objects in a namespace disjoint from
both local identifier sorts; they are members of no local store. The
federated endorsed view is
 $\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{fed}}_{\mathbb{E}}(\widehat{c}):=\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}(c)\uplus\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ , and the federated Protected-LLM view replaces
 $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ by $\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{fed}}_{\mathbb{E}}$ in
Definition S2.3. Local transitions read
 $\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ only through the $\mathsf{CC}$ branch of
Rule R4(a); log transitions change only $L$ (hence the
virtual view) and never any local block
(Lemma S5.8).

Definition S4.2 (Federated step; log slot).

The federated product system extends the slot structure of
convention (C9) with a log slot preceding the typed slot: a
federated interpreter step first appends the step’s exogenous
log-event prefix (possibly empty) to $L$ — under A8,
coupled identically across compared runs — then delivers the typed
and paste slots, then $P$ emits one operation, executed against the
federated view
 $\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{fed}}_{\mathbb{E}}=\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}\uplus\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ with $L$ 
as delivered by this step’s log slot. Locally generated
federation events — all events the
federated definitions specify for local acts
(Definitions S4.4, S4.6,
Rule R12): the $\mathsf{submit}(v)$ of Publish-Acc
and the signed protocol-bearing gate decisions being the principal
examples, realized demand events (e.g. a dereference of a live
community entry) included — are appended to $L$ atomically
at the close of the emitting operation’s transition, after its local
effects; the $\mathsf{CC}$ branch of Rule R4(a) is evaluated,
within the invocation’s atomic step, against the prefix delivered by
that step’s log slot, so a step’s own appended events never affect
its own gate. In the two-run comparison these locally appended events
are equal across the compared runs — each is a function of matched
components (the emitted operation, the displayed bytes, and the
logged decision, all transcript-pinned; a demand event, arising from a matched operation against the matched federated view, likewise) — so the full delivered logs coincide: the exogenous prefixes by A8, the local suffixes by this derivation. Log-slot transitions
acknowledge the log service, never $P$ : nothing is appended to $P$ ’s
reply stream, no local block is created, altered, or removed
(Lemma S5.8), and the only $P$ -visible effect is
through $\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ , the deterministic replay of the
delivered prefix (Definition S4.1). The product system
is specified on paper only; it lies outside the mechanized surface
(§S9).

Commentary
 (non-normative; distributed realizations of the live-entry test). The abstract model linearizes each invocation at a definite log
position: the call’s $\mathsf{CC}$ check reads the prefix delivered to its
step, so a call and a concurrent demotion are ordered — the call
linearizes before or after the demotion, never against a stale view.
A distributed deployment realizes this by pinning each invocation to
its interpreter’s delivered log position (a per-call certificate);
the fidelity of that delivery is a freshness obligation of the TCB
log service (convention (C3)), and the delegated guarantee
(Proposition S5.10(i)) is stated relative to the delivered
prefix.

Definition S4.3 (Identity pools).

The attestation layer partitions attested identities into pools
on cryptographic and organizational facts (attestation provenance,
organization, deployment cohort), fixed TCB configuration, never
assigned or influenced by any model. An identity’s pool is set at
attestation and immutable thereafter; the adversary’s $f$ identities
occupy whatever pools their attestations place them in and can
concentrate in at most $f$ pools. The diversity requirement demands
that ordinary promotion’s $N$ endorsers span at least $D_{\mathrm{pool}}$ distinct
pools with at most $\lceil N/D_{\mathrm{pool}}\rceil$ counted per pool, and panels
accept at most $\lceil k/D_{\mathrm{pool}}\rceil$ verdicts per pool
(Rule R13(iii)). Deployment parameters satisfy $1\leq D_{\mathrm{pool}}\leq N$ (with $N$ endorsers
one cannot span more than $N$ pools, so larger $D_{\mathrm{pool}}$ makes ordinary
promotion unsatisfiable) and $D_{\mathrm{pool}}\leq k$ where panels are read
against the same pool partition. (What diversity does and does not buy:
N11, S13.)

Definition S4.4 (Demand).

A user demands the pair $(v,t)$ at the first interpreter event in
the epoch (convention (C4)) at which the user’s own attempted use of $v$ 
at tool $t$ fires the gate of Rule R4 (because $v$ is
unpromoted at $t$ , disputed, outside the user’s local scopes, or
expressed through a handle — which gates at every tool regardless of
any live community entry, Rule R4(c))
— a covered gate (Rule R13(i)) is not a demand event,
nor is a cache confirmation of Options O1–O2 a
log-demand event on either branch (accept-inert on acceptance,
Definition S4.6(iii); its rejecting branch, though
protocol-bearing, initiates or corroborates without registering
demand — while the attempted use firing it realizes the user’s
latent indicator per the required-approval clause,
§S6.1) — or
dereferences $v$ as a $\mathsf{CC}$ -block at $t$ , or the user’s own accepting
or gating non-covered reasoning-context confirmation of $v$ (a covered reasoning gate, Rule R13(i), is not a demand event, exactly as a covered tool gate is not), or a dereference of a
live $\mathsf{CC}$ -at- $\mathsf{reasoning}$ entry of $v$ — demand of the pair
 $(v,\mathsf{reasoning})$ . A user demands the item
 $v$ at their first demand of any pair $(v,t)$ ; $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v}$ counts distinct realized
demanders of $v$ within the epoch and $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v,t}$ counts distinct
demanders of the pair: a user who uses $v$ at several tools contributes
to each corresponding $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v,t}$ and once to $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v}$ , so $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v,t}\leq n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v}$ ; the reference model’s latent counts $n_{v},n_{v,t}$ (§S6.1) dominate these pathwise (§S8.1).
Demand order is the log order
of these first events (convention (C3)). The owner’s creating
confirmation of $v$ , and any publish decision
(itself federation-inert as an inspection,
Definition S4.6), is a demand event,
so every item has, in each epoch of its publication or re-entry, $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v}\geq 1$ with its owner among its realized
demanders; in the reference model the owner-coupling convention
(§S6.1) mirrors this latently, so $n_{v}\geq 1$ holds
in those epochs as well. A retired item is no longer surfaced
(Rule R14), so no further confirmations accrue to its
shared-tier ledger; the same bytes encountered later through a user’s
own channels are ordinary personal content, counted in the single-user
stream, not in $v$ ’s ledger (this ledger-residual demand is bounded pathwise by
Theorem S6.2(i), persistence exceptions per
INV-3; the aggregate expected per-user contribution of its
benign, A11-conforming component vanishes under the
vanishing conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii), while poisoned
or assumption-violating demand remains in
 $r^{(e)}_{\mathrm{bad}}$ , Corollary S6.10).

Definition S4.5 (Shared-presence freshness).

An attested identity’s inspection of a shared candidate $v$ is
fresh if the identity has no prior logged decision
(a protocol-bearing decision: accepting endorsement, qualifying rejection, or panel verdict; Definition S4.6) on
 $v$ ’s exact bytes; otherwise it is repeat. Decisions are
logged exactly when protocol-bearing (Definition S4.6), so freshness is
shared-presence-first, not life-first: a personal decision on
the same bytes taken outside any shared-tier presence (before first
publication, or after retirement) is not logged and does not consume
freshness — a disclosed boundary of the byte-permanence guarantee
(INV-1); the behavioural parameters $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}},p_{b}$ are
accordingly read over the log-defined fresh class
(Remark S5.19). Freshness is decidable from the federation log, is
permanent while so defined, and never renews: across entries,
dormancy, freezes, suspensions, and epochs within and across the
bytes’ shared-tier presences, one logged decision per identity is
final. All promotion weight and all panel verdicts are carried by
fresh inspections (Definition S4.6,
Rule R13(iii)); a repeat inspection can contribute only a
rejection. This is INV-1; the safety of the asymmetry is
INV-2 (rationale: Remark S4.7).

Definition S4.6 (Endorsement counting and banking).

A published item enters the promotion pipeline by submission
(Rule R12); each pipeline entry opens a fresh finalisation
window of length $T_{\mathrm{win}}$ at (re-)submission.
A gate decision is protocol-bearing for a shared candidate iff
all of the following hold: (i) its exact bytes have, at decision time,
a shared-tier presence (a candidate or $\mathsf{CC}$ entry that is not
retired; frozen and suspended presences included, since freeze- and
suspension-time decisions are precisely the verdicts, corroborations,
and bankable credits the counting rules consume); (ii) its target is a
canonical tool in $\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}\cup\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}$ or the canonical pseudo-target
 $\mathsf{reasoning}$ (§S4.1); (iii) the gate is not a
covered gate (Rule R13(i)); and, if
it is a cache confirmation of Options O1–O2, its
outcome is rejecting — cache gates are accept-inert (their
accepting branch is never protocol-bearing), while their rejecting
branch on presence-coinciding bytes is protocol-bearing like any
rejection (INV-2); (iv) its outcome is a durable accepting
pair-confirmation or a rejecting review
(Definition S2.5). Only protocol-bearing decisions are
appended to the federation log as decisions on the candidate: only
they consume shared-presence freshness, carry promotion weight,
initiate or corroborate disputes, or become panel verdicts. A
publish decision is logged as a submission event
(Publish-Acc, Rule R12), not as an inspection: it
carries no promotion weight and consumes no freshness, while
remaining a demand event for its item
(Definition S4.4). All other confirmations — personal
content — are purely local events with no federated effect and no
freshness consequence for shared-tier counting. Cache confirmations
are accept-inert regardless of whether their bytes coincide
with a shared-tier presence: their accepting branch is never logged as
a shared-candidate decision (no promotion weight, no credit, no
notification cohort, no freshness consumption); their rejecting branch
on presence-coinciding bytes remains a qualifying rejection like any
other (INV-2), consuming freshness as any logged rejection
does. (Their accepting branch, being unlogged, retains the identity’s
freshness on the bytes; the acceptance itself carries no weight, and
any later non-cache decision by the same identity is one more fresh
decision inside the log-defined class the weakest-target parameters
 $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}},p_{b}$ are already required to cover, Remark S5.19.)
Inertness and coverage are decided by interpreter-only,
attacker-immutable attributes (provenance,
Definition S2.2; the owner-side coverage test,
Rule R13(i)).

(a) 

Once per identity per item, keyed to bytes, log-scoped,
authority-gated. An identity’s first logged accepting decision
on $v$ ’s bytes (Definition S4.5) is its one weight-bearing
endorsement provided the identity holds dispute-initiation
authority at the moment of the decision (not rejection-suspended,
below its ceiling $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ , with a free $r_{\max}$ slot, and within its
per-item initiation allowance, Rule R13(i),(v)); it is
counted at the single tool that decision names and toward the pipeline
entry open at the time of the decision, subject to the credit caps of
clause (f). An accepting decision by an identity lacking that
authority is logged, consumes freshness, and creates the decider’s
local $\mathsf{C}$ copy (Lemma S6.1), but is
authority-inert: it carries no promotion weight, is never
credited (in-entry, banked, or vindication-credited), and joins no
notification cohort. Authority is a deterministic predicate of the
prior log (Rule R13(v)), evaluated interpreter-side at the
decision’s header atom and recorded with it. Every later acceptance by
the same identity (at any tool, in any entry, in any epoch, in any
shared-tier presence) carries no weight (INV-1).

(b) 

Banking. A first decision recorded during dormancy (window
closed, not yet resubmitted or retired) or during a freeze with no open
entry is banked: credited as endorsement weight at its tool when
the owner’s next entry opens (subject to clause (f)), a deterministic relabel of the
already-logged event, not a new confirmation and not a finalisation.
Banked credits expire if the item retires first (INV-4).

(c) 

Finalisation only at window end. Within an entry, $v$ is
promoted to $\mathsf{CC}$ at a specific tool when the weight of distinct,
un-revoked accepting endorsers at that tool reaches $W$ in credited endorsements (unweighted:
 $N$ credited endorsers; crediting per clause (f)), drawn from at least $D_{\mathrm{pool}}$ diverse pools, with
finalisation only at the end of the window; any qualifying rejection
logged within the window intervenes.

(d) 

Dormancy. Between entries the item remains surfaced and
personally usable; a gate confirmation during dormancy is logged as a
decision, consuming freshness, counted in the item’s ledger
(Lemma S6.7), banked per (b), and creating the user’s local
 $\mathsf{C}$ copy, after which their gate never re-fires at that tool
(INV-3).

(e) 

Consequence. The weight-bearing endorsement sets of
successive entries of the same bytes are identity-disjoint across the
entire deployment history, and every weight-bearing endorsement is its
endorser’s fresh inspection of the bytes
(Lemma S5.3).

(f) 

Credit caps (per entry, per tool). Crediting is greedy
in log order and capped: a qualifying accepting decision (in-entry,
or banked and credited at the entry’s opening in log order of the
original decisions) is credited to the entry at its tool iff,
at the moment of crediting, the entry holds fewer than
 $\lceil N/D_{\mathrm{pool}}\rceil$ credits from the decider’s pool at that tool
(Definition S4.3) and fewer than
 $N^{+}:=D_{\mathrm{pool}}\lceil N/D_{\mathrm{pool}}\rceil$ credits at that tool in total. Crediting requires authority at decision time (clause (a)); the caps bind over authority-active credits only.
An uncredited accepting decision retains every personal effect (the
decider’s local $\mathsf{C}$ copy, Lemma S6.1; freshness
consumed, Definition S4.5) but carries no promotion
weight, joins no notification cohort, and is never re-credited. The
caps are promotion-neutral: a pool-capped decision never counted
toward promotion under Definition S4.3 in any case, and
the total cap binds only once the entry holds $N^{+}\geq N$ credits
which, under the per-pool cap, necessarily span at least $D_{\mathrm{pool}}$ 
pools — i.e. only when finalisation at window end is already
enabled. Weighted deployments cap credited weight analogously (per
pool and in total), bounding credited endorsers by
 $\mathcal{O}(W/w_{\min})$ : constants change, never exponents
(Remark S5.30).

An endorsement is not a separate protocol act: it is the endorser’s own
accepting confirmation of $(v,\mathsf{tool})$ at their gate
(Theorem S3.21), signed and appended to the federation
log. On re-entry, the interpreter notifies exactly the weight-bearing
endorsers credited to the immediately preceding entry; no earlier
cohort is ever re-notified, so the notified set per re-entry is bounded
by that single entry’s credited endorsements, at most $N^{+}$ per demanded tool (clause (f)), independently of the
item’s history. Notifications are deduplicated jointly with the
suspension prompts of Rule R13(vi), at most one invitation per
identity per item per epoch. An invitation is valid only in the epoch of its issuance: an unopened
invitation expires at the epoch boundary and is not reissued for the
same re-entry; acting on an invitation (accepting, rejecting, or
declining) is an event of the issuance epoch. Narrowing the cohort weakens nothing: no
safety bound relies on repeat inspections (INV-1,
INV-2), and notifications carry no protocol authority. A
notified endorser’s re-inspection is repeat: its accepting branch
carries no weight, its rejecting branch is a qualifying rejection like
any other (Rule R13(i)). Declining costs nothing
(Theorem S6.2; Remark S4.7).

Commentary
 (non-normative; role of the credit caps). Clause (f) supplies the counting the budget ledger consumes. Without
it, finalisation only at window end would let a popular entry accrue
credited endorsers without bound inside one window, and the
weight-bearing row of Table S2, the notified re-entry
cohort, and the reconsideration term of Lemma S6.7 would
all grow with the item’s popularity. With it, credited endorsers per
(entry, tool) number at most $N^{+}<N+D_{\mathrm{pool}}\leq 2N$ (using
 $D_{\mathrm{pool}}\leq N$ , Definition S4.3), deterministically; the
overflow — in-window accepting demanders beyond the caps — is
within-window demand, counted by the window-bucket row of
Table S2 under A10’s demand clause. Safety is unaffected a
fortiori: promotion still requires $N$ credited fresh endorsements,
Theorem S5.13 lower-bounds honest decisions (of
which credits are a subset), rejections are never capped
(INV-2), and promotion-neutrality (clause (f)) leaves
Assumption A11’s sufficiency reading unchanged.

Remark S4.7 (Why the freshness asymmetry is safe).

The asymmetry of Definition S4.5 (repeat acceptances
inert, repeat rejections still qualifying) is deliberate: a rejection
can only freeze or corroborate, never promote (INV-2), so
re-exposure of bytes to a previously fooled inspector can help the
defence but never the attack, and the defence never rests on a person
catching on a second logged look what they missed on the first: no logged repeat
inspection carries weight. (Unlogged personal inspections outside a
shared-tier presence are outside this discharge,
Definition S4.5; their calibration consequence is
Remark S5.19.) Banking is safe in both directions per INV-4 (as restated there:
starting weight only, window’s rejection opportunity never bypassed,
identity-disjointness preserved).
Declining a re-entry notification costs the decliner nothing: a user
who has personally confirmed holds a local in-scope $\mathsf{C}$ copy and
reuses it at that tool without any gate (INV-3); the floor distinguishes such
voluntary repeat acts from required confirmations
(Theorem S6.2(ii), Remark S6.4).

Definition S4.8 (Reach-free bound).

A safety bound is reach-free if it does not depend on the number
of honest identities to whom the item is presented (its reach),
only on protocol constants and the adversary’s resources
(INV-6), and the stated behavioural parameters $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ , read as weakest-target infima (INV-6).

## S4.2 Protocol rules

The rules below are normative; all rationale lives in
Appendix S7 under the keys cited, or in the marked
commentary blocks of §S1.1. Clause numbering of
Rule R13 preserves the roman numerals (i)–(vii) cited
throughout.

Rule R12 (Sharing eligibility and the publish gate).

Only $\mathsf{C}$ -labelled blocks with provenance $\mathsf{confirmed}$ (never
 $\mathsf{cache}$ , Options O1–O2) are eligible for
submission, by a deterministic label-and-provenance membership test
(provenance is interpreter-only, Definition S2.2, hence
attacker-immutable); a model may nominate but cannot submit;
submission requires the owner’s accepting
 $\mathsf{confirm}(v,\mathrm{publish})$ on the decision pane
(Publish-Acc), recorded in the owner’s transcript, which
emits one signed $\mathsf{submit}(v)$ event to the federation log and
creates the candidate’s shared-tier presence; it creates no local
block and consumes no freshness (Definition S4.6). Each
identity emits at most $\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ submission events per epoch; re-entry
of an item into the promotion pipeline counts as a fresh submission, and a submission of bytes whose per-epoch entry cap is exhausted (Rule R14(i)) is ineligible.

Required deployment parameters.

A deployment instantiating
Rule R13 fixes, as TCB configuration: the measurement window
and minimum sample size of the charged-rejection rate trigger (v)(c);
the suspension and cool-down durations of (v)(c) and (vii); the
escalation schedule and the attestation-weight reduction function of
(vii); the derivation-cascade relation of (iv); and the
lifetime-account trigger of (v)(f) where selected. The theorems below
consume only the explicit caps and threshold properties stated in
their hypotheses ( $r_{\max}$ , $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ , $\theta_{\mathrm{acc}}>1-p_{b}$ , $k$ , $m$ ,
 $E_{\max}$ , $c_{\mathrm{p}}$ ); no proof depends on the unspecified schedule shapes.

Rule R13 (Dispute and demand-paced panel resolution).

(i) 

Qualifying rejection $=$ protocol-bearing gate rejection.
Every attested identity’s rejecting decision on a shared candidate at
its own protocol-bearing confirmation gate
(Definition S4.6(iii): tool- or reasoning-target,
non-covered; cache gates included on their rejecting branch)
is automatically a signed, logged qualifying rejection. There is no
separate filing step and no rejection weight floor: inspection and
report are one act. A user who merely
abandons the dialog records nothing (abstention is counted as a miss
inside $1-q$ ; Remark S5.19). Each identity holds at most $r_{\max}$ open
(unresolved) rejections.
Covered gates are inert. A gate event fired by a
handle-expressed use (Rule R4(c)) of a pair for which
the identity already holds an in-scope local $\mathsf{C}$ copy — a
covered gate; a literal-expressed reuse takes the fast path
(Rule R4(a)) and never gates — is protocol-inert for
the shared tier, on both branches: its accepting decision carries no
promotion weight, consumes no freshness, and joins no notification
cohort (a redundant confirmation, §S6.1); its
rejecting decision is logged as corroboration if the item is frozen
and as an advisory flag otherwise — no dispute initiation, no
verdict, no accountability exposure. Coverage is decided owner-side on
the identity’s own local store, a deterministic test independent of
any shared-tier or attacker-influenced state.
Reasoning coverage. A gate event with target
 $\mathsf{reasoning}$ on bytes of which the identity’s local store
holds any readable $\mathbb{E}$ -level block ( $\mathsf{T}$ , $\mathsf{PC}$ , or $\mathsf{C}$ of any
scope; Rule R5, Definition S2.6) is
likewise a covered gate, protocol-inert on both branches exactly as a
tool-target covered gate: its accepting decision is a redundant
confirmation (§S6.1) — no promotion weight, no
freshness consumption, no notification cohort — and its rejecting
decision is logged as corroboration if the bytes are frozen and as an
advisory flag otherwise. The coverage test is owner-side and
deterministic: it reads only the identity’s own local store’s
readability facts, never any shared-tier or attacker-influenced state.
Within an epoch, only an identity’s first rejecting decision
on a given byte string can take effect as a dispute-initiating
rejection; every later rejecting decision by the same identity on
the same bytes within the epoch is logged as corroboration if the
item is frozen, and otherwise as an advisory flag (the
zero-authority class above): no protocol effect, no accountability
exposure. The allowance renews at the epoch boundary. A rejecting
decision that is verdict-eligible under clause (iii) is cast as a
panel verdict regardless of this cap, exactly as under
clause (v)(e); the cap restricts dispute initiation only,
and capped rejections remain visible as flags. An identity whose rejection rights are under
suspension (clause (v)) files advisory flags instead: no
protocol effect, no penalty; flags alert other users.

(ii) 

Disputed state. A qualifying rejection freezes promotion
of $v$ at every tool, immediately, and visibly flags the item. (A
qualifying rejection on bytes holding $\mathsf{CC}$ status at any tool is
instead a post-promotion rejection, routed to clause (vi).) A frozen
item remains retrievable (surfaced flagged,
Rule R14(ii)) and personally usable through each user’s own
gate confirmation; the freeze suspends community promotion only. The
item’s pre-dispute endorsers are notified. Pre-dispute endorsements are
retained on the log for audit and reputation but carry no weight in
resolution.

(iii) 

Panel formation (demand-paced, fresh-juror). The panel is
not a body convened in advance but a capped tally of decisions: it
comprises the first $k$ eligible verdicts logged on the disputed
item, with no global deadline. Because a disputed item is unpromoted,
every demander must personally inspect-and-confirm it to use it
(Theorem S3.21); the interpreter displays the exact bytes at
the demander’s own gate, visibly carrying the dispute flag and the
rejecter’s stated ground. At the moment a demander records a
decision, and only then, the interpreter checks eligibility on log
facts:

(a) 

attestation weight $\geq w_{\mathrm{seat}}$ ;

(b) 

fresh (Definition S4.5);

(c) 

no verdict already cast by this identity on this dispute
instance; verdict eligibility is scoped to the dispute instance for
its entire life: an identity casts at most one verdict on a given
dispute, ever (INV-5);

(d) 

at most $\lceil k/D_{\mathrm{pool}}\rceil$ verdicts per identity pool,
likewise scoped per dispute instance;

(e) 

fewer than $k$ verdicts so far.

A cache gate (Options O1–O2) is never a verdict
surface: its rejecting decision may initiate or corroborate a dispute
per clause (i), but is not verdict-eligible and occupies no panel
seat. (Only a cache gate’s rejecting branch is logged,
Definition S4.6(iii); admitting such gates as verdict
surfaces would make verdict membership outcome-selected there,
against the benign-panel hypothesis of
Proposition S5.20. The exclusion is theorem-compatible and resolves an otherwise outcome-selected surface: Theorem S5.13 and Proposition S5.20 bound tallies over verdicts actually cast, and the eligible class is simply defined without cache gates; no pathwise monotonicity is claimed.)
A dispute instance opens at its qualifying rejection and closes at
resolution or expiry; its verdict tally and pool caps persist unchanged
across epoch boundaries (convention (C4)), and a boundary-spanning
dispute counts toward Rule R14(i) in the epoch in which it
opened. If eligible, that gate decision is the verdict, recorded
once, verdicts ordered by log order of decisions; otherwise the
decision is an ordinary personal confirmation (a bystander rejection
still logs as corroboration). A user who opens the dialog and abandons
it records nothing and occupies nothing: verdict capacity is consumed
only by cast verdicts, so abandonment cannot exhaust or stall the
panel, and no seat allocation, timer, or release mechanism exists. The
dispute, like freshness, is byte-level: the panel adjudicates the
bytes, each juror inspecting them at the juror’s own attempted tool,
with the dispute flag and the rejecter’s stated ground displayed.
Pre-dispute endorsers cast no verdicts: a notified endorser’s
re-inspection is repeat (Definition S4.5); its accepting
branch has no protocol effect, and its rejecting branch is a further
qualifying rejection with no additional protocol effect while the item
is frozen (logged as corroboration). Consequently every verdict-bearing
inspection is the juror’s first logged decision on the bytes
(Definition S4.5; INV-1, INV-2).

(iv) 

Resolution (early and decisive). Verdicts are tallied as
cast. The moment rejecting verdicts reach $m_{\mathrm{rej}}$ :
evicted: demoted to $\mathsf{U}$ in the shared tier, cool-down, cascade
to shared derivations. The moment accepting verdicts reach $m$ :
vindicated: the byte-level freeze lifts and the interrupted
pipeline entry resumes, its window clock excluding the freeze;
vindication consumes neither the owner’s rate limit nor an entry of
Rule R14(i), and it never directly promotes. Each juror’s
accepting verdict is simultaneously that juror’s gate
confirmation at the juror’s own tool (its one fresh decision on the
bytes), credited toward the entry’s per-tool endorsement sets subject
to the authority gate and credit caps of
Definition S4.6(a),(f); it joins those sets together
with the retained pre-dispute endorsements, which carry no weight in
resolution but keep their promotion weight, being fresh inspections the
panel’s vindication never contradicted. Freeze-time bystander
confirmations, fresh accepting gate confirmations by demanders whose
decisions are not verdicts (under-weight, pool-capped, or
post-capacity), are credited identically on vindication at their own
tools (per Definition S4.6(a),(f)); crediting adds no confirmation event
(Lemma S6.7). If no entry was open at the freeze,
vindication lifts the freeze and the item returns to dormant status,
its verdict-confirmations credited on the next entry’s opening subject to Definition S4.6(a),(f), or
expiring with it (Definition S4.6(b),
INV-4). Every promotion, after any number of disputes,
therefore still requires $N$ fresh-inspection endorsements at the
promoted tool itself (Lemma S3.14).

Default bars. The default bars are
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}=k+1-m$ with $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ and $m_{\mathrm{rej}}>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ 
(jointly forcing $k\geq 2f_{\mathrm{s}}+1$ ); their consequences (mutually exclusive
outcomes and decisive, tally-deadlock-free resolution) are
Lemma S5.7. Deployments choosing
looser bars ( $m+m_{\mathrm{rej}}\geq k+2$ ) retain a neither-bar outcome,
in which the item returns to unpromoted candidate status and re-entry
consumes the owner’s rate limit (Rule R12) and gathers a
fresh endorsement set. A disputed item that attracts no new demand for
 $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ expires (Rule R14(i)): retired unpromoted, all open
rejection slots on it freed, no penalty to any party.

(v) 

Rejecter accountability.

(a) 

Panel verdicts are never penalised and never charged.

(b) 

A dispute-initiating rejection is charged when its dispute
ends in vindication, at the moment the vindication is logged, to the
epoch in which that vindication is logged. Rejections whose disputes
end in eviction or expiry charge nothing.

(c) 

The first charge per identity per epoch carries no penalty.
Repeated charges escalate to suspension of rejection rights
only (endorsement weight and ordinary use untouched), restored after
cool-down. Escalation is rate-based, never count-based: the trigger is
the identity’s charged-rejection rate per fresh inspection, over
a minimum sample size, at a threshold $\theta_{\mathrm{acc}}$ calibrated strictly
above the honest baseline $1-p_{b}$ (Remark S5.5).

(d) 

Hard ceiling. Independently of (c): once $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ charges
have accrued to an identity within an epoch, the identity’s further
dispute-initiating rejections for the remainder of the epoch are filed
as advisory flags (the zero-authority class of clause (i)); the
allowance renews at the epoch boundary.

(e) 

Suspension and the ceiling restrict dispute-initiating
rejections only: a rejecting decision that is verdict-eligible under
clause (iii) is cast as a panel verdict regardless of the identity’s
accountability state, so the panel arithmetic of
Theorem S5.13 and Proposition S5.20 is independent
of accountability state; freeze-time corroborating rejections, having
no protocol effect, are logged either way.

(f) 

Deployments may additionally maintain a lifetime
charged-rejection account per identity, with the same per-inspection
normalisation and a comparative (population-outlier) trigger,
suspending rejection rights directly for extreme outliers.

(g) 

Both layers touch rejection rights only, never endorsement
weight or any local store (Lemma S5.8).

(vi) 

Post-promotion rejection. A qualifying rejection against a
promoted item moves it to Suspended. Suspension invalidates the
shared entry for the fast-path test of Rule R4(a)
(§S4.1): community authority is withdrawn for the duration
of the review, so every user’s use of the pair gates — except a user
holding a personally confirmed in-scope local $\mathsf{C}$ copy, who continues
through that copy (Lemma S6.1), unaffected. Suspension
additionally imposes the byte-level freeze of clause (ii) on promotion at
every tool. A fast-track panel (same parameters) attempts completion within $T_{\mathrm{pan}}$ ; failure to complete by $T_{\mathrm{pan}}$ leaves the item Suspended with verdict collection continuing demand-paced, exactly as an ordinary dispute. Its verdicts arise at the gates suspension re-arms:

(a) 

Demanders during suspension: any user demanding the pair
while the entry is suspended (new adopter and prior ungated adopter
alike) gates, and verdict eligibility is decided by the log-fact check of
clause (iii) exactly as in an ordinary dispute. In particular a prior
ungated adopter with no logged decision on the bytes is fresh, while one
with any prior logged decision (a rejection, or an acceptance at
another tool) is repeat and ineligible, its rejecting branch a qualifying
rejection like any other (logged as corroboration while frozen).

(b) 

Prompted re-inspection: the suspension notification carries
a voluntary re-inspection prompt to current adopters not presently
demanding the pair; accepting it re-displays the exact bytes at the
adopter’s own gate under the dispute flag, eligibility again per
clause (iii); an accepting inspection earns the adopter a local $\mathsf{C}$ 
copy, covering all later use of the pair. Declining costs nothing beyond
the suspension itself: the adopter’s next actual use of the pair gates
once, at the floor (Corollary S6.5).
Prompts are issued to at most $c_{\mathrm{p}}k$ adopters per suspension;
re-inspection invitations of either kind, re-entry notifications
(Definition S4.6) and suspension prompts, are
deduplicated jointly, at most one invitation per identity per item per
epoch. Prompts expire at the end of their issuance epoch; a review left
unresolved at the boundary continues demand-paced but issues no new
prompts merely for having crossed it. At most $E_{\max}$ fast-track reviews are opened per item per
epoch; once the cap is reached, the item remains Suspended for the
remainder of the epoch, verdict collection continues demand-paced
exactly as in an ordinary dispute, and further post-promotion
rejections are logged as corroboration of the open review (the
zero-authority class of clause (i): no protocol effect, no
accountability exposure).

The panel decides demotion-with-cascade or reinstatement. If $k$ fresh
verdicts do not arrive within $T_{\mathrm{pan}}$ , the item remains Suspended and
verdict collection continues demand-paced, exactly as an ordinary
dispute; a Suspended item attracting no fresh demand for $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ is
retired from the shared tier (Rule R14(i)): no
reinstatement, no eviction, no accountability consequence for either
side, and every personally confirmed local copy untouched
(Lemma S5.8).

(vii) 

Publisher accountability. When a panel evicts an
item, the publishing identity incurs an escalating penalty: the first
eviction per identity per epoch triggers a submission cool-down;
repeated evictions within an epoch escalate to suspension of
submission rights and reduction of attestation weight. The
penalty touches shared-tier rights and attestation weight only, never
any local store, label, or scope (Lemma S5.8), and is
triggered only by a resolved panel’s eviction verdict, never by
rejections alone and never by expiry
(Proposition S5.22).

Commentary
 (non-normative; role of clause (v)). Clause (v)’s induced deterministic bound $c_{\mathrm{acc}}=c_{\mathrm{ceil}}+r_{\max}$ is
Lemma S5.4; these clauses enter only the
budget constants of Appendix S6, never any safety
theorem (Figure S1). Calibration guidance and the honest
cost analysis: Remark S5.5, Remark S5.19, S15.

Remark S4.9 (Per-pair dispute scoping (deployment variant)).

A deployment may scope disputes and panels per (value, tool) pair: a
qualifying rejection then freezes promotion at the rejected pair
only, and jurors are drawn from that pair’s demanders, adjudicating
the rejected tool’s context. This removes the cross-tool-jury
residual of S4: the applicable $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ becomes the
targeted tool’s. The costs are per-pair panel formation and freeze
scope, panels and prompts multiplying by up to $c_{\mathrm{tool}}$ per item, and
a re-keying decision — whether freshness and verdict capacity
remain byte-level or become pair-level — that this document does
not make; an analogous per-attempt bound is plausible but not formally instantiated here, the freshness and verdict-capacity re-keying being deliberately undecided. No theorem of this document
consumes the variant.

Rule R14 (Candidate hygiene).

The shared candidate store is curated by three deterministic,
interpreter-applied policies.

(i) 

Entry cap and expiry. At most $E_{\max}$ pipeline entries
open per exact byte string per epoch — promoted, evicted, and
unpromoted entries alike, republications and re-entries included; a
submission of bytes whose per-epoch entry cap is exhausted is
ineligible (Rule R12) and takes the uniform reject of
Publish-Rej, eligibility returning at the epoch boundary.
The cap is availability-only: banked credits and any live promotion
status persist across the boundary (convention (C4); N14).
Additionally, a published candidate is retired from the shared tier
when any of the following holds:

(a) 

it completes $E_{\max}$ pipeline entries without promotion within
the epoch (the count is cumulative across republications within the
epoch and resets only at the epoch boundary);

(b) 

it accumulates $E_{\max}$ non-vindicated disputes within the
epoch (disputes ending in vindication are exempt; under the default
bars this trigger fires only through the optional neither-bar outcome
of Rule R13(iv); rationale: N15);

(c) 

it remains dormant (window closed, not resubmitted) for
 $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ ;

(d) 

it attracts no demand for $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ , including a disputed
candidate attracting no new demand for $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ .

Entries and dormant intervals alternate: each pipeline entry is
followed by at most one dormant interval, ending at the next entry or
at retirement; by the entry cap, entries per item per epoch number at
most $E_{\max}$ and dormant intervals begun within the epoch at most
 $E_{\max}$ , deterministically (one further dormant interval may be
carried across the boundary; the ledger’s conservative common bound
is $E_{\star}=E_{\max}+1$ , Lemma S6.7). Because the cap and the
count of clause (a) are per epoch, a persistently demanded benign
item may re-enter in later epochs; safety is unaffected, every
federated safety bound being per-attempt (Definition S5.1) with lifetime exposure counted
by Corollary S5.27. The owner’s local $\mathsf{C}$ copy is
untouched (Lemma S5.8); the owner may republish,
subject to Rule R12 and the entry cap. Retirement of a
disputed item frees all open rejection slots on it and is neither an
eviction nor a vindication: no accountability consequence for any
party.

(ii) 

Surfacing order. The $\leq c_{\mathrm{cand}}$ candidates a retrieval
surfaces are ordered by trust status ( $\mathsf{CC}$ , then undisputed candidates
by endorsement weight, then disputed items, flagged), computed on log
facts, never by a model.

(iii) 

Demotion hygiene. Items evicted by panel verdict are
removed from retrieval entirely for the cool-down period.

These policies only refuse entry openings, remove items, or reorder items in the shared tier; their
safety- and budget-preservation is Lemma S5.9.

## S4.3 Federated assumptions, adversary, and TCB additions

Assumption A7 (Federated endorsement transfer).

A Confirmed item endorsed at a specific tool by a qualifying
weighted set of distinct attested identities is authoritative for that
exact tool for any user (and likewise at the pseudo-target: a
qualifying quorum at $\mathsf{reasoning}$ is authoritative for
informing any user’s reasoning), provided the item is
context-independent, a semantic property judged by the publishing
owner and the inspecting community, not decided by the type system.
Tool identifiers are canonical across the federation (TCB), so “the
same tool” is well-defined; there is no transfer between tools.
(Scope: as at §S2.1, endorsement unit; the baseline data-tool path is governed by Rule R4(b) and Corollary S3.23(b).)
The
type-level half is discharged by Lemma S5.2 and is not
assumed. (All formal guarantees are integrity guarantees; semantic
confidentiality of published content rests on the publish gate and is
not separately proved: §S7.4.)

Assumption A8 (Exogenous federation log).

In the two-run comparison of Theorem S3.25 extended to the
federated setting (Proposition S5.10), the exogenous
log prefix delivered at each step’s log slot
(Definition S4.2) — every federation-log event not
generated by the compared local run itself: other parties’
endorsements, rejections, promotions, suspensions, panel verdicts,
and accountability events — is an exogenous input stream, coupled
identically across the compared runs, exactly as A6 couples
the user’s typed stream. The compared run’s own locally
generated federation events (Definition S4.2) are not
assumed coupled: their equality across the compared runs is
derived in Proposition S5.10(ii) from the matched
components, so the full delivered logs coincide without constraining
either attack. The federated Reasoning
Integrity statement therefore isolates the effect of unendorsed,
undisplayed untrusted bytes; attacker influence exercised through
legitimate log activity is governed not by that theorem but by
the safety bounds of Appendix S5
(Theorems S5.11, S5.13).

Assumption A9 (Verdict rate).

There is $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}\in(0,1]$ that lower-bounds, uniformly over disputed
items, times, and histories, the conditional probability, given all
prior protocol events, that the next arriving demander of a disputed
item casts an eligible verdict (Rule R13(iii)): weight
 $\geq w_{\mathrm{seat}}$ , fresh, no prior verdict on the dispute instance, within
the pool caps, and recording a decision rather than abandoning.
The bound is asserted jointly over behaviour and protocol
state: eligibility is consumed endogenously (freshness spent,
Definition S4.5; pool caps filled,
Rule R13(iii)(d); capacity exhausted, (iii)(e)), so
A9 entails in particular that the item’s remaining
fresh, seat-weighted, pool-diverse demand does not run out while the
dispute is open — a demand-composition property, not a
psychological one.
The bound is read per the conditioning convention of §S8.1:
conditional on all logged atoms prior to the arrival, and uniformly over
demand realisations.

Commentary
 (non-normative; epistemic status and failure mode of A9). A9 has two components with different characters. The
behavioural component (a demander who could cast an eligible
verdict does so at rate $\geq\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ ) has the same epistemic status as
 $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}},p_{b}$ . The structural component is stronger and can fail
deterministically, with no human variation at all: freshness
consumption and the pool caps are protocol mechanics, so an item
pre-exposed to most of a pool’s identities, or whose demand is
concentrated in few pools, can reach a state where no arriving
demander is eligible — the conditional probability is then $0$ , and
no behavioural assumption restores it. The consequences of failure
are confined by design: every result resting on A9 is
availability-side (Lemma S5.6, Lemma S5.24(ii),
the ledger’s bucket rows, and through them the budget
asymptotics); no safety theorem consumes it
(Figure S1), the unresolved dispute stalls frozen
(Rule R13(ii)), and each affected demander still pays at
most the floor (Theorem S6.2(i),
Lemma S5.24(ii)’s unconditional clause). Validation:
verdict-eligibility rates on disputed items, stratified by pool and
by the item’s prior exposure (N17). Quantitative
consequences: Lemma S5.6. The eligible-verdict class excludes
cache-gate decisions (Rule R13(iii)); $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ is read over
the class as so defined. $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ and $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ condition on
the same human act consistently: an abstaining demander is a miss
inside $1-q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ and a non-verdict inside $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ ; high
abstention degrades resolution speed, never the validity of the
safety parameter (Remark S5.19).

Adversary (federated).

The adversary controls, over the deployment’s lifetime, $f$ qualifying
identities of total weight $\leq W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ , of which at most $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ are ever
seat-eligible (static corruption, §S4.1). It does not
control honest users’ decisions, the labelling, the interpreter, or the
log. It may present a poisoned item to whom it likes, in what order it
likes, and may stop or continue as a function of past outcomes, but
never as a function of an inspection not yet performed: formally, its
strategy is a predictable process with respect to the protocol
filtration (§S8.1, convention (C2)), against which
Theorem S5.13 is stated. Two structural constraints on its
adaptivity (within-entry byte immutability, and the reading of $q$ as
a uniform bound over the honest identities the adversary can
target) are recorded in §S8.1 and Remark S5.19.

Definition S4.10 (Exogenous item classification).

Fix a classification $\mathsf{Bad}\subseteq\mathcal{V}\times(\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{sens}}\cup\mathsf{Tools}_{\mathrm{data}}\cup\{\mathsf{reasoning}\})$ ,
exogenous to the protocol: no rule reads it, no transition depends on it,
and no model computes it. An item $v$ is poisoned if
 $\mathsf{Bad}(v,t)$ holds for some tool $t$ , and benign otherwise;
“harmful at $t$ ” means $\mathsf{Bad}(v,t)$ ; harm at $\mathsf{reasoning}$ means content that misleads reasoning if adopted (A5). Every behavioural parameter
( $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}},p_{b},q_{c}$ ) and every safety and budget statement of
Appendices S5–S6 is read relative to this
classification; the classification is a modelling device for stating the
bounds, not a protocol object, and the protocol’s behaviour is identical
under any choice of it.

TCB additions (federated).

Beyond §S2.5: the attestation layer and its weight
assignment on cryptographic facts; the pool assignment of
Definition S4.3; canonical tool identifiers shared and
attested across the federation; and the log service of
convention (C3) (sequencing and authenticated history). The Sybil
conditions (finiteness of $W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ and the seat margin
 $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ ) require an attestation authority or identity cost
Douceur (2002); Dwork & Naor (2007). Because $f$ , $W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ , and $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ are
lifetime totals (§S4.1), they are enforceable only to the extent that the attestation or
identity-cost mechanism supplies certified lifetime bounds:
the issuance policy
must bound the identities and weight any party can ever acquire, and
that bound is what the constants denote. Where issuance is open or
uncosted, no finite $f$ exists and the federated safety results are
compatibility statements — valid for whatever lifetime totals the
deployment can certify — while the single-user layer and the cap
(Theorems S3.21, S3.25, S6.2) are
unaffected, consuming no Sybil bound.

## Appendix S5 Federated Guarantees

This appendix proves the guarantees of the protocol of
Appendix S4: the structural lemmas
(§S5.1), then the integrity lifting, the deterministic
safety theorem, panel safety, wrongful eviction, publisher safety,
denial-of-service, and submission-budget results
(§S5.2). Dependencies are as in Figure S1.

## S5.1 Structural lemmas

Definition S5.1 (Promotion attempt).

Fix a byte string $v$ . A promotion attempt is a maximal segment
of one shared-tier presence of $v$ beginning at an anchor and
ending at the first finalisation thereafter — the close of a
pipeline entry’s window at its full length, where promotion is decided
(Definition S4.6(c)) — or, where no finalisation
occurs, at the end of the presence (retirement, or demotion by panel
eviction) or at a loose-bar neither-bar resolution returning the item
to candidate status (Rule R13(iv)). Anchors are: the
presence-creating submission event (first attempt), and each
attempt-ending event just listed other than the end of the presence
(each later attempt). A window closed early at an epoch boundary is
not a finalisation (convention (C4)): it banks its accrued
endorsements to the item’s next entry, which belongs to the
same attempt.

Anchors are log events, and every decision credited to an attempt’s
entries — in-window, banked in a dormant or frozen interval,
boundary-banked, or vindication-credited
(Definition S4.6(b),(f), Rule R13(iv)) — is
logged strictly after the attempt’s anchor: in-window and interval
decisions follow the preceding anchor by construction, and
boundary-banked decisions were logged in an earlier entry of the same
attempt, itself opened after the anchor. Likewise no ordinary dispute and no
fast-track review is open at
any anchor: either state imposes the byte-level freeze
(Rule R13(ii),(vi)), which interrupts a window rather
than letting it finalise (Rule R13(iv)), and retirement
frees all open rejection slots (Rule R14(i)); so every
dispute or review bearing on an attempt opens, and casts all its
verdicts, after the attempt’s anchor.

Attempts partition each presence; every entry belongs to exactly one
attempt (the attempt whose anchor precedes its opening); at most one
entry of an attempt lies in any single epoch (a second entry of the
same attempt arises only across an epoch boundary, via a
boundary-closed window); and only an attempt’s final entry can
finalise. An attempt succeeds if its finalisation is a
promotion; an attempt with no finalisation — ending at retirement,
eviction, or a neither-bar resolution, or with no entry ever opened
— succeeds with probability zero. Attempts whose finalisation lies
in a given epoch number at most the epoch’s entries of the item, hence
at most $E_{\max}$ per item (Rule R14(i)), each such
finalising entry opened by a rate-limited (re-)submission of the same
epoch (Rule R12; windows are epoch-contained,
convention (C4)). Dually, the attempts anchored in a given
epoch are begun at that epoch’s presence-creating submissions and
finalisation events (under the default bars; under loose bars also
its neither-bar resolutions), which is the counting
Corollary S5.27 consumes.

Commentary
 (non-normative; role of the attempt unit). The attempt is the analysis unit at which the per-submission safety
bound is conditioned. Banked decisions credited to an entry precede
the entry’s opening, and the owner — or an adversary — may decide
whether to open the entry as a function of the banked outcomes;
a bound conditioned at the entry’s opening would therefore condition
on an adversarially selected event (an optional-selection error). The
attempt’s anchor precedes every decision creditable to its entries by
construction, so conditioning at the anchor is sound; and an attempt
whose intervals bank decisions is an attempt whether or not a further
entry opens, so the choice not to open is a choice among attempts
already counted, at success probability zero. No protocol rule reads
attempts; like the ledger’s charging convention
(Lemma S6.7), they are a hindsight bookkeeping device over
the same window/interval alternation the ledger’s buckets partition.

Lemma S5.2 (Type-level sharing exclusion).

No $\mathsf{T}$ -block, no $\mathsf{U}$ -block, no cache-marked block, and no block lacking an accepting publish
confirmation by its owner ever enters the shared candidate store.

Proof.

Label and provenance membership are deterministic and attacker-immutable
(A1–A2; provenance is interpreter-only, Definition S2.2); the publish gate of
Rule R12 is a confirmation event under
Rule R8 and A3; nomination has no authority.
∎

Lemma S5.3 (Entry disjointness).

For a fixed byte string, the weight-bearing endorsement sets of
distinct pipeline entries are disjoint in identities over the
deployment’s entire history, and every weight-bearing endorsement
(in-entry or banked-and-credited) is its endorser’s fresh, authority-active inspection of
the bytes (Definition S4.6(a)).

Proof.

By Definition S4.6(a), an identity’s weight-bearing
endorsement of the bytes is its first logged decision on them (Definition S4.5), taken while authority-active (Definition S4.6(a)), (when credited, Definition S4.6(f)), counted toward exactly one entry (the entry
open at decision time, or, via banking,
Definition S4.6(b), the next entry to open). Every later
acceptance by the same identity is repeat and carries no weight
(INV-1). Hence no identity contributes weight to two
entries, and each contribution is by construction a fresh inspection.
∎

Lemma S5.4 (Deterministic accountability bound).

Fix an identity and an epoch. The identity’s dispute-initiating
rejections filed within the epoch that both take protocol effect and
are eventually charged (contradicted by vindication), in this epoch
or any later one, number at most $c_{\mathrm{acc}}:=c_{\mathrm{ceil}}+r_{\max}$ .

Proof.

By Rule R13(v)(b), a rejection is charged at the moment its
vindication is logged. Suppose first that the epoch’s $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ -th
charge to this identity occurs, at some moment $T$ . From $T$ onward,
the identity’s further dispute-initiating rejections in the epoch are
filed as advisory flags with no protocol effect
(Rule R13(v)(d)), hence are never counted. Every counted
rejection is therefore either charged no later than $T$ (and the
epoch’s charges up to and including $T$ number exactly $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ , by
the choice of $T$ ) or still open at $T$ and charged afterward; open
rejections number at most $r_{\max}$ at any moment
(Rule R13(i)). The two classes are disjoint, a rejection
being charged exactly once; summing gives $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}+r_{\max}$ . If instead
fewer than $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ charges accrue within the epoch, at most
 $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}-1$ counted rejections are charged within it, and every one
charged in a later epoch is open at the epoch boundary, at most
 $r_{\max}$ again.
∎

Remark S5.5 (Calibration of rejecter accountability).

The rate trigger of Rule R13(v)(c) is normalised per
fresh inspection, never per rejection. The normalisation matters:
when poison is rare, almost all of an honest inspector’s rejections are
wrongful rejections of benign items and almost all are vindicated, so
charges per rejection approach one for honest and adversarial
identities alike and cannot separate them; charges per
inspection sit near $1-p_{b}$ for an honest identity and near its full
rejection rate for a campaign. The ceiling $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ is calibrated,
like the rate trigger, strictly above the honest baseline: an honest
identity accrues charges at rate at most $1-p_{b}$ per benign fresh
inspection, and its fresh inspections per epoch are bounded by its own
required-confirmation stream (small, and shrinking with population,
Appendix S6), so $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ set at a high quantile of
 $(1-p_{b})\times$ (honest per-epoch fresh-inspection count), with margin,
is reached by an honest identity only rarely; an honest identity that
does reach it retains advisory flags and ordinary
use — though its acceptances are authority-inert for the duration
(Definition S4.6(a), Remark S5.18) —
loses nothing permanent, is restored at the epoch boundary, and
its inspections during that state are excluded trials, not misses
(Remark S5.19), so no safety statement is affected. Renewal semantics: convention (C4) (the accountability allowances are the only freshness-like quantities that renew). (This calibration concerns the per-fresh-inspection rejection-rate trigger only; suspension-induced required confirmations are priced separately, once per demander, per Note N14.) Mis-calibration risk: S15.

Lemma S5.6 (Verdict pacing under A9).

Under A9, for any disputed item and any adversary
strategy (convention (C2)): the number of demanders who arrive while
the dispute is unresolved is stochastically dominated by the index
of the $(k{+}1)$ -st success in an i.i.d. $\mathrm{Bernoulli}(\rho_{\mathrm{seat}})$ sequence, i.e. by a sum of $k{+}1$ 
independent $\mathrm{Geometric}(\rho_{\mathrm{seat}})$ variables. In particular
its expectation is at most $\kappa:=(k{+}1)/\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ , and for any
 $c_{0}>1$ the probability that more than $c_{0}\,\kappa$ demanders
arrive while the dispute is unresolved is at most
 $e^{-\lambda_{c_{0}}k}$ for a constant $\lambda_{c_{0}}>0$ depending
only on $c_{0}$ and $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ .

Proof.

Index trials by arrivals: let $O_{i}$ be the event that an $i$ -th demander
of the item arrives while the dispute is unresolved, measurable at that
arrival’s rendering atom, and let $Z_{i}$ indicate that this arrival’s
disposition is an eligible verdict on the dispute instance. Under the
atomic reading of §S8.1, each arrival’s disposition is realized
at its own log position, so the $\sigma$ -algebra of atoms prior to the
 $i$ -th rendering atom contains the dispositions and protocol effects of
all earlier arrivals; A9 gives
 $\mathbb{P}[Z_{i}=1\mid\mathcal{G}_{i}]\geq\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ on $O_{i}$ at exactly these
 $\sigma$ -algebras, and Lemma S8.2(ii), in the form of
Remark S8.4, supplies i.i.d. $\mathrm{Bernoulli}(\rho_{\mathrm{seat}})$ variables $Y_{i}$ with $Y_{i}\leq Z_{i}$ on $O_{i}$ .
A dispute instance accepts at most $k$ eligible verdicts in its entire
life (Rule R13(iii)(e)), and every verdict is cast at an
arrival; hence on $O_{n}$ , $\sum_{i\leq n-1}Z_{i}\leq k$ , so
 $\sum_{i\leq n-1}Y_{i}\leq k$ , i.e. $n\leq G:=\min\{j:\sum_{i\leq j}Y_{i}=k+1\}$ .
The arrival count is therefore pathwise at most $G$ , a sum of $k{+}1$ 
independent $\mathrm{Geometric}(\rho_{\mathrm{seat}})$ variables. Linearity gives the
expectation; the tail is the Chernoff bound for sums of independent
geometrics. (Because dispositions resolve at arrival, the sharper count
 $\sum_{i\leq n-1}Z_{i}\leq k-1$ in fact holds while the dispute is unresolved,
improving $\kappa$ to $k/\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ ; we retain the conservative
 $\kappa=(k{+}1)/\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ , which every downstream constant consumes, the
slack being monotone.)
∎

Lemma S5.7 (Decisive resolution under the default bars).

Under the default bars $m_{\mathrm{rej}}=k+1-m$ with $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ and
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ :
(i) eviction and vindication are mutually exclusive;
(ii) every panel that gathers $k$ verdicts reaches exactly one bar,
and the first bar reached in log order is the bar reached by the full
tally of $k$ verdicts: early resolution changes timing, never the
outcome;
(iii) both bars are reachable by honest verdicts alone, and the
adversary’s at most $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ verdicts (INV-5) alone reach
neither; hence no tally deadlock exists once $k$ verdicts are
gathered, and neither bar can be reached by the adversary’s verdicts
alone; whether $k$ eligible verdicts arrive is the separate,
availability-side matter of A9 (Remark S5.21).

Proof.

(i)–(ii) $m+m_{\mathrm{rej}}=k+1$ , so $k$ verdicts cannot meet both
bars and must meet at least one; verdict counts are non-decreasing
along the verdict sequence, so a bar is reached at some prefix iff it
is reached by the full tally. (iii) From $k=m+m_{\mathrm{rej}}-1$ with
 $m,m_{\mathrm{rej}}>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ : $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}\geq m$ and $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}\geq m_{\mathrm{rej}}$ ,
so honest verdicts alone suffice for either bar; and $f_{\mathrm{s}}<m$ means the
adversary’s at most $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ verdicts alone cannot reach the $m$ -bar, and
symmetrically $f_{\mathrm{s}}<m_{\mathrm{rej}}$ for the other bar.
∎

Lemma S5.8 (Third-party immutability of local stores).

For every user $u$ and every block $b$ in $u$ ’s local store (the
user-owned tier: $\mathsf{T}$ -, $\mathsf{C}$ -, $\mathsf{PC}$ -, and $\mathsf{U}$ -blocks; community status lives
in the shared tier and is referenced, never stored, locally,
§S4.1), no protocol event initiated by any identity
other than $u$ alters $\mathrm{bytes}(b)$ , $\ell(b)$ , or $\mathsf{scope}(b)$ .
Dispute, suspension, vindication, demotion, eviction, expiry, and
every accountability penalty of Rule R13(v),(vii) act on the
shared tier alone: they invalidate or flag shared entries, restrict
shared-tier rights, or reduce attestation weight. Demotion of a $\mathsf{CC}$ 
item withdraws community authority from users who have not personally
confirmed it, whose gates simply fire again, within the single-user cap of
Theorem S6.2, and leaves every personally confirmed $\mathsf{C}$ 
copy intact (with a notification). Suspension likewise withdraws community authority for the review’s duration (Rule R13(vi)), equally without touching any local block. Consequently no third-party protocol transition ever directly alters or
removes any block of any user’s local store; first-party removal by the
owner’s own agent ( $\mathsf{evict}$ , including evictions the agent
performs in reaction to notifications or other legitimate log events) is
the disclosed residual N13. The induced cost bound, that no
campaign pushes any user above the single-user cap, is
Theorem S6.2, which rests on the present lemma.

Proof.

By Figures S2–S3, local stores mutate only via owner-invoked
 $\Sigma$ -operations and owner confirmations
(A1–A2); $\mathsf{evict}$ is owner-only
(first-party eviction by the owner’s own agent is a disclosed
residual: N13, §S7.4). Community status is
shared-tier state (§S4.1), so demotion and suspension
update the shared entry and never touch a local block; promotion, demotion,
and expiry are interpreter relabels or retirements of the shared log
entry (Rules R13, R14); the accountability
penalties of clauses (v) and (vii) act on rejection rights, submission
rights, and attestation weight, none of which is a local-store
attribute; the cascade quarantines shared derivations, not local
stores; notifications are advisory.
∎

Lemma S5.9 (Hygiene preserves safety through bound uniformity).

The policies of Rule R14 preserve every theorem of this
document. The entry cap only refuses entry openings, and retirement and demotion hygiene only remove shared-tier
items; none of these can enable a promotion. Surfacing reordering is
not monotone by event-set inclusion — reordering, and removal of one
candidate, can increase another item’s reach within the
 $c_{\mathrm{cand}}$ -capped surface — but the safety bounds are unaffected
because Theorems S5.11 and S5.13 are uniform over
adaptive presentation schedules and reach-free (INV-6): any
demand redistribution hygiene induces is one more presentation
schedule inside the quantified class. Lemma S5.8 is
unaffected because no local store is touched; the ledger of
Appendix S6 is unaffected at the moment of any hygiene
action, which adds no confirmation event. Retirement does redirect
later demand: a user who encounters the retired bytes
afterwards through their own channels confirms them as ordinary
personal content, in the single-user stream
(Definition S4.4), bounded by the cap
(Theorem S6.2), its benign,
A11-conforming component vanishing in expectation under
the vanishing conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii) and the
remainder entering $r^{(e)}_{\mathrm{bad}}$ 
(Corollary S6.10); the shared-tier ledger rows
are never increased by removal or reordering.

Proof.

Each policy is a deterministic interpreter action on the shared tier:
the entry cap refuses an entry opening, retirement removes a candidate,
surfacing order permutes at most
 $c_{\mathrm{cand}}$ surfaced candidates, demotion hygiene removes an evicted item
from retrieval. None relabels any local block, forces any
confirmation, or adds any promotion path. For the safety theorems, the
relevant quantification is over adaptive presentation and demand
schedules (convention (C2)); the schedules realizable under any
hygiene policy are a subset of that class, and the bounds of
Theorems S5.11 and S5.13 are uniform over it and
independent of reach, so they apply verbatim to the hygiene-modified
deployment. The post-retirement reclassification is
Definition S4.4’s retirement clause.
∎

## S5.2 Main theorems

Proposition S5.10 (Delegated Action Integrity and federated Reasoning
Integrity).

Assume A7 and A8, and read the interpreter
over federated product configurations
(Definition S4.1) under the federated step of Definition S4.2, with
Definition S3.3(iii) in its federated form (every virtual
 $\mathsf{CC}$ authority in force backed by a currently valid promotion
certificate on $L$ ). Then:

(i) 

a value flows on community authority without personal
confirmation only if it byte-equals a live virtual entry naming the
exact tool (Rule R4(a), live-entry test), each such
entry backed at that tool by qualifying fresh endorsements
(Definition S4.6, Lemma S3.14), each
satisfying A3–A4 or A5 for its
endorser and authoritative by A7: PerMIT has
delegated Action Integrity (Definition S3.10);

(ii) 

under A8 the coupled log slots deliver equal
prefixes, and locally emitted federation events are equal across
matched runs (Definition S4.2), hence equal virtual
views $\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ at every step; the matched relation of
Definition S3.6, read over $\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{fed}}_{\mathbb{E}}$ 
and extended by equality of delivered log prefixes, is preserved by
log-slot transitions — which emit no reply and alter no matched
component except the coupled virtual view — and by every local
transition exactly as in Theorem S3.25; hence
Theorem S3.25 and Corollary S3.23 lift to the
product system with “ $\mathsf{C}$ ” read as “ $\mathsf{C}$ or live $\mathsf{CC}$ ”;

(iii) 

suspension, demotion, retirement, and every accountability event
act on $L$ alone: they withdraw virtual authority and never create,
alter, or remove any local block (Lemma S5.8).

Proof.

(i) The single-user results are personal
(Lemma S3.16); community authority enters only through the
live-entry test, whose backing endorsements are fresh accepting
confirmations at the named tool (Definition S4.6(c),
Lemma S3.14), authoritative by A7; the scope
test is the same singleton-membership test as for $\mathsf{C}$ , and handle
gating is label-independent (Rule R4(c)). (ii) Log-slot transitions: both runs append the same coupled prefix
(A8), producing equal $S_{\mathrm{sh}}(L)$ by
deterministic replay (Definition S4.1), appending
nothing to either reply stream and touching no local store
(Definition S4.2, Lemma S5.8). Locally
emitted federation events extend the two logs equally, by
cases. (1) $\mathsf{submit}(v)$ : the publish decision is a
transcript event (matched) on bytes of a local $\mathsf{C}$ -block of the
owner ( $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ matched). (2) Protocol-bearing gate decisions:
displayed bytes, target, and decision are transcript-pinned;
shared-presence status, coverage, cache classification, freshness,
and authority are deterministic predicates of the matched local
store and the equal delivered prefix
(Definitions S4.5, S4.6,
Rule R13(i),(v)). (3) Realized demand events: fired by
the matched operation against the matched federated view, the
acting identity common to both runs, and epoch-first-demand status
a deterministic predicate of the equal delivered prefixes
(Definition S4.4). By induction on steps: equal
delivered prefixes give equal
 $\mathsf{LiveCC}(L)$ ; together with the matched-local-state
induction hypothesis and the single-user unwinding
(Theorem S3.25), this gives matched local transitions,
and the case analysis then gives equal appended suffixes, hence
equal delivered prefixes at the next step. Local transitions: the unwinding of
Theorem S3.25 runs verbatim with $\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{fed}}_{\mathbb{E}}$ 
for $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ — the $\mathsf{CC}$ branch of the gate is a function of the
argument list and the step’s delivered coupled log prefix
(Definition S4.2), matched across the runs, so the
gate-or-no-gate decision of Step 5 remains matched. (iii) is
Definition S4.1 with Lemma S5.8.
∎

Theorem S5.11 (Deterministic safety under perfect honest detection).

Under A1–A5 and
Rules R12–R13, with perfect honest detection
( $q=1$ : every honest inspection of the poisoned item rejects): if
 $W>W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ and $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ , then no poisoned item (Definition S4.10) is ever promoted to $\mathsf{CC}$ 
at any tool, at any population size and under any attack, through
either the ordinary or the panel path.

Proof.

With $q=1$ , every accepting endorsement and every accepting panel
verdict on a poisoned item is adversarial. The ordinary path requires
accepting weight at least $W$ at the tool in question, while the
adversary’s weight is at most $W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ and $W>W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ : impossible. The
panel path requires at least $m$ accepting verdicts, while the
adversary casts at most $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ verdicts
(INV-5), $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ , and honest accepting verdicts are
zero: impossible. Vindication-credited and dormancy-banked endorsements
are covered identically: with $q=1$ every honest decision on the poison
rejects, so every credited acceptance is adversarial and already
counted inside $W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ (respectively inside the $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ verdict cap).
Deterministic and population-free.
∎

Remark S5.12 (Scope of Theorem S5.11).

The hypothesis $q=1$ is a behavioural idealization, the strongest in
this document, not the absence of one: the Sybil conditions
 $W>W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ , $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ exclude adversarial weight, while honest
acceptances of the poison are excluded only by $q=1$ itself. The
theorem is therefore not a fallback for the
unrecognizable-poison regime $q\to 0$ , where honest inspectors
themselves supply the threshold and no community-truthfulness
guarantee is available within a human-judgment design. What survives
that regime is the local layer — personal Action Integrity, the
single-user cap, and no-deletion (Theorem S3.21,
Theorem S6.2, Lemma S5.8) — together with
the one-sided detection clause (a single honest effective
qualifying rejection, whenever
one occurs, freezes promotion immediately, Rule R13(i),(v)) and
re-verification against never-decided inspectors (N16).
See S14.

Theorem S5.13 (Panel safety under adaptive presentation).

Adopt Rules R12–R13 with the default bars of
Rule R13(iv) ( $m_{\mathrm{rej}}=k{+}1-m$ , $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ ,
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ ; Lemma S5.7) and the probabilistic
framework of §S8.1. Suppose:

(a) 

ordinary promotion at a tool requires $N$ distinct accepting
endorsements counted toward one pipeline entry
(Definition S4.6), with $N>f$ ;

(b) 

at most $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ of the adversary’s identities are seat-eligible,
with $m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ ;

(c) 

there are $q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}}\in(0,1]$ such that: each honest
fresh, authority-active (Definition S4.6(a))
inspection of the poisoned item at a non-covered, non-cache
confirmation gate
ends in an effective qualifying rejection — a recorded
rejecting decision that takes protocol effect under
Rule R13(i) — with conditional probability at least
 $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ ; and each honest eligible verdict on the item rejects
