with conditional probability at least $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ , conditionally on
the verdict being cast, i.e. at the verdict’s header-inclusive
 $\sigma$ -algebra (§S8.1) (Rule R13(iii)). Both
bounds are read per the conditioning convention of §S8.1
(all prior logged atoms; the demand realisation), and both are
uniform lower bounds over the honest identities and
presentation contexts the adversary can target — the catch rate of
the most fallible targetable honest cohort, not a population average
(Remark S5.19) — $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ ’s contexts are the unfrozen candidate contexts —
in-window and candidate-dormancy inspections, any advisory-flag state
— where an effective qualifying rejection is possible
(Rule R13(i),(ii)); freeze- and suspension-time fresh
inspections carry no initiation and belong to $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ ’s province
where verdict-eligible, their non-verdict rejections being
corroborations, consumed by no trial of this theorem. $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ ’s
contexts include the freeze itself: verdicts are cast while the item
is frozen. Both bounds are read over the target contexts (tools and
 $\mathsf{reasoning}$ ) in which the trials actually occur: $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ is a
uniform bound over the tool contexts of eligible jurors, which
under byte-level panels may include tools at which the item is
innocuous — for tool-targeted poison the applicable $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ may
therefore be small (S4). Authority-inert inspections,
covered gates, and cache gates (Options O1–O2)
belong to no trial class of this theorem: an
authority-inert acceptance carries no promotion weight and an
authority-inert rejection no initiation
(Definition S4.6(a)), covered gates are
protocol-inert on both branches (Rule R13(i),
Remark S5.19), and cache gates are
accept-inert and verdict-ineligible
(Definition S4.6(iii), Rule R13(iii)) —
their rejecting branch is one more effective qualifying rejection,
which can only deny the case-(a) path — so promotion still requires
 $N-f$ honest authority-active fresh non-cache credits, and $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ is quantified over exactly
the class whose members can both accept with weight and reject with
effect. Abstention by an authority-active fresh inspector records
nothing and is counted as a miss inside $1-q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ ; the two parameters
deliberately live at different conditioning levels — $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ at the
disposition of a fresh inspection, where abstention must count as a
miss; $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ at a cast verdict, where abstention is already excluded
by eligibility — and $q_{\mathrm{pre}}\leq q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ is typical but not assumed;

(d) 

the panel margin is positive:
 $\delta:=\frac{m-f_{\mathrm{s}}}{k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}-(1-q_{\mathrm{pan}})>0$ .

Then for any poisoned (Definition S4.10) submission, every
promotion attempt (Definition S5.1) succeeds with
probability at most the display below, conditionally on the $\sigma$ -algebra generated by all atoms up to and including the attempt’s anchor atom, uniformly over adaptive
presentation, stopping, and re-entry strategies and regardless of how
many users the item is shown to (INV-6),

 $\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[\text{promotion}]$ 
 $\displaystyle\leq(1-q_{\mathrm{pre}})^{N-f}+\mathbb{P}\!\big[\mathrm{Bin}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}},1-q_{\mathrm{pan}})\geq m-f_{\mathrm{s}}\big]$ 

 $\displaystyle\leq(1-q_{\mathrm{pre}})^{N-f}+e^{-2\delta^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}.$ 

Remark S5.14 (Scope of Theorem S5.13).

Theorem S5.11 is the special case $q=1$ . The bound covers a
single promotion attempt (Definition S5.1); for attempts anchored while the bytes hold live $\mathsf{CC}$ status at some tool (additional-tool promotion of already-promoted bytes, possible only after a prior success), hypothesis (c) is read with $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ ’s contexts extended per the closing paragraph of the proof; first-promotion attempts need no extension; entries embed injectively into attempts, so per-entry counting statements read a fortiori; attempts are counted at their anchors, and the per-anchor-epoch union
bound taken, in Corollary S5.27. No hypothesis is
placed on repeat inspections: none carries promotion weight or a
verdict (INV-1, INV-5;
Remark S4.7). The panel arithmetic rests on
INV-5 alone, which is stated independently of the
freshness rule precisely so that it never depends on
INV-1.
The default bars are a hypothesis of the theorem: case (b) uses decisive
resolution (Lemma S5.7) to cap honest verdicts at $k-a$ .
Under the looser bars permitted by Rule R13(iv)
( $m+m_{\mathrm{rej}}\geq k+2$ ), the same argument bounds the vindication
event verbatim — vindication still requires $m$ accepting verdicts among
at most $k$ , so the case-(b) term stands with $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ trials — and the
additional neither-bar outcome returns the item to unpromoted candidate
status, whose re-entry opens a new attempt with an identity-disjoint
credited set, counted separately and marginally by
Corollary S5.27. The bound therefore extends to loose-bar
deployments per entry; only the decisiveness clauses (mutual exclusivity,
tally-deadlock-freeness) are specific to the defaults.

Remark S5.15 (Single-parameter reading).

Every appearance of a single $q$ elsewhere in this document
(Theorem S5.11’s hypothesis $q=1$ ; the corridor row
 $q+p_{b}>1$ ; Corollary S5.27; the composed summary of
§S7.5; the correlated rate $q_{c}$ of S13) is
read as $q:=\min(q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}})$ : the bounds of
Theorem S5.13 are monotone in each parameter, so the
single- $q$ forms are the special case $q_{\mathrm{pre}}=q_{\mathrm{pan}}=q$ and remain
valid verbatim. $q=1$ means both acts are perfect;
 $q\to 0$ (S14) is the regime where the smaller of the two
vanishes.

Remark S5.16 (Itemwise reading; heterogeneous item difficulty).

Theorem S5.13 is stated and proved per promotion attempt (Definition S5.1): nothing
in its trials couples distinct items, so its hypotheses and bound are
itemwise — for each poisoned item $v$ , the bound holds with
 $v$ ’s own weakest-target rates $q_{\mathrm{pre}}(v),q_{\mathrm{pan}}(v)$ , and it is
monotone in each. Heterogeneous item difficulty is therefore inside
the model, transparently: easily recognized poison contributes a
negligible term; near-unrecognizable poison ( $q_{\mathrm{pre}}(v)\to 0$ ) degrades
its own term toward the disclosed residual S14, leaving
every other item’s bound untouched. The per-anchor-epoch union bound of
Corollary S5.27 holds in the same itemwise form,
 $\sum_{e}\big[(1-q_{\mathrm{pre}}(v_{e}))^{N-f}+e^{-2\delta(v_{e})^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}\big]$ 
(terms with $\delta(v_{e})\leq 0$ read as the exact binomial tail), the
single- $q$ display being the uniform special case; a deployment-level
constant is thus dominated by the hardest items the adversary can
mint — exactly the calibration duty of S1. No
parametric ability-or-difficulty model (jury, psychometric, or
crowdsourcing) is assumed: the weakest-target conditional floor
subsumes any such structure, at the price that the floor, not the
average, is the deployed constant (Remark S5.19, N17).

Remark S5.17 (The certified per-attempt ceiling $B_{\mathrm{att}}$ ).

Write

 $B_{\mathrm{att}}\;:=\;(1-q_{\mathrm{pre}})^{N-f}\;+\;\mathbb{P}\!\big[\mathrm{Bin}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}},\,1-q_{\mathrm{pan}})\geq m-f_{\mathrm{s}}\big]$ 

for the right-hand side of Theorem S5.13’s bound in its exact
binomial form, and call its two summands the ordinary term and the
panel term. The word certified names this analytic bound and
is unrelated to the promotion certificates of
Definition S4.1, which are log objects. Three properties are
used throughout the empirical evaluation: the first two are immediate from
the display, the third from the theorem’s statement.

The panel term is a floor: at fixed panel geometry $(k,m,f_{\mathrm{s}})$ and
fixed $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ it does not contain $N$ , so raising the endorsement quorum
shrinks only the ordinary term and $B_{\mathrm{att}}$ can never fall below
it — panel geometry, not quorum, is the binding deployment parameter,
which is the frontier Proposition S5.20 reads against and which
the corridor of §S6.5 moves only by scheduling $k$ itself.
The two terms fall in different protocol constants and must be
pushed down independently: the ordinary term geometrically in $N-f$ , the
panel term as a binomial tail in $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ whose Hoeffding relaxation
 $e^{-2\delta^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}$ is exponential in that constant. And
 $B_{\mathrm{att}}$ is per promotion attempt (Definition S5.1)
and reach-free (INV-6); deployment-wide exposure is obtained by
summing over anchored attempts and byte-distinct variants, through the
union, expectation and deterministic-cap forms of
Corollary S5.27 and the lifetime ceiling of
Remark S5.29, never by reading the per-attempt figure as a
per-epoch one.

Reporting discipline. Any numerical value quoted for
 $B_{\mathrm{att}}$ is the exact binomial tail above, never the Hoeffding
relaxation that Theorem S5.13 displays alongside it. The two
differ materially, and by different factors on the two sides of the
design: at $k=31$ , $m=20$ , $f_{\mathrm{s}}=1$ with $q_{\mathrm{pan}}=0.6$ the relaxation is
 $3.81\times 10^{-2}$ against an exact panel term of $8.30\times 10^{-3}$ , a
factor of about $4.6$ ; on the benign side of
Proposition S5.20, at $p_{b}=0.9$ and the same $\delta$ , the
numerically identical relaxation stands against an exact
wrongful-eviction tail of $8.91\times 10^{-5}$ , a factor of about $430$ . A
reader recomputing either relaxation from a quoted exact value will
therefore not reproduce it, and — because the two relaxations coincide
numerically while their exact counterparts differ by two orders of
magnitude — the two slacks must never be interchanged.

Proof of Theorem S5.13.

Fix the attempt and condition on the $\sigma$ -algebra through and
including its anchor atom (Definition S5.1). The
behavioural bounds of hypothesis (c) are asserted uniformly over
histories, so the argument restarts after any fixed anchor-inclusive
history; every trial charged below occurs strictly after the anchor,
and each trial’s own conditioning $\sigma$ -algebra
(§S8.1) refines the anchor-inclusive one. Partition by whether an effective
qualifying rejection was logged after the anchor and before the
attempt’s finalisation.

Throughout, the bytes’ promotion status at every tool is constant
within the attempt: promotion occurs only at a finalisation, which
ends the attempt, and demotion by eviction ends the presence
(Definition S5.1, Definition S4.6(c)),
so whether the bytes hold live $\mathsf{CC}$ status at any tool is decided
at the anchor. Suppose first that they do not — the case of every
attempt preceding the bytes’ first promotion, hence of every
attempt the ever-promoted events of Corollary S5.27
require; the complementary case is scoped at the close of the
proof. Then no suspension can arise within the attempt (suspension
presupposes promoted bytes, Rule R13(vi)), every
qualifying rejection on the bytes within it is dispute-initiating
(Rule R13(i),(ii)), and every non-freeze piece of the
attempt is an entry window or a candidate-dormancy interval
(presence taxonomy, Lemma S6.7): every unfrozen header
within the attempt is an unfrozen candidate context in the
sense of hypothesis (c).

(a) Promotion with no qualifying rejection. By
Rule R13(i),(v), an honest authority-active
identity’s rejecting gate decision on an unfrozen candidate takes
protocol effect, so the case-(a) event entails that no honest
authority-active inspection of the item ended in an effective
qualifying rejection before the attempt’s finalisation. Promotion at
the finalisation requires $N$ credited accepting endorsements at the
finalising entry (in-window, interval-banked, boundary-banked, or
vindication-credited, Definition S4.6), of which at
least $N-f$ come from honest identities; each is a distinct honest
identity’s fresh, authority-active, non-covered decision on the bytes
(Lemma S5.3, Definition S4.6(a)), and each
is logged strictly after the attempt’s anchor and before its
finalisation (Definition S5.1).

Index the trials by log order within the attempt: let $O_{i}$ be the
event that an $i$ -th protocol-bearing decision at a non-covered,
non-cache gate on the item, by an honest, fresh, authority-active
identity, is logged after the attempt’s anchor and before the
attempt ends, at an unfrozen header — by the opening reduction,
an unfrozen candidate context in the sense of hypothesis (c). $O_{i}$ 
is measurable at that record’s header atom (§S8.1):
attempt membership is decided by the anchor, a past log event, and
freshness, coverage, authority, and frozen status are deterministic
predicates of the prior log
(Definitions S4.5, S4.6(a),
Rule R13(i),(ii)) — no trial’s occurrence depends on
the attempt’s future. Because no event interleaves between a
position’s rendering and header atoms (§S8.1), the
status at the header is the status under which hypothesis (c)’s
disposition-level bound is read. Let $Z_{i}$ indicate that the
trial’s content is an effective qualifying rejection.
Hypothesis (c) lower-bounds by $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ the disposition-level
probability of such a rejection at the trial’s rendering atom, on
all of $O_{i}$ and irrespective of whether case (a) occurs — the
class is confined to exactly the unfrozen candidate contexts over
which $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ is quantified; conditioning on a recorded decision
only raises the bound (Lemma S8.1(iii)). Hence
 $\mathbb{P}[Z_{i}=1\mid\mathcal{G}_{i}]\geq q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ on $O_{i}$ at the
header-inclusive $\sigma$ -algebras of the conditioning convention
(§S8.1), each of which refines the anchor
 $\sigma$ -algebra, so Remark S8.4 applies under the
anchor-conditional measure directly.

Only now is the case-(a) event used. On it, no effective qualifying
rejection is logged after the anchor and before the attempt’s
finalisation; since no dispute or review is open at the anchor
(Definition S5.1) and every freeze arising within the
attempt presupposes such a rejection, every trial of the class
preceding the finalisation occurs at an unfrozen header, and the
 $N-f$ honest credited endorsements — in-window, interval-banked
in a candidate-dormancy interval, or boundary-banked, by the
opening reduction; a vindication-credited endorsement presupposes a
panel, hence a case-(b) run — are all logged at trials of the
class, each necessarily non-cache (cache acceptances are never
logged, hence never credited, Definition S4.6(iii)).
The case-(a) success event is therefore contained in
 $O_{N-f}\cap\{Z_{1}=\cdots=Z_{N-f}=0\}$ : at least $N-f$ trials of the
class are logged before the finalisation, so the first $N-f$ trials
in log order all precede it, and each has $Z_{i}=0$ , an effective
qualifying rejection before finalisation being a case-(b) run. The
joint all-zeros bound of Lemma S8.2(ii), in the
 $\sigma$ -algebra form of Remark S8.4, gives
 $\mathbb{P}[\text{case (a)}\mid\text{anchor}]\leq(1-q_{\mathrm{pre}})^{N-f}$ .
Additional reach creates both rejection opportunities and possible
weight-bearing acceptances (INV-2); no pathwise
monotonicity in reach is claimed. What the bound uses is only the
 $N-f$ honest fresh authority-active trials promotion itself requires,
so the stated bound is unchanged at every reach (INV-6).

(b) Promotion after a qualifying rejection. The byte-level
freeze then blocks every tool’s pipeline until an accepting panel resolution
(Rule R13(ii)–(iv),(vi)), so the case-(b) success
event is contained in the accepting outcome
(vindication, or reinstatement under Rule R13(vi)) of the
first panel instance opened
within the attempt — an ordinary
dispute panel or a fast-track suspension review, with the same
 $k,m,m_{\mathrm{rej}}$ parameters and bars; that panel instance opens, and casts all its verdicts,
after the attempt’s anchor (Definition S5.1), so the
argument below runs under the anchor-conditional measure verbatim: the
accepting-verdict count must reach $m$ . Suppose the adversary casts
 $a\leq f_{\mathrm{s}}$ accepting verdicts (at most one per identity for the entire
life of the panel instance (INV-5, read over the instance; Rule R13(iii),(vi)), across any number of epoch boundaries,
by INV-5; seat floor and pool caps per
Rule R13(iii); abandonment casts none). An accepting resolution then
requires $m-a$ honest accepting verdicts; with the default bars, at most $k$ verdicts are cast before a bar is
reached (Lemma S5.7), so at most $k-a$ are honest. Each
honest verdict is a recorded decision at a fresh inspection.
By the atomic decomposition of §S8.1, the event that an
 $i$ -th honest verdict is cast is measurable at that verdict’s
header atom, strictly before its content atom, and honest verdicts
are log-ordered with every earlier verdict’s content preceding
every later verdict’s header. By hypothesis (c), each honest verdict rejects with conditional
probability at least $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ at its header-inclusive $\sigma$ -algebra (§S8.1); equivalently, its conditional accept probability there is at most
 $1-q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ (a cast verdict’s content is binary). Apply
Lemma S8.2(i), in the $\sigma$ -algebra form of
Remark S8.4 at those $\sigma$ -algebras, to the accept
indicators $Z_{i}$ of the honest verdicts: there are i.i.d. $\mathrm{Bernoulli}(1-q_{\mathrm{pan}})$ majorants
 $Y_{1},\dots,Y_{k}$ with $Z_{i}\leq Y_{i}$ pointwise, unrealised verdicts
contributing zero, so the early decisive truncation of
Rule R13(iv) removes only nonnegative terms
(Lemma S8.2(i)). On the case-(b) event,
 $m-a\leq\sum_{i\leq k-a}Z_{i}\leq\sum_{i\leq k-a}Y_{i}\leq\sum_{i\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}Y_{i}+(f_{\mathrm{s}}-a)$ , whence
 $\sum_{i\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}Y_{i}\geq m-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ : the case $a=f_{\mathrm{s}}$ dominates,
uniformly in $a$ , and $\sum_{i\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}Y_{i}\sim\mathrm{Bin}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}},1-q_{\mathrm{pan}})$ .
Hoeffding’s inequality (Lemma S8.8) at the margin $\delta$ 
gives the second term. The events are disjoint;
sum.

Reach-freeness. The bound of case (a) is independent of reach: it counts only the
 $N-f$ honest fresh decisions promotion itself requires, however many
further inspections occur; in case (b), $k$ and $m$ are protocol constants,
viewers whose decisions are not verdicts do not vote, and logged-repeat
viewers cannot vote at all (INV-1). An expired dispute
retires the item unpromoted (Rule R14); republication is a
fresh submission whose endorsement set is identity-disjoint from all
prior sets (Lemma S5.3), counted separately and
marginally (Corollary S5.27). The case-(b) bound holds
regardless of who filed the rejection, so self-dispute gains nothing
(S2). Finally, the adversary’s choice whether to open an entry after
favourable banked outcomes is a choice among attempts already counted:
an attempt whose intervals bank decisions is an attempt whether or
not the entry opens, and an unopened attempt succeeds with
probability zero (Definition S5.1).

Attempts anchored with live $\mathsf{CC}$ status. For an attempt at
whose anchor the bytes hold live $\mathsf{CC}$ status at some tool —
possible only after a prior successful attempt of the same bytes,
itself bounded marginally at its own anchor — the inter-window
spans are stable promoted intervals, first decisions banked there
are creditable (Definition S4.6(b),
Lemma S6.7), and a fresh inspector’s rejection there is
a post-promotion rejection routed to Rule R13(vi) rather
than a dispute initiation. The two-case argument applies to such
attempts under a correspondingly extended reading of
hypothesis (c): $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ quantified over the attempt’s unfrozen
contexts including its stable promoted intervals, with the
effective qualifying rejection there being the review-opening
post-promotion rejection of Rule R13(vi) within its
per-epoch review cap — an extension a deployment must calibrate
separately, disclosed here (cf. S4). The event
that any byte string is ever promoted at all is a union of
first-promotion events, whose attempts are anchored $\mathsf{CC}$ -free and
need no extension.
∎

Remark S5.18 (Authority-gated crediting: soundness and cost).

The authority gate of Definition S4.6(a) restores the
quantifier compatibility that deterministic silencing would otherwise
break: without it, an adversary steering presentations toward
identities whose rejection rights are suspended, ceiling-capped, or
slot-exhausted would face inspectors whose effective rejection
probability is zero while their acceptances still carried full
promotion weight, and no $q_{\mathrm{pre}}>0$ could satisfy hypothesis (c)
uniformly over targetable cohorts. With the gate, the two capacities
are coupled at one deterministic log predicate: a silenced identity
can neither reject with effect nor accept with weight, so targeting
it gains the adversary nothing. The split is exact: verdict
capacity is never authority-gated
(Rule R13(v)(a),(e)) — an authority-inactive juror’s
accepting verdict counts toward the $m$ -bar like any other, keeping
the panel arithmetic of Theorem S5.13 and
Proposition S5.20 calibration-independent — while
promotion crediting always is
(Definition S4.6(a)): the same verdict contributes
weight only if its caster held authority at decision time, in-entry,
banked, and vindication-credited cases alike. Honest cost is negligible: an honest
identity is authority-inactive only in the rare calibrated states of
Remark S5.5, and its acceptance in that state still buys its
personal $\mathsf{C}$ copy — INV-3 is unaffected, the cap
counting required confirmations, never credits. Safety is monotone:
authority-gating removes acceptances from the promotion count and
removes nothing from the rejection side (rejections by inactive
identities remain advisory flags or corroboration exactly as before,
and verdict-eligible rejections are cast regardless of accountability
state, Rule R13(v)(e)); Theorem S5.11 is unaffected
(its counting is adversarial-weight only), $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ needs no gating
(verdicts are never silenced, Rule R13(v)(a),(e)), and
verdict eligibility, hence A9, is untouched. One
availability-side consequence is disclosed: an adversary who
engineers charges against honest identities (S15) now also
delays their crediting capacity, slowing benign promotion —
an exposure of the same character, availability-only, bounded by the
same calibration margins (Remark S5.5) and priced inside
 $\varepsilon(M)$ where it matters (A11, heuristic
sufficiency).

Remark S5.19 (Interpretation of the parameter $q$ ).

 $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ is the probability that an honest inspector, presented the
item at their own confirmation gate, detects the poison and
records a rejecting decision that takes protocol effect (quantified over unfrozen candidate contexts, Theorem S5.13(c));
 $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ is the probability that an honest eligible juror’s
cast verdict rejects it. The split matters for calibration: the two
acts have different filters (seat weight, pool caps, and verdict
capacity apply only to jurors) and different honest baselines, so
their log validation is stratified separately (N17);
a deployment estimating one number reads it as
 $q:=\min(q_{\mathrm{pre}},q_{\mathrm{pan}})$ , under which every bound of
Theorem S5.13 holds with $q$ in both roles
(Remark S5.15). Both parameters are uniform, weakest-target,
conditional lower bounds (§S8.1: conditional on all prior logged atoms and the demand
factor): because the adversary chooses whom to present the item to,
each is the catch rate of the most fallible honest cohort the
adversary can reach, never a population average, and aggregate log
rates do not directly estimate them (N17). Because a gate rejection is automatically
qualifying (Rule R13(i)), detection and reporting are a
single act: there is no separate filing step whose friction could
decouple them. Abstention (a user who suspects the item and abandons
the dialog)
records nothing and is counted as a miss inside $1-q$ . An honest
identity whose rejection rights are suspended, whose $r_{\max}$ slots are
transiently full, whose per-epoch ceiling $c_{\mathrm{ceil}}$ is reached, or whose
single per-item initiation is spent is authority-inactive: its
inspections are excluded trials, not silenced misses — its
acceptance carries no promotion weight and its rejection no
initiation (Definition S4.6(a),
Remark S5.18) — and such states are rare for honest
identities (honest charges accrue at rate at most $1-p_{b}$ per benign
fresh inspection, both the rate trigger and the ceiling calibrated
strictly above that baseline, Remark S5.5; slots free on
resolution or expiry, Lemma S5.24; the ceiling and the
per-item allowance renew each epoch).
Covered gates (Rule R13(i)) are not silenced misses but
excluded trials: both branches are protocol-inert, so they
belong to no trial class of Theorem S5.13, and the
identity’s inspection of the bytes, where one is counted at all, is
the earlier decision that created its coverage; so, symmetrically, are authority-inactive inspections (Definition S4.6(a)), and so are cache gates on the
accepting side (accept-inert, Definition S4.6(iii)),
whose rejections take effect as dispute initiations or corroborations
but never as verdicts (Rule R13(iii)).
Freshness being shared-presence-scoped (Definition S4.5),
the fresh class can include identities with a prior unlogged
personal decision on the bytes (before first publication, or across
a retirement gap); $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ and $q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ are lower bounds over this
log-defined class, so a previously fooled but log-fresh inspector is
inside the class the parameters must cover — one more reason the
bounds are weakest-target infima, never averages, and one the
validation strata of N17 must include (prior-exposure
stratification).
Case (a) of
Theorem S5.13 needs only $q_{\mathrm{pre}}>0$ : one honest effective qualifying rejection
— a rejecting decision at a protocol-bearing gate by an identity
currently holding dispute-initiation authority (Rule R13(i),(v)) — denies the ordinary path, a one-sided
disjunction, not a vote; coverage-silenced and authority-inactive detections are excluded trials rather than misses (Definition S4.6(a)), the corresponding acceptances carrying no weight; post-promotion rejections beyond the review cap of Rule R13(vi) concern the suspended state only and touch no case-(a) trial. The panel is a bounded jury: joint safety and
a low wrongful-eviction bound need the better-than-chance condition $q_{\mathrm{pan}}+p_{b}>1$ ; safety alone
can be forced for any $q_{\mathrm{pan}}>0$ by raising $m$ toward $k$ , at the price of
a weaker wrongful-eviction bound. The regime $q\to 0$ is the disclosed limitation
S14. The exponents $N-f$ and $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ are protocol constants;
the bases $1-q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ , $1-q_{\mathrm{pan}}$ are behavioural parameters of the targeted population.
Because panel verdicts are never penalised and first charges are free
each epoch (Rule R13(v)), the protocol attaches no penalty to
a first good-faith rejection, so it does not disincentivise the gate
behaviour that generates $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ . Finally, $q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ and $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ condition on
the same human act consistently: an abstaining demander is a miss
inside $1-q_{\mathrm{pre}}$ and a non-verdict inside $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ ; high abstention
degrades resolution speed, never the validity of the safety parameter.

Proposition S5.20 (Wrongful-eviction bound).

Adopt the default bars, and suppose each honest juror’s fresh
inspection of a benign item (Definition S4.10) ends in an accepting decision with
conditional probability at least $p_{b}$ given all prior protocol
events, read per the conditioning
convention of §S8.1. If $\delta_{b}:=p_{b}-\frac{m}{k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}>0$ (the
wrongful-eviction margin), then, uniformly over
adversary strategies,

 $\mathbb{P}[\text{wrongful eviction}]\ \leq\ e^{-2\delta_{b}^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}.$ 

Moreover both margins ( $\delta>0$ and $\delta_{b}>0$ ) are simultaneously
achievable by an integer $m$ whenever $(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})(q+p_{b}-1)>f_{\mathrm{s}}+1$ ; in
particular, whenever inspectors discriminate better than chance
( $q+p_{b}>1$ ) and $k$ is large enough.

Proof.

Under the default bars, a resolved panel’s outcome is eviction or
vindication, exclusively (Lemma S5.7(i)–(ii)), and
eviction requires $m_{\mathrm{rej}}=k+1-m$ rejecting verdicts. Suppose
the adversary casts $a\leq f_{\mathrm{s}}$ verdicts (at most one per identity for
the dispute’s entire life, INV-5); at most $a$ of them
reject, so eviction requires at least $m_{\mathrm{rej}}-a$ honest
rejecting verdicts, while honest verdicts number at most $k-a$ . For an
honest verdict, writing $p_{\mathrm{acc}},p_{\mathrm{rej}}$ 
for its conditional accept/reject disposition probabilities at its
rendering atom, with $p_{\mathrm{acc}}\geq p_{b}$ and
 $p_{\mathrm{acc}}+p_{\mathrm{rej}}\leq 1$ (§S8.1),
Lemma S8.1(ii) bounds the conditional rejection
probability at the verdict’s header-inclusive $\sigma$ -algebra by $1-p_{b}$ ; these $\sigma$ -algebras satisfy the occurrence and measurability conditions of
Remark S8.4 exactly as in
Theorem S5.13(b). Lemma S8.2(i), in that
form, applied to the rejection indicators $Z_{i}$ , gives
i.i.d. $\mathrm{Bernoulli}(1-p_{b})$ majorants $Y_{i}$ with
 $Z_{i}\leq Y_{i}$ pointwise, unrealised verdicts contributing zero. On the
wrongful-eviction event,
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}-a\leq\sum_{i\leq k-a}Z_{i}\leq\sum_{i\leq k-a}Y_{i}\leq\sum_{i\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}Y_{i}+(f_{\mathrm{s}}-a)$ , whence
 $\sum_{i\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}Y_{i}\geq m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ , uniformly in $a$ .
Since $m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}=(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})-(m-1)=\big((1-p_{b})+\delta_{b}^{\prime}\big)(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})$ with
 $\delta_{b}^{\prime}:=p_{b}-\tfrac{m-1}{k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}\geq\delta_{b}>0$ , Hoeffding’s
inequality (Lemma S8.8) bounds the event by
 $e^{-2\delta_{b}^{\prime 2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}\leq e^{-2\delta_{b}^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}$ . Simultaneity: an integer $m$ lying strictly
between $f_{\mathrm{s}}+(1-q)(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})$ and $p_{b}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})$ exists whenever the gap
between those quantities exceeds one, i.e. whenever
 $(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})(p_{b}+q-1)>f_{\mathrm{s}}+1$ .
∎

Remark S5.21 (Resolution as a demand-side event).

Resolution is a demand-side property: the demand-paced panel has no
deadline, $g_{k}(M)$ may grow arbitrarily slowly, and an item without
demand expires with no protocol consequence, for which liveness (in
the classical, eventual-success sense) is vacuous (Remark S5.26).
Proposition S5.20 and Theorem S6.13(ii) bound
wrongful eviction unconditionally, asserting no probability for
resolution itself. As a corollary reading: conditional on resolution,
vindication has probability at least
 $1-e^{-2\delta_{b}^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}/\mathbb{P}[\text{resolution}]$ .

Proposition S5.22 (Publisher accountability is safe for benign items).

Under Rule R13(iv),(vii):

(i) 

the adversary’s panel verdicts alone can never trigger a
publisher penalty against any publisher, since eviction requires
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ rejecting verdicts;

(ii) 

with the default bars and wrongful-eviction margin $\delta_{b}>0$ ,
write $p^{*}:=e^{-2\delta_{b}^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}$ ; each panel on a benign item
wrongfully evicts (penalising the publisher) with probability at most
 $p^{*}$ conditionally on all prior protocol events
(Proposition S5.20, whose hypothesis is conditional). Three
consequences, in decreasing strength of hypothesis.
(Fixed instances.) For any $r$ dispute instances on benign
items of the same publisher, predictably specified:
membership of an instance in the family is determined no later than
that instance’s opening atom, measurably in the opening-inclusive
log history and before any verdict content of that instance (any
advance deterministic choice included); a family selected
retrospectively from panel outcomes is not a fixed-instance family
— it is covered by the capped tier where the deterministic cap
 $L\leq\bar{\ell}$ is available, and otherwise only by the linear
random-count tier. For such a family, the probability that all $r$ 
instances end in wrongful eviction is at most $(p^{*})^{\,r}$ ,
uniformly over adversary strategies and over any dependence among
the panels.
(Random count, linear.) With $L$ the number of dispute
instances opened on the publisher’s benign items in the epoch,
indexing panel trials at their opening events (an instance
unresolved at the epoch horizon evicts nobody and carries indicator
zero), the expected number of wrongful evictions is at most
 $p^{*}\,\mathbb{E}[L]$ , whence
 $\mathbb{P}[\text{at least }r\text{ wrongful evictions}]\leq p^{*}\mathbb{E}[L]/r$ 
(Markov); $\mathbb{E}[L]$ is bounded by the dispute ledger
(Lemma S6.7, $\bar{D}_{v}$ ) summed over the publisher’s
items, and, for fast-track reviews, deterministically by $E_{\max}$ per
item per epoch (Rule R13(vi)). No $r$ -fold exponent is
claimed at this generality.
(Random count, capped.) If the deployment enforces a
deterministic per-epoch cap $L\leq\bar{\ell}$ on dispute instances
against one publisher’s items, then
 $\mathbb{P}[\text{at least }r\text{ wrongful evictions}]\leq\binom{\bar{\ell}}{r}(p^{*})^{\,r}$ ,
decaying geometrically in $r$ whenever
 $2\delta_{b}^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})>\ln\bar{\ell}$ ;

(iii) 

every eviction of an adversary-published poisoned item
consumes the adversary’s allowance and, on repetition within an epoch, its
submission rights and attestation weight; eviction of poison published by
an honest-but-deceived identity penalises that publisher, by design
(S10);

(iv) 

no publisher penalty ever touches a local store
(Lemma S5.8).

Dispute expiry (Rule R14(i)) is neither an eviction nor a
vindication and triggers no penalty on either side. Protection is
claimed for publishers of benign items: an honest-but-deceived
publisher of attacker-crafted content is penalised on eviction,
by design (S10).

Proof.

(i) Eviction requires at least $m_{\mathrm{rej}}$ rejecting verdicts
with $m_{\mathrm{rej}}>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ ; by INV-5 the adversary
casts at most $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ verdicts on the dispute over its entire life,
however many epoch boundaries it spans, so at least one honest panelist
must reject; with no honest rejections there is no eviction and hence
no penalty. (ii) Under the default bars a resolved panel’s outcome is
eviction or vindication, exclusively (Lemma S5.7(i)–(ii)), so the
penalising event is exactly wrongful eviction, bounded per panel by
Proposition S5.20. For the fixed-instance product bound, the $r$ predictably specified instances are indexed in opening
order, and their honest verdicts jointly in log order of their
header atoms; a verdict’s instance membership and within-instance
honest ordinal are thereby determined at its header atom,
 $\mathcal{G}_{i}$ -measurably, as Lemma S8.7 requires (§S8.1). The benign-item hypothesis
of Proposition S5.20 bounds each honest verdict’s
conditional rejection probability by $1-p_{b}$ at its header-inclusive
 $\sigma$ -algebra — including the outcomes of earlier-resolved
panels — so Lemma S8.2(i), in the $\sigma$ -algebra
form of Remark S8.4, applied once to the joint
sequence, supplies coupling variables with $Z_{i}\leq Y_{i}$ pointwise on
realised verdicts. Allocate cells: assign the $j$ -th specified
instance’s honest verdicts, in log order, to cells
 $(j,1),\dots,(j,k-f_{\mathrm{s}})$ , discarding any beyond the $(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})$ -th; a
verdict’s cell is determined at its header atom (its dispute instance
and within-instance honest ordinal are log facts prior to its
content), hence $\mathcal{G}_{i}$ -measurably and injectively, the
instances’ index sets disjoint — not necessarily contiguous, which
the argument never needs. By Lemma S8.7, the cell array
 $(X_{j,\ell})_{j\leq r,\,\ell\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}$ (coupling variables on filled
cells, fresh independent uniforms on never-filled cells) is i.i.d. $\mathrm{Bernoulli}(1-p_{b})$ . On the all- $r$ -evict event, instance $j$ 
resolves in eviction: with $a_{j}\leq f_{\mathrm{s}}$ adversarial verdicts
(INV-5), honest verdicts number $h_{j}\leq k-a_{j}$ , of which
at least $m_{\mathrm{rej}}-a_{j}$ reject. If $h_{j}\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ , every honest verdict
sits among the first $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ cells, which then contain at least
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}-a_{j}\geq m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ rejections; otherwise
at most $h_{j}-(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})\leq f_{\mathrm{s}}-a_{j}$ of the rejecting ones lie beyond cell
position $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ , and the first $k-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ honest verdicts contain at
least $(m_{\mathrm{rej}}-a_{j})-(f_{\mathrm{s}}-a_{j})=m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ 
rejections. In either case
 $\sum_{\ell\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}X_{j,\ell}\ \geq\ \sum_{\ell\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}Z_{(j,\ell)}\ \geq\ m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}$ , never-filled cells contributing
nonnegatively, uniformly in $a_{j}$ . The all- $r$ -evict event is
therefore contained in
 $\bigcap_{j\leq r}\big\{\sum_{\ell\leq k-f_{\mathrm{s}}}X_{j,\ell}\geq m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}\big\}$ ; the array being i.i.d. with the rows
indexed by disjoint cell sets, the intersection’s probability is the
product of $r$ identical binomial tails, each at most $p^{*}$ by the
margin computation of Proposition S5.20
( $m_{\mathrm{rej}}-f_{\mathrm{s}}=\big((1-p_{b})+\delta_{b}^{\prime}\big)(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})$ ,
 $\delta_{b}^{\prime}\geq\delta_{b}$ , Hoeffding via Lemma S8.8), giving
 $(p^{*})^{\,r}$ . For the
linear tier, let $W_{j}$ indicate wrongful eviction of the $j$ -th
instance opened in the epoch, indexed at its opening event, with
 $W_{j}=0$ for instances unresolved at the horizon;
 $\mathbb{P}[W_{j}=1\mid\text{prior atoms}]\leq p^{*}$ by Proposition S5.20
(its hypothesis conditions on all prior protocol events, the opening
atom included), so the delayed-marks lemma (Lemma S8.5, with
 $\mathcal{G}_{j}$ generated by all atoms up to the $j$ -th opening event
and $W_{j}$ resolved only at that instance’s resolution, possibly after
later openings) gives $\mathbb{E}[\sum_{j}W_{j}]\leq p^{*}\,\mathbb{E}[L]$ and Markov the
tail. For the capped tier, fix any set of $r$ indices
 $j_{1}<\dots<j_{r}\leq\bar{\ell}$ ; the fixed-instance argument above, applied
to the $j_{1}$ -th, …,  $j_{r}$ -th instances opened in the epoch (the
instances being determined by their opening order, and their honest
verdicts jointly log-ordered), bounds the probability that all $r$ 
resolve in eviction by $(p^{*})^{\,r}$ . At least $r$ wrongful evictions
entails that some such index set evicts wholly; the union bound over
the $\binom{\bar{\ell}}{r}$ index sets gives the claim. No direct
stochastic domination of delayed, opening-indexed eviction indicators
is asserted. The same-bytes sharpening is
INV-5 with Definition S4.5: a cast verdict is a
logged decision on the bytes, so its caster is ineligible for any
later dispute instance on the same bytes under
Rule R13(iii)(b). (iii) By
construction of clause (vii), read per publisher. (iv) Clause (vii) modifies submission
rights and attestation weight, which are shared-tier attributes;
Lemma S5.8 covers the rest. The expiry clause is
Rule R14(i) verbatim.
∎

Remark S5.23 (Reading of Proposition S5.22(ii)).

The escalating penalties of Rule R13(vii), requiring
 $r\geq 2$ wrongful evictions within one epoch, are exponentially rare
per specified pair of instances, bounded in expectation in general,
and carry the $r$ -fold exponent exactly where a deterministic
instance cap holds (capped tier). For repeated panels on the
same byte string the bounds sharpen: every verdict is a logged
decision consuming shared-presence freshness
(Definition S4.5, Rule R13(iii)(b)–(c)), so
each identity casts at most one verdict across all of the bytes’
panels ever; in particular the adversary’s $f_{\mathrm{s}}$ seat-eligible
identities are spent once across those panels, not once per panel.

Lemma S5.24 (Bounds on rejection-based denial of service).

(i) 

At any time, at most $f\cdot r_{\max}$ items are held in Disputed by
adversarial rejections. Moreover, adversarial dispute-initiations against
any single item number at most $f$ per epoch (Rule R13(i),
single-initiation clause), independently of $r_{\max}$ .

(ii) 

(Demand-paced resolution.) Unconditionally, with no
behavioural assumption (persistence exceptions per INV-3), a freeze costs each affected demander at most
one accepting confirmation per demanded pair, and only if the demander
holds no local in-scope copy: exactly the required confirmation any
demander of an unpromoted pair owes regardless
(Theorem S6.2(i)). A demander who rejects or abandons
incurs inspection effort, a distinct resource disclosed in N18, not bounded by the cap. A dispute therefore suspends only the community
amortisation for its demanders and never pushes anyone above the
single-user cap, however long the freeze
lasts. Under A9, the number so affected is additionally
bounded: the demanders affected while the dispute is unresolved number at most $\kappa$ in
expectation, with exponential tail (Lemma S5.6), independent
of the item’s popularity and of the freeze’s wall-clock duration. A
dispute on an undemanded item affects no user and expires within $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ of its
last demand (Rule R14). (Wide pre-exposure:
Remark S5.25; S6.)

(iii) 

No panel-lapse exploitation; slot economics. There is no panel
lapse, hence no forced re-entry: an unresolved dispute never drains the
owner’s submission budget. A rejection slot stays occupied until
resolution or expiry: against demanded items, disputes resolve at
demand rate into the accountability schedule; against undemanded items,
the slot pins only an undemanded item and frees at expiry. (Remark S5.26; S5.)

(iv) 

A sustained wrongful-rejection campaign alters no local store
(Lemma S5.8) and costs each user at most the single-user
floor (Theorem S6.2); beyond the $f$ free first charges each
epoch, it expends the adversary’s rejection rights at an escalating rate
(Rule R13(v)). Its throughput is bounded by three deterministic
caps, each in its own currency: charged dispute-initiations (those
eventually contradicted by vindication) number at most $c_{\mathrm{acc}}f$ per
epoch (Lemma S5.4); concurrent open rejections number at
most $fr_{\max}$ at any moment, and freezes against any single item at most
 $f$ per epoch (clause (i), single initiation); and uncharged
terminal disputes (ending in eviction or expiry) number at most
 $E_{\max}$ per shared-tier presence per item per epoch
(Rule R14(i)), each retiring that presence. No single
constant bounds all wrongful rejections across all items — a slot freed
by expiry may be re-used against another item — and what each re-use
buys is one freeze, resolved or expired at that item’s own demand clock,
within the single-user cap. The allowances renew at epoch boundaries (N14).

Proof.

(i) Each adversarial identity holds at most $r_{\max}$ open rejections
(Rule R13(i)) and the adversary controls at most $f$ 
identities. The per-item clause caps each identity at one initiation
per item per epoch. (ii) The harm of a freeze is exactly the
amortisation it suspends: the demanders who must confirm personally
while the item is frozen; by Rule R13(iii)–(iv) the eligible
deciding demanders cast the verdicts and, with the default bars,
resolution occurs by the $k$ -th verdict; each affected demander’s
accepting confirmation, where one occurs, is the one required
confirmation they would owe on any unpromoted pair,
so the per-user floor is untouched (INV-3); the
in-expectation and tail bounds are Lemma S5.6; an item with no
new demand meets the expiry clause of Rule R14(i).
(iii) By Rule R13(iv) the only re-entry paths are the
optional neither-bar outcome and post-expiry republication, both
owner-initiated; slots free on resolution or expiry
(Rules R13(iv), R14(i)). (iv) Every
dispute-initiating rejection contradicted by vindication charges the
rejecter’s schedule and ceiling (Rule R13(v)); by
Lemma S5.4 the adversary’s effective charged
dispute-initiations are deterministically at most $c_{\mathrm{acc}}$ per identity per epoch,
bounding per-epoch charged-initiation throughput at $c_{\mathrm{acc}}f$ as a protocol fact; the concurrency, single-initiation, and terminal-dispute caps are Rule R13(i) and Rule R14(i) verbatim. By
Lemma S5.8 no local store is altered, and each user’s
required cost remains at most the single-user cap
(Theorem S6.2).
∎

Remark S5.25 (Wide pre-exposure).

An item pre-exposed so widely that few verdict-eligible demanders
remain simply stalls frozen: the freeze blocks promotion at
every tool for as long as the panel is unresolved, and the
demand-paced panel carries no deadline an adversary could exploit
(Rule R13(ii)–(iii)); this is the deterministic failure
mode of A9, and its cost is availability, never safety.
For a poisoned item the stall is borne by the adversary’s own
submission. A benign item can also be stalled this way — by
pre-exposure or pool-skewed demand, with or without adversarial
help — and then the availability cost falls on its publisher and
demanders; the harm is bounded exactly as for any freeze: each
demander pays at most one required confirmation per pair, within the single-user cap
(Lemma S5.24(ii), unconditional clause), the item remains
personally usable through each user’s own gate
(Rule R13(ii)), local stores are untouched
(Lemma S5.8), and an item whose demand lapses expires
harmlessly (Rule R14(i)). (S6.)

Remark S5.26 (Roles of $T_{\mathrm{win}}$ , $T_{\mathrm{pan}}$ , $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ ; the demand-paced
design).

The window $T_{\mathrm{win}}$ forbids the promotion race: finalisation occurs only
at window end, so any qualifying rejection intervenes even if $N$ 
acceptances arrive first: this is what makes the partition of
Theorem S5.13 well-defined; and each attempt’s identity-disjoint credited endorsement set (Lemma S5.3, Definition S5.1) is what makes the per-attempt
marginal bounds and their union valid
(Corollary S5.27). $T_{\mathrm{pan}}$ survives only as the clock of
fast-track suspension panels,
whose log-fresh jurors are drawn from demanders during suspension and
prior adopters without a logged decision on the bytes
(Rule R13(vi)); their supply is not guaranteed by
construction — prior adopters with logged decisions are ineligible
— but is availability-side, exactly A9’s province;
the ordinary panel carries no global
deadline; it is demand-paced: verdicts accumulate at the item’s own
demand rate, so resolution time scales inversely with demand while the
harm of the freeze scales directly with it, and their product is
bounded (Lemma S5.24(ii)). A disputed item with too little
fresh demand simply waits, exactly as it would have waited for ordinary
promotion; an item nobody demands expires harmlessly within $T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ .
Zero-tolerance designs lack an accountability clock entirely; the
demand-paced panel provides one wherever fresh demand exists. An
evict-and-restart rule would instead multiply per-item cost by the
number of rejections; the panel’s decisive, demand-paced resolution is
what keeps the dispute load an additive
 $\mathcal{O}(E_{\max}N+c_{\mathrm{tool}}\bar{D}_{v}\kappa)$ per demanded item, with $\bar{D}_{v}$ the
expected dispute count of Lemma S6.7, absorbed by the cross-term and
shrinking-front-loading conditions of Theorem S6.13
(S5).

Corollary S5.27 (Submission budget and publisher accountability).

Promotion occurs only at an attempt’s finalisation
(Definition S5.1), and every attempt is counted at its
anchor — a logged event preceding every decision creditable
to the attempt, hence a sound conditioning point
(Definition S5.1, §S8.1). Under the default
bars, the anchors lying in a given epoch are of exactly two kinds,
each bounded by the submission rate limit: presence-creating
submissions, and finalisation events, each of which closes an entry
opened by a same-epoch rate-limited (re-)submission (windows are
epoch-contained, convention (C4); Rule R12). Hence the
promotion attempts of adversary-published poisoned items
anchored in a given epoch number at most $2f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ (paraphrase
variants and re-entries included). Each such attempt gathers an
identity-disjoint credited endorsement set (Lemma S5.3)
and is bounded marginally by Theorem S5.13 at its own anchor
(under its hypotheses); the union bound, valid under arbitrary
dependence across attempts, gives

 $\mathbb{P}[\text{any adversary-submitted poison promoted at an attempt
anchored in the epoch}]\\
\ \leq\ 2f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}\,\Big((1-q)^{N-f}+e^{-2\delta^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}\Big).$ 

(Attempts anchored while the bytes hold live $\mathsf{CC}$ status — additional-tool promotions of already-promoted bytes — are covered under the extended context reading at the close of Theorem S5.13’s proof; the event that any poison is ever promoted at all consumes only $\mathsf{CC}$ -free anchors.)
Every promotion belongs to an attempt anchored in some epoch, so
summing over the anchor epochs $1,\dots,E$ from deployment
inception bounds the probability that any adversary-submitted poison
is ever promoted within that horizon by
 $2f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}\,E\,\big((1-q)^{N-f}+e^{-2\delta^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}\big)$ . For an interval not beginning at deployment inception, no bound
conditional on its realised carry-in pool is asserted — the set of
attempts still live at the interval’s start is itself
outcome-selected, for the reason of Remark S5.28 —
and the valid statement is the anchor-epoch bound applied to all
anchor epochs from deployment inception through the interval’s end. (Under the looser bars
permitted by Rule R13(iv), neither-bar resolutions are
additional anchors; the numeric count $2f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ is asserted for the
default bars, and loose-bar deployments use the expectation form
below, with the anchored-attempt count enlarged accordingly;
per-attempt coverage per Remark S5.14.)

Poisoned content published by an honest-but-deceived identity
(S10) is not counted by $2f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ . Writing
 $S_{\mathrm{anc}}$ for the (in general random) number of poisoned
promotion attempts anchored in the epoch, whatever their publishers:
indexing attempts at their anchor atoms and applying the delayed-marks
lemma (Lemma S8.5) to the per-attempt success indicators
— each bounded, conditionally on the anchor-inclusive
 $\sigma$ -algebra of its anchor atom, by the per-attempt marginal
bound, and each resolved only at the attempt’s finalisation, possibly
after later attempts’ anchors, which Lemma S8.5 permits
—

 $\mathbb{P}[\text{any poison promoted at an attempt anchored in the epoch}]\\
\leq\mathbb{E}[S_{\mathrm{anc}}]\Big((1-q)^{N-f}+e^{-2\delta^{2}(k-f_{\mathrm{s}})}\Big).$ 

and a deployment enforcing a deterministic cap
 $S_{\mathrm{anc}}\leq\bar{S}$ may substitute $\bar{S}$ for the
expectation. (No bound keyed to the attempts finalising in an
epoch is asserted: that family is outcome-selected,
Remark S5.28. Nor is a bound conditional on a realised
value of $S_{\mathrm{anc}}$ asserted: the attempt count may be
adaptively determined by earlier outcomes; the expectation and
deterministic-cap forms are the valid system-wide statements.)
 $\mathbb{E}[S_{\mathrm{anc}}]$ is not bounded in terms of the adversary’s
resources alone — it depends on how often false endorsement
(A5) and publication co-occur among honest identities —
though the per-identity rate limit yields the crude default-bar cap
 $S_{\mathrm{anc}}\leq 2M\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ over $M$ eligible identities, and each
such item, on eviction, penalises its publisher
(Rule R13(vii)), economically suppressing repeat deception.
Under permanent freshness, the credited endorsement sets of all
attempts of a given byte string are identity-disjoint over the
deployment’s entire history, not merely within an epoch
(Lemma S5.3); consequently the number of attempts of the
same bytes that can ever gather $N$ credited endorsements is at most
 $\lfloor H_{\mathrm{acc}}/(N-f)\rfloor$ , where $H_{\mathrm{acc}}$ is
the total number of honest identities that ever accept those bytes at
a gate. Moreover, every eviction of a poisoned attempt triggers the
publisher-accountability consequences of Rule R13(vii)
(cool-down; on repetition within an epoch, suspension of submission
rights and attestation-weight reduction); the quantitative effect on
future submission capacity depends on the deployment’s escalation
schedule (§S4.2, required parameters) and is consumed
by no proof, while a benign publisher’s exposure to the same penalty is
bounded by Proposition S5.22(ii).

Proof.

An attempt is counted at its anchor. Under the default bars every
anchor is a presence-creating submission or a finalisation
(Definition S5.1); distinct attempts have distinct
anchors; presence-creating submissions of adversary-published items in
the epoch number at most $f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ (Rule R12); and each
finalisation in the epoch closes an entry whose opening
(re-)submission lies in the same epoch (windows are epoch-contained,
convention (C4)), so finalisation anchors also number at most
 $f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ : at most $2f\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ anchored attempts. Union bound over these
attempts, each covered marginally by Theorem S5.13 at its
anchor on its own credited set (Lemma S5.3); a union
bound requires no independence across the events it covers. The
horizon form sums the per-epoch display over the anchor epochs of the
horizon, every promotion belonging to the attempt anchored in some
such epoch (from deployment inception; a general interval is covered by summing from inception through its end, no carry-in-conditional bound being asserted). The
system-wide form is the delayed-marks lemma (Lemma S8.5)
applied to anchor-indexed success indicators. Its occurrence events lie in the conditioning $\sigma$ -algebras: take
 $\mathcal{G}_{i}$ generated by all atoms up to and including the $i$ -th
anchor atom, so that $O_{i}$ is $\mathcal{G}_{i}$ -measurable; the
per-attempt bound of Theorem S5.13 is stated conditionally
on exactly this anchor-inclusive $\sigma$ -algebra, so it bounds each
mark conditionally on $\mathcal{G}_{i}$ , the mark resolving only at the
attempt’s finalisation, which Lemma S8.5 permits; the
deterministic-cap form substitutes $\bar{S}$ . The lifetime clause is
immediate from identity-disjointness of credited decisions across all
attempts. The accountability clauses are Rule R13(vii) and
Proposition S5.22(ii)–(iii).
∎

Remark S5.28 (Why attempts are keyed to anchor epochs, not
finalisation epochs).

An earlier version of Corollary S5.27 counted the
attempts whose finalisation lies in the epoch. That attribution
is unsound, for the same optional-selection reason the attempt anchor
exists (Definition S5.1, commentary): banked decisions
logged during dormancy are public log events preceding the owner’s
resubmission decision, so the set of attempts finalising in a given
epoch is outcome-selected — an adversary maintaining many anchored
dormant attempts on distinct byte strings and resubmitting only those
with favourable banked outcomes makes the per-finalisation-epoch
attempt count small while the epoch’s promotion probability is
governed by the whole maintained pool. Anchors, by contrast, precede
every decision creditable to their attempts, so the anchored-in-epoch
family is determined before any of the outcomes it is charged with,
and the delayed-marks lemma applies. The per-attempt bound of
Theorem S5.13 and the lifetime ceiling of
Remark S5.29 are unaffected: only the calendar
attribution of attempts to epochs changes.

Remark S5.29 (Lifetime exposure under permanent freshness).

The horizon union over anchor epochs in Corollary S5.27, and the lifetime ceiling,
count anchored attempts — the lifetime ceiling, threshold-reaching attempts — whose honest endorsers are never re-used: every
pipeline entry of a given byte string that reaches its threshold
consumes $N-f$ honest identities’ first-and-only logged
decisions on those bytes, so the adversary cannot re-use a
previously logged-deciding population, and
 $\lfloor H_{\mathrm{acc}}/(N-f)\rfloor$ is a hard ceiling on the
number of threshold-reaching attempts of the same bytes over
the deployment’s lifetime, independent of the number of epochs.
Unsuccessful attempts — each gathering fewer than $N-f$ new honest credited
acceptances — and presentations yielding no logged acceptance are
not bounded by this ceiling; they are governed by the per-epoch rate
limit $\rho_{\mathrm{sub}}$ and the per-attempt marginal bound of
Theorem S5.13, each attempt consuming its own union-bound
term.
The ceiling is per exact byte string (convention (C5)): an adversary
minting byte-distinct paraphrase variants holds a fresh ceiling per
variant, each consuming its own rate-limited submission and
union-bound term (S1, Corollary S5.27);
the lifetime clause therefore hard-bounds successful re-entry of
fixed bytes, while variant families are governed by the
per-epoch rate and calibration clauses, not by this ceiling.

Remark S5.30 (Weighted form).

With endorsement weights, replace the exponent $N-f$ by
 $(W-W_{\mathrm{adv}})/w_{\max}$ . With seat weights in $[w_{\mathrm{seat}},w_{\max}]$ and a
vindication weight bar $W_{m}$ , honest accepting weight at least
 $W_{m}-f_{\mathrm{s}}w_{\max}$ is required; seat weights are attestation-layer
constants fixed at verdict time, hence predictable in the sense of
Lemma S8.8’s second clause, and Hoeffding for weighted
sums (Lemma S8.8) gives
 $\exp(-2t^{2}/((k-f_{\mathrm{s}})w_{\max}^{2}))$ with $t$ the weight margin: constants
change, exponents do not, and the bound remains reach-free.

Remark S5.31 (Per-source-class thresholds).

Promotion thresholds may be set per source class of the
underlying content (authentication is not trust: a signed phishing
message is still an attack; so source identity only tunes the
threshold, never bypasses the quorum, and never widens a tool scope).
All bounds hold with $W$ , $N$ , $m$ , $k$ read as the minimum over source
classes; Theorem S5.11 requires
 $\min_{\mathrm{class}}W>W_{\mathrm{adv}}$ and $\min_{\mathrm{class}}m>f_{\mathrm{s}}$ .

## Appendix S6 The Memory-Verification Budget

Scope.

All results in this appendix count confirmations attached to
reusable memory content, per (value, tool) pair: a one-time personal cap of private
values, and the stream of shareable Confirmed items. Per-task
confirmations (for freshly composed sensitive arguments and for
crystallizing ephemeral task content) are common to every gated
design, scale with usage rather than population, and are orthogonal to
the amortisation analysed here. PerMIT’s distinction is layered. Pathwise — independent of every federated and demand assumption, persistence exceptions per INV-3 — no user’s required
cost exceeds once per (value, tool) pair, ever, and the epoch’s
expected shared required load never exceeds the standalone single-user
mass $d_{\mathrm{tool}}$ — the cost of having no community at all
(Theorem S6.2(i)–(ii)); this cap is the appendix’s firmest guarantee: pathwise, consuming no federated or demand assumption (its expected clauses read the reference model’s masses), subject only to the stated first-party persistence exceptions. On top
of it, the ledger-charged shared stream is at most the
front-loading multiple $B_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\bar{\gamma}$ of the standalone mass,
independently of item popularity (Theorem S6.9,
Remark S6.11); and the ledger residual — demand
arriving outside every charged piece, in particular handle-expressed
arrivals of already-certified bytes, which gate by design
(Rule R4(c), S18) — has its benign,
assumption-conforming component vanish under the full vanishing
conditions of Theorem S6.9(iii): the
coverage-and-representation assumption A11 together with
the product-rate condition $D_{0}(M)V(M)=o(M)$ ,
 $\varepsilon(M)\,d_{\mathrm{good}}(M)\to 0$ (under
A13, from $\varepsilon(M)\to 0$ ; $d_{\mathrm{good}}$ 
per Lemma S6.8), and bounded tool spread
(A12); these conditions are sufficient, not
necessary, and demand on poisoned or assumption-violating pairs
remains in $r^{(e)}_{\mathrm{bad}}$ (Corollary S6.10).
Under the measured corridor of §S6.5 — whose
front-loading rows describe deployments whose time geometry changes
with scale, not consequences of population growth alone
(Remark S6.12) — both shared components vanish; where
the corridor or A11 fails, the universal fallback
applies to the required component: the epoch’s expected shared
required load remains at most the standalone mass
 $d_{\mathrm{tool}}$ (Theorem S6.2(ii)), the once-per-pair
cap binding throughout. Voluntary invitation confirmations remain
governed by the ledger rows of Lemma S6.7; rejection and
attention effort is accounted separately in N18. No
standalone-mass ceiling is claimed for those additional resources.
Headline readings of this appendix elsewhere in the paper must carry
these conditions: “shared verification load vanishes” is asserted
only under measured saturation, smoothness, coverage, juror-supply,
and bounded-mass hypotheses (A10–A13),
never from population growth alone (Remark S6.12); the
firmest statements are the pathwise once-per-pair cap and the
reference-model standalone-mass ceiling (Theorem S6.2).

## S6.1 Model

• 

A population of $M$ users; a universe $\mathcal{V}$ of shareable
Confirmed items. The accounting unit is the (value, tool) pair
(the endorsement unit, §S2.1): a user’s demand, required
cost, and local coverage are all per pair. Personal and
context-dependent values lie outside $\mathcal{V}$ (Rule R12,
Lemma S5.2); $d_{\mathrm{priv}}$ is the expected number of distinct personal (value, tool)
pairs a user ever confirms, once each over the deployment’s lifetime
(the pair is the endorsement unit, §S2.1; a personal value
used at $j$ tools contributes $j$ pairs). $d_{\mathrm{priv}}$ is a lifetime
quantity; where it appears alongside single-epoch masses
(Theorem S6.2(ii)), the sum is read as a lifetime personal
term plus a per-epoch shareable term, each bounding its own component.

• 

The independent reference model (IRM) Fagin (1977), per pair
and per user: user $u$ demands the pair $(v,t)$ within the epoch with
probability $p_{u,v,t}$ , independently across users; the model is
heterogeneous, and $p_{v,t}:=M^{-1}\sum_{u}p_{u,v,t}$ ,
 $p_{v}:=M^{-1}\sum_{u}\mathbb{P}[\text{$u$ demands $v$ at some tool}]$ denote
the population-average marginals, so $p_{v,t}\leq p_{v}$ and
 $\mathbb{E}[n_{v,t}]=Mp_{v,t}$ . Every expected-count argument in this
appendix consumes only cross-user independence and this identity,
never identical distribution across users (the owner’s degenerate
indicator, next bullet, is the one systematic heterogeneity). The
single-epoch masses are
 $d:=\sum_{v}p_{v}$ and $d_{\mathrm{tool}}:=\sum_{v,t}p_{v,t}$ , both finite,
owner coupling folded in (next bullet),
with $d_{\mathrm{tool}}\leq c_{\mathrm{tool}}\,d$ under A12. Under
this model $n_{v}$ and $n_{v,t}$ denote the latent
counts $\sum_{u}D_{u,v,t}$ ; the log-defined realized counts satisfy
 $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v,t}\leq n_{v,t}$ pathwise
(§S8.1), so every expected bound stated against $Mp_{v,t}$ 
covers the realized events the ledger charges.

• 

Owner coupling. The owner’s publication schedule for
each item — the epochs in which the owner submits or resubmits $v$ 
(Rule R12) — is carried in the exogenous demand factor
alongside the demand indicators (§S8.1): it is owner
behaviour, exogenous to the protocol and the attacker exactly as the
typed stream is (A6). In every scheduled epoch the owner’s
own demand indicator for $v$ is set to one, at the owner’s creating
tool (formally, the owner’s marginal $p_{u,v,t}$ is degenerate at one
in scheduled epochs; other users’ indicators and the cross-user
independence are untouched — the heterogeneous form of the previous
bullet): submission is modelled as demand-driven, latently mirroring
Definition S4.4’s clause that the owner’s publish
confirmation is a realized demand event. Consequently every epoch
containing a submission or re-entry of $v$ has $n_{v}\geq 1$ , and every
realized demander is a latent demander, so
 $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v}\leq n_{v}$ pathwise. A shared-tier presence can, by
contrast, be carried into an epoch with no current-epoch owner
activity (a dormant candidate short of its retirement clock, a live
 $\mathsf{CC}$ entry, or the one carry-in freeze of Lemma S6.7);
the vanishing clause of Lemma S6.7(iii)–(iv) therefore
rests on the absence of events, not of presence: on
 $\{n_{v}=0\}$ there is no entry (an entry requires a current-epoch
submission, hence owner demand), no dispute or review opening (each
opens at a latent
demander’s protocol-bearing gate decision — attempted use realizes
the latent indicator, required-approval clause; the opener need not be
a realized log-demander, e.g. at a cache gate’s rejecting branch,
Definitions S4.6(iii), S4.4), no
freeze-bucket arrival, no invitation (a re-entry notification
requires an entry, and a carried-in review issues no new prompts,
Rule R13(vi)), and no publish confirmation. The masses
 $p_{v,t},p_{v},d,d_{\mathrm{tool}}$ are read with this coupling folded
in (the owner’s degenerate indicator contributes its own mass like
any user’s), so every expected bound stated against them is unchanged
in form.
Scope of the exogenous-schedule reading. The reference model’s
expectation-form conclusions (Lemma S6.7(iii),
Theorem S6.9, Corollary S6.10) are asserted under
exogenously scheduled publication: the owner’s schedule is carried in
the demand factor, like the typed stream (A6), not chosen as
a function of protocol outcomes. Adaptive republication reacting to
banked or dispute outcomes is covered elsewhere: by the deterministic
caps (the $E_{\max}$ -rows and the pathwise clause of
Lemma S6.7(iv), which hold for every schedule), and, on
the safety side, by the anchor-keyed counting of
Theorem S5.13 and Corollary S5.27, which
quantifies over adaptive strategies.

• 

Promotion threshold $N$ per tool (weighted: distinct endorsers in
 $[W/w_{\max},W/w_{\min}]$ , changing constants not exponents); panel cap $k$ ; credited endorsements per (entry, tool) are capped at
 $N^{+}=\Theta(N)$ (Definition S4.6(f)).

• 

A confirmation is an accepting gate decision
(convention (C6)); rejection effort is bounded separately
(N18). Accepting decisions fall into three exhaustive
classes. A confirmation of the pair $(v,t)$ is required if the
gate fires for the user’s own use of $v$ at $t$ and the user holds no
local endorsed copy of $v$ whose scope admits $t$ (for the pseudo-target: no local readable $\mathbb{E}$ -level copy of $v$ at all — reasoning coverage, Rule R13(i)).
Required-approval clause (a reference-model coupling). The
reference model is read with attempted use coupled to latent demand:
a user’s own attempted use of $v$ at $t$ within the epoch — any
event firing a gate for it, inside or outside a shared-tier presence,
covered and cache gates included — occurs only on
 $\{D_{u,v,t}=1\}$ ; equivalently, every such event realizes the
user’s latent indicator for the epoch. This is a modeling
stipulation on the demand factor (§S8.1), not a
consequence of independent sampling: it is the natural extension of
the existing convention that a realized shared-tier demand
presupposes attempted use. Every user making a required approval of a pair is
therefore a latent demander of it; the log-defined shared-tier demand
of Definition S4.4 ( $n^{\mathrm{log}}$ ) remains the
subclass realized during a shared-tier presence, with
 $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v,t}\leq n_{v,t}$ pathwise. It is
voluntary if it is recorded at an interpreter-issued invitation
surface (a re-entry notification or suspension prompt,
Definition S4.6, Rule R13(vi)), declinable at
zero required cost; voluntary confirmations exist only through
such surfaces, deduplicated jointly at one invitation per identity per
item per epoch. It is redundant if the gate fires for the
user’s own use while an in-scope local copy is held — possible only
through handle-expressed reuse (Rule R4(c)) or a reasoning-target confirmation of locally readable bytes (reasoning coverage, Rule R13(i)), a
covered gate — in which case the decision is protocol-inert
on both branches (Rule R13(i)): no promotion weight, no
freshness consumption, no dispute initiation, no verdict. Redundant
confirmations are excluded from every count below and disclosed as
attention cost in N18. $C_{u}$ counts user $u$ ’s required and
voluntary shareable confirmations; $\bar{C}_{M}=\frac{1}{M}\sum_{u}C_{u}$ . The
cap (Theorem S6.2) is stated over required confirmations;
the ledger (Lemma S6.7) over required and voluntary.

• 

Epoch vs. lifetime masses. $p_{v,t}$ is a per-epoch
probability. The lifetime mass is
 $d^{\infty}_{\mathrm{tool}}:=\sum_{v,t}p^{\infty}_{v,t}$ with
 $p^{\infty}_{v,t}$ the probability that a user ever demands the
pair; single-epoch and lifetime expected bounds are stated against the
matching mass and never conflated (Theorem S6.2(ii)–(iii)).
Disputes add the ledger rows of Lemma S6.7; revocation
adds Corollary S6.5 (re-verification mechanics:
N16).

• 

Saturation: $V(M):=\mathbb{E}|\{v:n_{v}\geq 1\}|=o(M)$ ; concretely Heaps’
form $V(M)=\Theta(M^{\beta})$ , $\beta\in(0,1)$ Newman (2005).

Assumption A10 (Temporal smoothness, fractional form).

There are a constant $s_{0}\geq 1$ and a front-loading bound
 $\bar{\gamma}=\bar{\gamma}(M)\in(0,1]$ such that, for every pair $(v,t)$ demanded in
the epoch: the number of distinct demanders of $(v,t)$ whose first
demand of the pair falls within any single finalisation window (length
 $T_{\mathrm{win}}$ ) or any single candidate-dormancy interval (an
inter-window span carrying no live community entry, of length
 $\leq T_{\mathrm{exp}}$ by the retirement clock; stable promoted intervals
— a live, unsuspended $\mathsf{CC}$ presence with no open window — are
exempt from both clauses: demand there is fast-pathed or
ledger-residual, never bucketed, Lemma S6.7) is at most
 $\bar{\gamma}\,n_{v}+s_{0}$ ; and likewise the number of distinct identities whose first
audit-recorded decision on the pair $(v,t)$ in the epoch
(§S8.1: display-transcript decisions at non-covered gates,
cache gates on both branches included — the accepting branch
is audit-recorded though never federation-logged,
Definition S4.6(iii)) falls within any single such
window or interval is at most $\bar{\gamma}\,n_{v}+s_{0}$ (the
first-pair-decision clause).
Demander counts are realized shared-tier demand events
( $n^{\mathrm{log}}$ , §S8.1); decider counts are audit-recorded first pair-decisions (a class containing every federation-logged first pair-decision), whose deciders are latent demanders (required-approval clause, §S6.1) but need
not be realized log-demanders. Both are capped against the item’s
latent demand $n_{v}$ — the weaker denominator, since
 $n^{\mathrm{log}}_{v}\leq n_{v}$ and every decider is a latent demander.

Commentary
 (non-normative; reading and validation of A10). The cap is relative to the item’s own demand, never an absolute count:
it forbids front-loading (demand concentrating inside one
window), not popularity. For demand arriving steadily over an epoch of
length $L$ , $\bar{\gamma}\approx T_{\mathrm{win}}/L$ for every item, independently of
 $n_{v}$ and of $M$ ; the additive $s_{0}$ makes the cap satisfiable for
items of tiny demand. A10 is a hindsight property of
completed epochs, used only in the analysis: no protocol rule reads,
estimates, or depends on $\bar{\gamma}$ . Its violation is item-local and
graceful: an item whose demand spikes into one window
( $\gamma_{v}\approx 1$ ) simply fails to amortise: each of its demanders
pays the one required confirmation the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)) already
bounds, while every other item’s accounting
is untouched (S17). Demand-paced panels need no such cap:
panels accumulate verdicts across windows, so A10
constrains promotion finalisation windows and dormant intervals only;
the dormant-interval clause is the stronger half ( $T_{\mathrm{exp}}\gg T_{\mathrm{win}}$ 
typically), kept plausible by the dormancy-retirement clock of
Rule R14(i) and by its restriction to candidate
dormancy: a promoted item’s demand, however concentrated, is exempt
from both clauses (its cost is fast-pathed or ledger-residual,
Lemma S6.7). Validation: per-item realised fractions
 $\gamma_{v}$ , checked specifically on the most-demanded items
(N17). The first-pair-decision clause has the same character and the same
validation path: for demand arriving steadily, first pair-decisions
track first pair-demands; both realised fractions are logged per item
(N17). The audit-recorded class is validated the same
way, directly from display-transcript logs: both branches of every
gate, cache gates included, are recorded there
(Rule R8(e)), so realised first-pair-decision fractions
are observable without reference to the federation log. Its pair-level indexing is load-bearing: it is
what makes the dispute-initiation classification of
Lemma S6.7 exhaustive, since a cross-tool repeat decision
(a prior acceptance at another pair) and a decision postponed past an
abandoned dialog are each the identity’s first decision at
their own pair, counted where they fall. The pair-level form is the item-level cap read tool by tool, still
stated against the item’s total demand $n_{v}$ , the weaker and more
plausible denominator; steady demand gives $\bar{\gamma}\approx T_{\mathrm{win}}/L$ per
pair exactly as per item, and the same per-item logging of realised
fractions $\gamma_{v}$ validates it (N17).

Assumption A11 (Coverage sufficiency, operational form).

Adopt the presence taxonomy and charging convention of
Lemma S6.7: within an epoch, an item’s shared-tier
presence partitions into entry windows, candidate-dormancy intervals,
and freeze/suspension intervals (the ledger-charged pieces),
and stable promoted intervals (a live, unsuspended community
entry with no open entry window). For a pair $(v,t)$ and an epoch, let
 $U_{v,t}$ count the epoch’s residual required pair-approvals of $(v,t)$ : those charged to no ledger row (option-induced cache confirmations excluded: floor-charged, Corollary S6.6) — a
subclass of the required confirmations by demanders whose epoch-first
demand of the item falls in a stable promoted interval or outside
every shared-tier presence (a credited such decision is
weight-row-charged instead, Lemma S6.7, only shrinking
the residual) (before first publication, after
retirement, or after demotion; gates fired during a suspension are
freeze-bucket events, ledger-charged, never residual). Each belongs
to a distinct demander of the pair (INV-3), so
 $U_{v,t}\leq n_{v,t}$ pathwise. The residual class is
operational, keyed to gate behaviour, never to certificate
existence: a live $\mathsf{CC}$ entry naming $(v,t)$ fast-paths only
literal-expressed use (Rule R4(a)); a
handle-expressed use of the same pair gates at every tool
(Rule R4(c)) and, absent a local in-scope copy, is
required — and, in a stable promoted interval, residual. There are
 $D_{0}(M)\geq 0$ and $\varepsilon(M)\to 0$ such that, for every
benign (Definition S4.10) shareable item $v\in\mathcal{V}$ ,
every target $t$ , and every $n\geq 1$ , conditional on $n_{v,t}=n$ :

 $\mathbb{E}\big[U_{v,t}\mid n_{v,t}=n\big]\ \leq\ D_{0}(M)+\varepsilon(M)\,n.$ 

Like A10, this is a hindsight property of completed epochs,
read against latent demand (§S8.1); no protocol rule reads,
estimates, or depends on it, and it is trivially satisfied whenever
 $n\leq D_{0}(M)$ (since $U_{v,t}\leq n_{v,t}$ ).
The growth of $D_{0}$ is consumed only through the product
condition $D_{0}(M)\,V(M)=o(M)$ of Theorem S6.9(iii), a joint
property of the coverage threshold and the saturation law, never of
either alone; under the Heaps form $V(M)=\Theta(M^{\beta})$ 
(§S6.1), the sufficient rate is
 $D_{0}(M)=o(M^{1-\beta})$ , the form used in the corridor
(§S6.5) and in the sufficiency sketch below.
It bounds jointly:
(i) publication coverage — a demanded benign item its owner
never publishes has every gate-firing accepting demand residual, and
satisfies the bound only if that demand is small;
(ii) promotion coverage — demand at not-yet-promoted pairs
arriving outside every ledger-charged piece; and
(iii) representation coverage — demand for live- $\mathsf{CC}$ pairs
arriving handle-expressed (untrusted-channel arrival of
already-certified bytes), which gates and pays despite the
certificate (S18). An item or arrival pattern
violating it never amortises: each residual demander pays the one
required confirmation the pathwise cap (Theorem S6.2(i)) already bounds,
item-locally and gracefully, entering
the residual $r^{(e)}_{\mathrm{bad}}$ of Corollary S6.10.

Heuristic sufficiency and compatibility.

The following sufficiency sketch is heuristic — a plausibility
argument, not a derivation: it does not formally compose intervening freezes, wrongful evictions,
the entry cap, the pool-composition requirements, or the
arrival-representation mix into a proof of A11, which remains an
assumption; the compatibility calculation closing the paragraph is
exact. Heuristically sufficient, for a benign item and any fixed
 $\eta>0$ : the owner publishes the item before more than an
 $\varepsilon$ -fraction of each demanded pair’s demand arrives and
resubmits promptly after each unpromoted window (dormant intervals
short of the retirement clock, within the per-epoch entry cap of
Rule R14(i)); some entry, counting the banked credits of
its preceding interval (Definition S4.6(b)), accrues at
least $(1+\eta)N/p_{b}$ fresh byte-first demanders (a positive byte-first fraction per demanded tool — enough
demanders whose first decision on the bytes occurs at that tool —
is one empirical mechanism supporting this cohort’s existence; it is
a heuristic diagnostic for A11, not a hypothesis of any
theorem) whose demand spans at
least $D_{\mathrm{pool}}$ pools with at least $\lceil N/D_{\mathrm{pool}}\rceil$ accepting
demanders available per pool (so the per-pool credit caps of
Definition S4.6(f) do not bind below $N$ ), almost all of
them authority-active (Definition S4.6(a): honest
identities are authority-inactive only in the rare calibrated states
of Remark S5.5, folded into the same $\varepsilon$ ) — a
demand-composition property of the same character as
A9’s structural half, validated by the same
pool-stratified logging (N17); and demotion of promoted
benign entries is exponentially rare (Proposition S5.20), so promoted status, once reached,
persists; and, for the representation clause, demand for promoted
pairs predominantly arrives fast-path-expressed — through trusted
retrieval of the live entry (Definition S4.1,
Rule R14(ii)) or reuse of local readable copies — with
the handle-arrival fraction (S18) folded into the
same $\varepsilon$ : an arrival-channel property of the demand stream,
logged directly (N17), never derived from the protocol. For schedules with
 $N(M)\to\infty$ — every corridor schedule, §S6.5 —
the accepting count then crosses $N$ except with probability
 $e^{-2\eta^{\prime 2}N(M)/(1+\eta)^{2}}\to 0$ by Hoeffding’s lower tail, for a
margin $\eta^{\prime}$ depending on $\eta$ and $p_{b}$ , giving
 $\varepsilon(M)\to 0$ ; at fixed $N$ the same cohort yields only
a fixed failure probability, so $\varepsilon(M)\to 0$ under fixed
protocol constants requires instead that available cohorts grow
beyond a fixed multiple of $N$ , which A11 then asserts
rather than derives; intervening disputes are absorbed by the
vindication exemption of Rule R14(i)(b)
(N15) and resolve at the item’s own demand rate,
freezes excluding themselves from the window clock
(Rule R13(iv)). Consistency with A10:
under the fractional cap the two assumptions do not constrain one
quantity from opposite sides. A crossing window is permitted to
