coupled case, and no result of this document distinguishes them.

Commentary
 (non-normative; epistemic status of the blast law). Three things this law is not. It is not a safety claim: the safety
statement is Theorem S5.13, which assumes no independence and
quantifies over all adaptive schedules, and this law’s exponential decay
would not survive an adversary that stops at the first rejection — such
an adversary is confined to the ordinary path and is measured separately.
It is not a tightness claim (Remark S8.14). And it is
not an assumption of anything: no rule reads it, no proof cites it, and
the protocol’s behaviour is identical whether or not it holds; it is
accordingly absent from the assumptions ledger of
§S8.6.

What it is, is the instrument that makes the simulator falsifiable. Every
one of its five consequences is a prediction the implementation could
violate, three of them at the level of support rather than of a
tail probability — the structural zero, the $k$ -independence, and the
 $m_{\mathrm{rej}}$ -independence below the entry reach are all
byte-identity properties that cannot be passed by luck. Its own derivation
is verified against two independent oracles: direct enumeration of every
verdict sequence at small $(m,m_{\mathrm{rej}})$ , applying the decision
rule of Step 4 without reference to the closed form; and an uncollapsed
exact-rational sum over the rejecter’s position, so that an algebra error
in Step 6 cannot cancel.

These checks share an interpretive premise: Step 4 is a reading
of Rule R13, and the enumeration oracle shares that reading
with the claim it checks, so the two are not independent on that point.
Unlike the single-user core (§S9), none of
this is machine-checked. An implementation that disagreed with the law
would be either a simulator defect or a misreading of
Appendix S4, and the law’s role is to make the difference
cheap to find.

## S8.3 Explicit ledger constants

Lemma S8.18 (Explicit forms of $A(M)$ and $B_{\mathrm{fl}}$ ).

Summing the rows of Table S2 under the charging
convention of Lemma S6.7, with $\bar{D}_{v}$ as there and
 $\kappa^{\prime}:=\kappa+c_{\mathrm{p}}k$ :

 $\mathbb{E}[C^{\mathrm{pipe}}_{v}]\ \leq\ A(M)\,\mathbb{P}[n_{v}\geq 1]\;+\;B_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\bar{\gamma}\,\mathbb{E}[n_{v}],$ 

with

 $\displaystyle A(M)$ 
 $\displaystyle:=2E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}\big(N^{+}+1+s_{0}\big)$ 

 $\displaystyle+c_{\mathrm{tool}}\big(2(1-p_{b})E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}s_{0}+(1-p_{b})E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+c_{\mathrm{acc}}f+2E_{\max}+1\big)\,\kappa^{\prime},$ 

 $B_{\mathrm{fl}}\ :=\ 2\,E_{\star}\,c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}\Big[1+(1-p_{b})\,\kappa^{\prime}\Big],$ 

where $\kappa^{\prime}$ folds at most $c_{\mathrm{p}}k$ 
invited re-inspections per fast-track review (Rule R13(vi)) into
the freeze-bucket multiplier; the suspension-prompt row is majorised by the $c_{\mathrm{p}}k$ 
component of the freeze-bucket term
( $E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{p}}k\leq c_{\mathrm{tool}}\,\bar{D}_{v}\,c_{\mathrm{p}}k$ , since $c_{\mathrm{tool}}\geq 1$ and
 $\bar{D}_{v}\geq 2E_{\max}+1$ ), a deliberate slackening: the prompt row is
charged once in the ledger and bounded once here, by a dominating
expression. Using $1\leq D_{\mathrm{pool}}\leq N$ (Definition S4.3),
 $N\leq N^{+}<N+D_{\mathrm{pool}}\leq 2N$ and $E_{\star}\leq 2E_{\max}$ , these satisfy
 $A(M)=\mathcal{O}\!\big(E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}(N+c_{\mathrm{acc}}f)(1+\kappa)\big)$ and
 $B_{\mathrm{fl}}=\mathcal{O}\!\big(E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}(1+(1-p_{b})\kappa)\big)$ . The forms
are closed-form upper bounds, exact in structure and
monotone-slackened in constants; they are not claimed tight.

Proof.

Row sums under the charging convention of first item-arrival attribution,
splitting each $(\bar{\gamma}n_{v}+s_{0})$ term into its $\bar{\gamma}n_{v}$ part
(collected into $B_{\mathrm{fl}}$ ) and its $s_{0}$ part (collected into $A(M)$ ):

 $\underbrace{E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}}_{\text{weight}}+\underbrace{E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}(\bar{\gamma}\,n_{v}+s_{0})}_{\text{window bucket}}+\underbrace{E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}(\bar{\gamma}\,n_{v}+s_{0})}_{\text{dormancy bucket}}\\
+\underbrace{c_{\mathrm{tool}}\,\bar{D}_{v}\,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\text{freeze bucket (prompts
folded in)}}+\underbrace{E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}}_{\text{repeat}}+\underbrace{E_{\max}}_{\text{publish}}.$ 

The former reversal accounting appears in no separate term: a prior
rejecter’s later acceptances are charged to that demander’s bucket inside
the per-demander $\times c_{\mathrm{tool}}$ factors of the three bucket rows
(Lemma S6.7, charging convention), so no $\bar{D}_{v}(1+\kappa)$ 
reversal term arises. The suspension-prompt row is majorised by, not identical to, the
 $c_{\mathrm{p}}k$ component of the freeze-bucket term ( $c_{\mathrm{tool}}\geq 1$ ,
 $\bar{D}_{v}\geq 2E_{\max}+1$ ), so its events are charged once (to their row)
and bounded once (by the dominating freeze expression). Collecting the
coefficient of $\bar{\gamma}n_{v}$ : $2E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}$ from the window and
dormancy buckets, plus $2(1-p_{b})E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}\kappa^{\prime}$ from the freeze
bucket through $\bar{D}_{v}$ ’s first term, giving $B_{\mathrm{fl}}$ . Collecting the
demand-free remainder:
 $2E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+E_{\max}+2E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}s_{0}+c_{\mathrm{tool}}\kappa^{\prime}\big(2(1-p_{b})E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}s_{0}+(1-p_{b})E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+c_{\mathrm{acc}}f+2E_{\max}+1\big)\leq A(M)$ , using
 $2E_{\max}c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+E_{\max}+2E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}s_{0}\leq 2E_{\star}c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}(N^{+}+1+s_{0})$ ( $E_{\max}\leq E_{\star}$ , $c_{\mathrm{tool}}\geq 1$ , A12;
 $E_{\star}\geq 1$ , convention (C8)). Taking expectations over the demand, with
the indicator carried per Lemma S6.7(iii) (every row’s
pathwise count vanishes on $\{n_{v}=0\}$ ), gives the claim; the
 $\mathcal{O}$ -forms follow since $c_{\mathrm{p}}k=\mathcal{O}(\kappa)$ for $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ bounded
below, $\kappa=(k{+}1)/\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}\geq k$ , and $E_{\star}=E_{\max}+1\leq 2E_{\max}$ ( $E_{\max}\geq 1$ , convention (C8)).
∎

Remark S8.19 (Reading of the ledger constants).

(i) The bracketed
 $\big((1-p_{b})c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+c_{\mathrm{acc}}f\big)\kappa^{\prime}$ term is the dispute
cross-term: honest reconsideration capacity and adversarial dispute
capacity multiplied by panel cost, with suspension-prompt re-inspections
folded into $\kappa^{\prime}$ inside the freeze term, the prompt row majorised by the freeze term rather than independently summed; it is what forces
the cross-term condition $N(M)k(M)=o(M^{1-\beta})$ of
Theorem S6.13. (ii) The $\kappa^{\prime}$ -part of $B_{\mathrm{fl}}$ is damped by
 $1-p_{b}$ , the honest wrongful-rejection rate: the front-loaded freeze load
scales with honest error, not with demand alone. (iii) The front-loading
coefficient $B_{\mathrm{fl}}$ multiplies only the fraction $\bar{\gamma}$ of the item’s own
demand: item popularity enters the per-item cost solely through
 $\bar{\gamma}\,n_{v}$ and cancels against the population in
Lemma S6.8. (iv) The $c_{\mathrm{tool}}^{2}$ in both constants is the
pair-level discipline made explicit: one factor counts the pairs over
which a bucket’s demanders are enumerated (A10’s demand clause,
read per pair), the other the pairs each demander may confirm
(INV-3); both are $\mathcal{O}(1)$ (A12), so no
asymptotic statement changes.

## S8.4 Knowledge-based formulation of Reasoning Integrity

Theorem S3.25 is trace-wise per coin sequence. It induces
the following statement in the knowledge-based style of gradual release
Askarov & Sabelfeld (2007), recorded to dispel the classical worry that
conditioning on release events can itself be secret-dependent.

Proposition S8.20 (Gradual release).

Fix the strategies $P$ and $R_{\mathcal{E}}$ , the slot skeleton, a coin sequence
 $\omega$ , and a typed stream (A6; in the federated setting
also a federation log, A8). By Theorem S3.25,
the entire behaviour of a run of horizon $n$ from a sorted
configuration (its Protected-LLM operations, tool invocations, reply
stream, and ungated sink events) is a function
 $F(c_{0}^{\approx},\mathsf{DT},\omega,n)$ of the initial matched-configuration
class (Definition S3.6), the run’s display transcript
(the run suffix of Definition S3.8), and the coins,
with no residual dependence on $\mathcal{M}_{\mathsf{U}}$ . Consequently, for any
observer who knows these fixed inputs and the initial matched class,
and any prior over untrusted-store contents consistent with that
class, the posterior after observing the run to horizon $n$ equals the
prior conditioned on consistency with $\mathsf{DT}$ alone: knowledge about
untrusted memory increases only at display events, never through
undisplayed behaviour.

Proof.

By Theorem S3.25, any two untrusted-store contents
consistent with the same $\mathsf{DT}$ (within the fixed initial matched
class, matched coins, coupled typed stream and slot skeleton,
conventions (C1)/(C9)) yield identical
behaviour, so the likelihood of the observed behaviour is constant
across such contents; Bayes’ rule then collapses the posterior to the
prior restricted to $\mathsf{DT}$ -consistency.
∎

The observer here is granted $\omega$ and the typed stream,
strictly more than any real attacker knows. The statement is claimed
for this strong observer only: for an observer ignorant of $\omega$ ,
conditioning on the transcript can correlate the hidden store with the
coins, and behaviour may then reveal information about $\omega$ that,
jointly with the transcript, reveals more about untrusted memory than
the transcript alone; marginalising over $\omega$ therefore does not
yield the weaker-observer statement in general. Deployments wanting the
weaker-observer form may include the public coin outcomes in the
release history, whereupon this proposition applies verbatim with
 $\omega$ read as released.

## S8.5 Zipf case study for the budget

This subsection instantiates the saturation cost bound
(Theorem S6.9) under a Zipf
rank-popularity law, as a worked example;
Theorem S6.9 needs only sublinear distinct-count growth (with
A10). We caution that empirical rank exponents for human
reference streams are frequently at or below one Breslau et al. (1999), and
the rank/Heaps link has finite-size deviations Lü et al. (2010); the
clean rate below holds in the top-heavy regime $\alpha>1$ . Throughout,
the demand-independent per-item cap is $\hat{N}:=A(M)$ with $A(M)$ as
in Lemma S8.18; the front-loaded part
 $B_{\mathrm{fl}}\bar{\gamma}\,n_{v}$ of the ledger is carried separately (after summation
it contributes at most $B_{\mathrm{fl}}\bar{\gamma}\,d$ per user, uniformly over the
popularity law), so the head/tail analysis concerns the $\hat{N}$ part,
with the $B_{\mathrm{fl}}\bar{\gamma}\,d$ term added at the end.
The demand-independent invitation cap $K=E_{\max}(c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+c_{\mathrm{p}}k+1)$ 
of Lemma S6.7(iv) is likewise carried separately, as
 $K\,V(M)/M$ (Lemma S8.21); it plays no role in the
head/tail split. When $\hat{N}$ grows
with $M$ (through $N(M)$ , $\kappa(M)$ , and $f(M)$ under co-scaling), the head
term below becomes
 $\hat{N}^{\,1-1/\alpha}Z^{-1/\alpha}M^{-(1-1/\alpha)}$ , which still
vanishes whenever $A(M)=o(M)$ , amply covered by
 $A(M)=o(M^{1-\beta})$ .

Lemma S8.21 (Head/tail decomposition).

With head $H_{M}=\{v:Mp_{v}\geq\hat{N}\}$ , tail $L_{M}=\mathcal{V}\setminus H_{M}$ ,
and $K=E_{\max}(c_{\mathrm{tool}}N^{+}+c_{\mathrm{p}}k+1)$ as in
Lemma S6.7(iv), and with $\mathbb{E}[\bar{C}_{M}]$ read over the
pipeline component (Lemma S6.7; the ledger residual
adds the separately bounded term of Theorem S6.9(iii) on
top, uniformly over the popularity law, vanishing under that
theorem’s additional rate conditions),

 $\mathbb{E}[\bar{C}_{M}]\ \leq\ \frac{\hat{N}\,|H_{M}|}{M}+c_{\mathrm{tool}}\,\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}(M)+K\,\frac{V(M)}{M}+B_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\bar{\gamma}\,d.$ 

Proof.

Head items are bounded by clause (iii) alone
( $\mathbb{E}[C_{v}]\leq\hat{N}+B_{\mathrm{fl}}\bar{\gamma}\,\mathbb{E}[n_{v}]$ ); tail items by clause (iv)
in expectation ( $\mathbb{E}[C_{v}]\leq c_{\mathrm{tool}}\,Mp_{v}+K\,\mathbb{P}[n_{v}\geq 1]$ , since
 $\mathbb{E}[n_{v}]=Mp_{v}$ ); summing the head term and
the front-loaded parts over all items gives the first and last terms
after division by $M$ ; the tail items’ $K$ -terms are further enlarged
to the all-items sum $K\,V(M)$ , giving the third term. The quantity
 $\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}(M)$ is the expected number of distinct tail items
a single user demands: sub-threshold demand not yet amortised.
∎

Theorem S8.22 (Zipf decay, head and tail).

Under $p_{r}=r^{-\alpha}/Z$ (read as the homogeneous special case $p_{u,v,t}\equiv p_{v,t}$ of the reference model), $Z=\sum_{j=1}^{|\mathcal{V}|}j^{-\alpha}$ . Clauses (i)–(iii) concern
 $\alpha>1$ , where $Z\leq\zeta(\alpha)$ :

(i) 

rank $r$ is in the head iff $r\leq R_{M}:=(M/(\hat{N}Z))^{1/\alpha}$ ,
so $|H_{M}|=\min\{\lfloor R_{M}\rfloor,U\}\leq R_{M}$ and
 $\hat{N}|H_{M}|/M\leq Z^{-1/\alpha}\,(\hat{N}/M)^{1-1/\alpha}$ ;

(ii) 

let $U:=|\mathcal{V}|$ and $m_{\star}:=\lfloor R_{M}\rfloor+1$ ; for the
finite universe,
 $\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}(M)=\tfrac{1}{Z}\sum_{m_{\star}\leq r\leq U}r^{-\alpha}$ ,
empty (and zero) if $m_{\star}>U$ . For $m_{\star}\leq U$ , the floor-aware
two-sided comparison
 $\int_{m_{\star}}^{U+1}x^{-\alpha}\,dx\ \leq\ \sum_{r=m_{\star}}^{U}r^{-\alpha}\ \leq\ m_{\star}^{-\alpha}+\int_{m_{\star}}^{U}x^{-\alpha}\,dx$ 
gives

 $\frac{m_{\star}^{1-\alpha}-(U{+}1)^{1-\alpha}}{Z(\alpha-1)}\ \leq\ \mu_{\mathrm{tail}}(M)\ \leq\ \frac{1}{Z}\Big(m_{\star}^{-\alpha}+\frac{m_{\star}^{1-\alpha}-U^{1-\alpha}}{\alpha-1}\Big)\ \leq\ \frac{1}{Z}\Big(R_{M}^{-\alpha}+\frac{R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha}}{\alpha-1}\Big),$ 

using $m_{\star}>R_{M}$ and $\alpha>1$ ; since
 $R_{M}^{-\alpha}\leq R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha}$ for $R_{M}\geq 1$ , and
 $\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}\leq d=1$ always (absorbing $R_{M}<1$ ),
 $\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}(M)=\mathcal{O}\big((\hat{N}Z/M)^{1-1/\alpha}\big)$ 
uniformly; the matching lower order
 $\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}(M)=\Theta\big((\hat{N}Z/M)^{1-1/\alpha}\big)$ 
holds under the two additional (sufficient) non-saturation
conditions $R_{M}\to\infty$ (equivalently $\hat{N}Z/M\to 0$ ) and
 $U/R_{M}\to\infty$ : then $m_{\star}/R_{M}\to 1$ , so
 $m_{\star}^{1-\alpha}=(1+o(1))\,R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha}$ , and the
 $m_{\star}^{-\alpha}$ term and the $U$ -corrections are
 $o(R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha})$ . Neither condition may be omitted from this
sufficient criterion in
general: along a sequence with $R_{M}\to\infty$ , taking $U=m_{\star}$ 
leaves a single tail term of order
 $R_{M}^{-\alpha}=o(R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha})$ , so the matching order fails
though $R_{M}\to\infty$ holds; while $R_{M}\to 0$ (a per-item cap
outgrowing the population, e.g. $U=Z=1$ , $\hat{N}=M^{2}$ ) leaves
tail mass $\Theta(1)$ against a diverging comparison scale
 $(\hat{N}Z/M)^{1-1/\alpha}$ , though $U/R_{M}\to\infty$ holds. For an infinite universe,
 $U/R_{M}\to\infty$ is automatic while $R_{M}\to\infty$ remains an
additional requirement; $R_{M}\to\infty$ holds in the
fixed-adversary, fixed-constants regime of
Theorem S6.9(ii) and in the corridor of
Theorem S6.13 (where $\hat{N}=A(M)=o(M^{1-\beta})$ ),
while $U/R_{M}\to\infty$ remains a separate universe non-saturation
condition in either regime. Part (iii) consumes only the uniform
upper bound and requires neither condition;

(iii) 

hence
 $\mathbb{E}[\bar{C}_{M}]=\mathcal{O}\big((A(M)/M)^{1-1/\alpha}\big)+\mathcal{O}\big(K(M)\,V(M)/M\big)+B_{\mathrm{fl}}\,\bar{\gamma}\,d$ ,
plus the residual of Theorem S6.9(iii), the head and tail
terms decaying at the common rate $\mathcal{O}((A(M)/M)^{1-1/\alpha})$ (as
upper bounds) and the invitation term $K\,V(M)/M$ vanishing whenever
 $K(M)\,V(M)=o(M)$ — under fixed protocol constants with $f(M)=\mathcal{O}(1)$ and $\rho_{\mathrm{seat}}$ bounded below
( $A(M),K(M)=\Theta(1)$ ; the hypotheses of Theorem S6.9(ii)) automatic, giving the clean rate
 $\mathcal{O}(M^{-(1-1/\alpha)})+\mathcal{O}(V(M)/M)$ ; under co-scaling,
 $K(M)=\mathcal{O}(E_{\max}(c_{\mathrm{tool}}N+c_{\mathrm{p}}k))=o(M^{1-\beta})$ by the corridor
schedules, so $K\,V(M)/M=o(1)$ exactly as for $A(M)$ . The
front-loading term survives exactly as in Theorem S6.9,
vanishing under $\bar{\gamma}(M)(1+\kappa(M))\to 0$ (A13 is automatic here: $d=\sum_{r}p_{r}=1$ ).

For the boundary case $\alpha=1$ , read along finite-universe
sequences $U:=|\mathcal{V}|\to\infty$ , with
 $Z:=H_{U}=\sum_{r=1}^{U}r^{-1}=\ln U+\mathcal{O}(1)$ and
 $R_{M}:=M/(\hat{N}Z)$ : in the nonempty-head regime $1\leq R_{M}<U$ 
(below universe saturation), $R_{M}\sim M/(\hat{N}\ln U)$ and the head
term $\hat{N}|H_{M}|/M=\Theta(1/\ln U)$ — the $\hat{N}=A(M)$ 
dependence cancels; for $R_{M}<1$ the head is empty and the head term
zero — while after universe saturation ( $R_{M}\geq U$ ) the head term
equals $\hat{N}\,U/M$ . For $1\ll R_{M}<U$ , the tail sum
 $\sum_{R_{M}<r\leq U}r^{-1}/Z=\big(\ln(U/R_{M})+\mathcal{O}(1)\big)/\big(\ln U+\mathcal{O}(1)\big)$ ,
governed by how far the head boundary sits below the universe; (floor corrections at the boundary rank are absorbed into the $\mathcal{O}(1)$ ; the tail is empty for $m_{\star}>U$ ); no
power rate is asserted without an explicit relation between $U$ and
 $R_{M}$ , decay being controlled by saturation rather
than the exponent, which is why the main development is stated for
sublinear distinct-count growth (Remark S8.23).

Proof.

(i) $Mp_{r}\geq\hat{N}\iff r\leq R_{M}$ , and
 $|H_{M}|=\min\{\lfloor R_{M}\rfloor,U\}\leq R_{M}=\mathcal{O}((M/\hat{N})^{1/\alpha})$ ; $Z$ is bounded for
 $\alpha>1$ . (ii) with $m_{\star}=\lfloor R_{M}\rfloor+1$ : if $m_{\star}>U$ the tail is
empty and every bound holds trivially; otherwise apply the
floor-aware comparison as displayed (the lower bound from
 $r^{-\alpha}\geq\int_{r}^{r+1}x^{-\alpha}\,dx$ termwise, the upper from
splitting off the first term and bounding the rest by
 $\int_{m_{\star}}^{U}x^{-\alpha}\,dx$ ); dropping the nonnegative
 $U$ -terms and using $m_{\star}>R_{M}$ (so $m_{\star}^{-\alpha}<R_{M}^{-\alpha}$ 
and $m_{\star}^{1-\alpha}<R_{M}^{1-\alpha}$ ) yields the uniform upper
bound, with the trivial guard $\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}\leq d=1$ absorbing
 $R_{M}<1$ ; under $R_{M}\to\infty$ and $U/R_{M}\to\infty$ , $m_{\star}/R_{M}\to 1$ , so the
floor correction is negligible, the $U$ -corrections and the
 $m_{\star}^{-\alpha}$ term are $o(R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha})$ , and both ends
are $\Theta(R_{M}^{\,1-\alpha})$ , giving the matching order; substitute
 $R_{M}=(M/(\hat{N}Z))^{1/\alpha}$ . (iii) sum in
Lemma S8.21; the tail term
 $c_{\mathrm{tool}}\,\mu_{\mathrm{tail}}$ absorbs the old constant into
 $\mathcal{O}(\cdot)$ , and the invitation term is
 $K\,V(M)/M$ verbatim. The $\alpha=1$ clauses follow, along $U\to\infty$ , from
 $R_{M}=M/(\hat{N}\ln U)(1+o(1))$ read in the regime $R_{M}\geq 1$ (where
 $\lfloor R_{M}\rfloor\geq R_{M}/2$ ; the head is empty for $R_{M}<1$ ),
cancellation of $\hat{N}$ in $\hat{N}R_{M}/M$ , the exact identity
 $|H_{M}|=U$ when $R_{M}\geq U$ (where the tail is empty), and,
separately in the nonsaturated regime $1\ll R_{M}<U$ , the
harmonic-tail estimate
 $\sum_{R_{M}<r\leq U}r^{-1}=\ln(U/R_{M})+\mathcal{O}(1)$ Breslau et al. (1999).
∎

Remark S8.23.

The $\alpha=1$ boundary case is why the main development is stated in
terms of sublinear distinct-count growth
(Theorem S6.9), which holds across $\alpha$ : the Zipf form
sharpens the rate in the top-heavy regime $\alpha>1$ but is used
nowhere in any proof.

## S8.6 Assumptions ledger

Assum.

What it buys

Conditions and
caveats

A1

Labels attacker-immutable; injected content stays
 $\mathsf{U}$ .

Authenticated command channel (TCB,
§S2.5).

A2

Trust changes only by deterministic rules and
endorsement.

Storage and log integrity of $\mathcal{M}_{\mathbb{E}}$ (TCB).

A3

Faithful decision pane; TCB-authenticated attested
zone; marked untrusted strings.

Zoned rendering path (TCB,
R8). The payload of a narrative confirmation is
attacker-influenced text; that is A5’s subject.

A4

A confirmed atomic value is the intended value for that
tool.

User-as-oracle axiom; defensible because the set is small,
faithfully displayed, and shrinking (Appendix S6);
weakest at the batch gate (S12).

A5

A confirmed summary was checked against citations.

Stronger than A4; mitigated by community verification;
residual S10, incl. publisher penalty on
honest-but-deceived publication.

A6

Licenses the two-run comparison and the informal reading
of Cor. S3.26.

Human-channel idealization; practical
content discharged by logging both surfaces (N5,
N7, N8).

A7

Community endorsement at a tool speaks for any user
at that exact tool.

Only the semantic half (context-independence)
assumed; type-level half proved (Lem. S5.2); canonical
identifiers TCB.

A8

Licenses federated Reasoning Integrity

Individual-usage

R12–R14 are harness properties; validation
(N17) provides falsification and calibration evidence for
the uniform behavioural bounds, not certification, since sparse or

 $V(M)=o(M)$ ; inspector competence $q>0$ (safety) and $q+p_{b}>1$ (joint
safety and resolution correctness), $q$ read per Remark S5.19. The co-scaling
corridor additionally requires the cross-term condition
 $N(M)k(M)=o(M^{1-\beta})$ and the shrinking front-loading condition
 $\bar{\gamma}(M)k(M)\to 0$ (§S6.5).

## Appendix S9 Machine-Checked Verification in Lean 4

We mechanize the single-user core of PerMIT in Lean 4 (toolchain leanprover/lean4:v4.31.0; Mathlib v4.31.0, revision fabf563a). The development defines an executable interpreter for the memory interface and proves Theorems S3.21 and S3.25 over it for arbitrary Protected-LLM strategies, retrievers, attackers, and horizons, within the deployment, input, and starting-state conditions stated below. Building the development is the verification: Lean’s kernel checks every theorem, and every executable scenario is a build-time assertion.

Scope.

The mechanized surface is the single-user interface without run-level batched iteration: tool invocation, $\mathsf{write}_{P}$ , $\mathsf{recall}$ , $\mathsf{fill}$ , $\mathsf{crystallize}$ , $\mathsf{confirm}$ (tool and reasoning targets), $\mathsf{retrieve}$ , $\mathsf{display}$ , $\mathsf{evict}$ , and the typed and paste channels. The batch-confirmation surface of Rule R9 is mechanized as a standalone layer. Paper arguments alone cover the integration of $\mathsf{map}$ into the run-level interpreter, durable endorsement on aborted invocations (delta 6), arbitrary mid-run matched pairs, the index-oracle reduction (delta 2), Options O1–O2 beyond the gate level and Option O3, and the federated tier with every probabilistic and budget result.

Model.

The interpreter execOp is a total function with one arm per operation, so every invocation has defined behavior (Rule R10(b)). Its state IState represents the extended configuration of Definition S2.6, subject to the modeling deltas below; the display transcript, sensitive-sink log, and operation log are ghost state. Four modeling choices carry the proofs.

• 

Replies. The inductive type Reply (acknowledgment, handle, failure, decision, bytes) is the reply grammar of Rule R10; no other reply can be constructed. Bytes are returned only by $\mathsf{recall}$ of a non- $\mathsf{U}$ block and by trusted retrieval.

• 

Two-sorted identifiers. Endorsed-sort identifiers (BlockId.e) are allocated only by coupled events: typed input, the agent’s writes, and accepted confirmations. Untrusted-sort identifiers (BlockId.u) are allocated by attacker-dependent events: the initial untrusted store, paste, tool-output capture, and crystallization. Every endorsement writes a fresh copy at the next endorsed-sort identifier.

• 

Views. The Protected-LLM view Obs is computed from the endorsed store alone (memEOf), with the decision history, typed input, and handle count; the retriever likewise receives only the endorsed store.

• 

Actors. The Protected LLM is an arbitrary function of its emitted operations, reply stream, view, and coins (PStrat). The attacker supplies the initial untrusted store, every tool output (count and content), every paste event, and the Quarantined Reader’s candidates and summaries. The user supplies the typed stream and the decision and selection at each confirmation.

Checked statements.

Table S5 gives the correspondence. Action Integrity is a projection of an inductive invariant, Inv, established initially and preserved by every transition. It is proved from the empty endorsed store and from any state satisfying Inv. A precedence corollary places each endorsing confirmation in the transcript that exists when the sink event is recorded.

Reasoning Integrity is a lockstep simulation over MatchW, which equates everything the Protected LLM can observe and everything the transcript records, while leaving untrusted stores and hidden handle resolutions free. It is proved from empty endorsed stores and from any shared endorsed seed with independently chosen untrusted stores. Its conclusions exceed what Theorem S3.25 requires: equal transcripts force equal raw sink logs, operation sequences, reply streams, and final views.

Proposition S3.27 is witnessed by computation. A taint-gating baseline, with data taint carried inside the model, yields equal (empty) transcripts but different ungated sink projections. PerMIT’s interpreter under the analogous strategy is attack-invariant.

Eight scenarios check, by computation:

• 

the typed fast path;

• 

a blocked injection;

• 

endorsement followed by fast-path reuse;

• 

occupancy hiding;

• 

a finite noninterference instance;

• 

attack-surface confinement;

• 

ungated data-tool flow of agent-composed bytes;

• 

the post-endorsement boundary, whose closure under Option O2 is witnessed in Hardening.lean.

Sanity

Trusted base.

Lean’s kernel checks every theorem, and the build fails on any failed proof or false #guard. An omitted proof (sorry) produces only a warning, so completeness rests on the #print axioms report, reproduced verbatim in the artifact’s README from a clean build. It shows that the top-level theorems, and hence every lemma they use, depend only on propext and Quot.sound, and none on sorryAx.
The #guard scenarios additionally trust Lean’s evaluator. The correspondence between the mechanized model and the paper’s semantics is not machine-checked; Table S5 and the deltas below argue it.

Modeling deltas.

None is silent. Deltas 1 and 3 strengthen the checked statements.

(1) 

Reader. The Quarantined Reader is folded into the attacker, which chooses the reader’s candidates and summaries directly.

(2) 

Index oracles. User decisions and selections, and attacker outputs, are functions of event ordinals. Any run of a reactive user or attacker is the run of the oracle equal to its realized sequence. In Theorem S3.25, both runs share the user oracle, and transcript equality pins every decision a display-reactive user makes (Supplement, Proposition S3.29; a paper argument).

(3) 

Channels. Compared runs share only the slot skeleton and the typed stream; each attacker chooses paste presence and content independently.

(4) 

Starting states. Theorem S3.21 is proved from the empty endorsed store and from any state satisfying Inv. Theorem S3.25 is proved from empty or shared endorsed seeds, the canonical matched class of Definition S3.5. Arbitrary mid-run matched pairs are covered by the paper proof.

(5) 

Collection handles. Without run-level $\mathsf{map}$ , a handle to a multi-element capture is confirmed at a direct call by selecting one displayed element, so at most one displayed value flows per accepting decision.

(6) 

Aborted invocations.
The mechanized interpreter writes endorsed copies only when an invocation executes. Rule R2 also writes durable copies for the accepted positions of an aborted invocation; that case is covered by the paper proof only.

(7) 

Decision history.
The mechanized history also records the selection index and marks null-resolution aborts, so the mechanized view extends the paper’s $h$ . The operation semantics do not inspect these extra fields; both are transcript-recorded and therefore agree in the matched executions used by the proof.

(8) 

Selections. An out-of-range selection clamps to the first candidate; the flowed bytes remain the displayed bytes.

(9) 

Representation. One typed event is one $\mathsf{T}$ -block; bytes are lists of naturals; provenance marks are subsumed by labels and ghost logs. Inv checks endorsement against the transcript, without the sanitized-history record of Definition S3.3(ii).

(10) 

Outside the interpreter. Options O1–O2 are checked at the gate level only. The covered-gate and reasoning-coverage clauses of Rule R13(i) are federated accounting conventions, not transitions.

Specification corrections.

Mechanization corrected the specification in three places, each adopted in the paper.

• 

Endorsement must write a fresh copy at an operation-determined identifier. Relabeling the untrusted source in place would expose an attacker-dependent identifier in the endorsed store and break the matched-pair invariant; this is why identifiers are two-sorted.

• 

Channel events must be acknowledged to the channel, not to the Protected LLM. Otherwise an uncoupled paste would emit a visible acknowledgment, and Theorem S3.25 would fail.

• 

The fast-path case analysis of Lemma S3.13 is disjunctive: typed and endorsed matches may co-occur, and processArgs_spec proves exhaustiveness without exclusivity.

Artifact.

The Lean artifact is available upon request and will be released with the code when the paper is published. The development pins the toolchain (lean-toolchain) and all dependencies (lake-manifest.json). Running lake exe cache get && lake build reproduces the verification. The artifact includes CORRESPONDENCE.md (Table S5), a README with the verbatim axiom report, the log of a clean build, and a SHA-256 manifest of the sources.
